# Certification Status

Living document. Last updated: 2026-08-22 (unit 79).

Per-feature stage tracking. A feature is not "done" until all four stages are checked: **implemented** (code written) → **tested** (cscripts test exists and passes, both dev and production mode) → **certified** (verified against known-correct values / dense-vs-sparse regression / edge cases, per the certification standard below) → **documented** (`.sthlp` exists and is current, including any new options/limitations).

## Certification standard (applied to every row below)

- **Syntax**: valid Stata syntax, documented options work, invalid options fail cleanly, missing required inputs give an informative error.
- **Functional**: verified against a small network with an analytically known correct answer.
- **Regression** (where replacing/extending existing code): compared against the prior implementation on small networks; if results differ, the discrepancy is investigated and documented, not silently accepted.
- **Edge cases**: empty network, isolates, disconnected components, directed/undirected, weighted, self-loops, non-consecutive node IDs — as applicable to the feature.
- **Scalability** (sparse-backend features only): tested on a realistically large sparse network, memory/time checked, absence of accidental dense allocation confirmed.

## Regression-sweep tier (adopted 2026-08-21)

Through harmonisation units 19-28, every change - however small - triggered a full sweep of the
entire `cscripts/` suite (~95 files) in both dev and production mode, launched as a background
agent. This was safe but not efficient: as the suite grew, sweeps grew to 180-190 sequential Stata
invocations and 5-40+ minutes; one sweep (unit 29, `nwclique`) hit an infrastructure hang (a `while
read <&3` construct wedging a Stata child process via fd inheritance) and then a hard 10-minute
bash-tool timeout on the fallback `for`-loop rewrite, without ever producing a result. A full sweep
after every change also does not scale as a certification *strategy* - most changes have a narrow,
knowable blast radius, and re-proving 90 unrelated commands still work is waste, not rigor.

Adopted instead: match the certification tier to the change's actual dependency radius, escalating
only when the risk warrants it.

- **L1 - Local**: the changed command's own `cscripts/test_*.do`, both dev and production mode.
  Default for a self-contained new command or a fix confined to one `.ado` file.
- **L2 - Dependency**: L1 plus a handful of other tests that exercise the same `unw_core.do`
  function(s) the change touched, or sit nearby in the file. Default when `unw_core.do` changed but
  the change is a new, isolated addition (a new standalone function/class method not called by
  anything pre-existing) rather than a modification to an already-shared function like
  `neighbors()`, `get_matrix_mod()`, or `nw_syntax`'s own locals.
- **L3 - Domain**: all tests in the same functional family (e.g. every centrality command, every
  two-mode command, every structural-equivalence command). Default when a change touches logic
  genuinely shared across a family - a common helper function, a shared display/output convention,
  a cross-cutting bugfix pattern (e.g. the `_rc`-staleness fix applied identically across
  `nwbrokerage`/`nwego`/`nwaltergen`/`nwclique`).
- **L4 - Full**: the entire `cscripts/` suite, both modes. Reserved for genuine core/backend
  changes (editing an *existing*, widely-relied-on `unw_core.do` function's own logic, not just
  adding a new one), `nw_syntax`/`nwset`/netname-netlist-parser changes, and milestone/integration
  checkpoints - not routine per-unit certification.

If a lower tier's run surfaces an unexpected, seemingly-unrelated failure, escalate to the next
tier rather than assuming it's a fluke. Documentation-only changes (a `.sthlp` regenerated via
`nw_helpwriter`, a `.dlg` edit) need no numerical regression run at all - re-running the affected
file's own test (to confirm the regeneration didn't corrupt anything, per unit 16's own established
practice) is enough. Scalability/stress tests (100k-node sparse benchmarks) are their own separate
concern, run only when a change could plausibly affect performance or storage, not as part of
per-unit correctness certification.

## Session 2 additions (2026-08-21)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| `nwburt` (Burt structural holes: effsize/efficiency/constraint/hierarchy) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Revived from git history (`master:nwburt.ado`), modernized (`nw_syntax`, `st_store`, `replace` guard). Found and fixed a real bug in the process: matrix multiplication on a missing-diagonal input silently corrupts every two-step redundancy/constraint calculation to 0 — fixed by zeroing the diagonal before the matrix products. `test_nwburt.do`, both modes, matches both documented worked examples (star=3, K4=1) and a from-scratch regression re-derivation of the historical formulas. |
| `nwkcore` (k-core decomposition) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New command + new `NWdef::calculate_kcore()` Mata method (standard Seidman 1983 iterative degree-peeling, monotone `core[v]=max(klevel, degree_at_removal)`). Draft caught and fixed a real bug before testing: an initial `pointer(real matrix) rowvector` design aliased every pointer to the same reused loop variable — rewritten as a padded dense `adjmat` matrix (matching the existing `get_adjlist()` idiom) instead. Certified against 4 hand-computable cases: triangle+pendant (2,2,2,1), K5 (all 4, the (n-1)-core of Kn), empty network (all 0), path graph (all 1, no 2-core in an acyclic graph), plus a directed-network case and a `replace`-guard check. `test_nwkcore.do`, both modes. |
| `nwaltergen` (alter/neighbor attribute aggregation, `nwgen exposure = mean(alter.x)`-style) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New command + new `NWdef::calculate_alterstat()` Mata method. Implements `mean`/`sum`/`min`/`max`/`sd`/`count` aggregation of an existing Stata variable's values over each node's alters (out-neighbors for directed networks — a deliberate, documented difference from `nwkcore`'s union-of-both-directions convention, since exposure is inherently directional). Missing `srcvar` values among a node's alters are dropped before aggregating (available-case, matching `egen`), never silently propagated — the exact failure mode found in the `nwburt` matrix-multiplication bug earlier this session, guarded against explicitly here by design rather than discovered by accident. `nwgen` itself gained a narrow `regexm()` pre-check dispatching `mean(alter.x)`-style calls straight to `nwaltergen`, implemented as a pure early-return in `nwgen.ado` with zero changes to the fragile legacy string-parser in `nwgenerate.ado` — a regression test confirms the existing `nwgenerate` dispatch shortcuts (e.g. `duplicate(...)`) are unaffected. Certified against a star network (hand-computed mean/sum/min/max/sd/count with a missing alter value dropped correctly), a directed network (out-neighbor-only semantics), an isolate node (sum/count=0, others missing), the `nwgen` shortcut's output matching `nwaltergen` directly, the `replace` guard, and invalid-stat/missing-variable error paths. `test_nwaltergen.do`, both modes. |
| `nwgeodesic`: per-node eccentricity + network radius | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Additive extension to an existing, already-tested command — no `unw_core.do` change (reuses the already-computed distance matrix and the pre-existing `rowmax()` builtin), so no `.mlib` rebuild was needed. Adds `generate()` (default `_eccentricity`) and `r(radius)`, following the exact same "undefined" convention already used by `r(diameter)`/`r(avgpath)` (missing per-node, `r(radius)=-1` in aggregate) when the network has unconnected pairs and `unconnected()` wasn't specified. Found and worked around a genuine Stata `syntax`-parser bug during implementation: declaring a new `replace` option in the same syntax line as the file's pre-existing (dead, unused) `noreplace` option causes Stata to silently fail to populate the `replace` local at all, confirmed via an isolated minimal repro before touching the real file — worked around by reusing the already-functional `nwreplace` for the eccentricity-variable guard instead of adding a second, broken `replace` option; documented in-line. Certified against a path graph (ecc 4,3,2,3,4; radius=2), K4 (all ecc=1, radius=1), a disconnected network (undefined case, both aggregate and per-node), and the same case with `unconnected(max)` (all become finite); extended the pre-existing `test_nwgeodesic.do` (not a new file) with radius/eccentricity assertions on its existing directed-valued fixture across all 3 of its pre-existing invocation patterns, confirming zero regression to any prior assertion. Both modes. |
| `nwneighbor` sparse migration | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (re-certified, content unchanged) | Rewrote all 3 modes (`outgoing`/`incoming`/`either`) using the sparse `neighbors()`/`neighbors_in()` accessors instead of dense-matrix row/column comparison; no `unw_core.do` change (pure `.ado` rewrite reusing already-tested accessors), so no `.mlib` rebuild needed. Found a real, previously-undiscovered bug in the process: the old `incoming` branch had a stray unbalanced paren — a genuine Mata syntax error, meaning `mode(incoming)` could never have actually executed before this fix (and had zero test coverage, so the bug was invisible until read directly). `nzmask`'s `!=0 & !=.` edge convention in `build_sparse_index()` matches the dense comparison being replaced exactly, so results are identical, not merely equivalent — confirmed against a directed 3-node network by hand (outgoing(A)={B,C}, incoming(C)={A,B}, incoming(A)={}, either(B)={A,C}). Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwneighbor.do` with full `incoming`-mode coverage (previously entirely absent, for the reason above) plus a `generate()` check on that mode; the file's pre-existing `outgoing`/`either` assertions still pass unchanged. Both modes. |
| `nwsimindex` (common-neighbor similarity indices: common/jaccard/dice/cosine/Adamic-Adar) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New command + new `NWdef::calculate_similarity_index()` Mata method, distinct in purpose from the pre-existing `nwsimilar.ado` (which measures full tie-*profile* structural equivalence, not shared-neighborhood overlap — deliberately given a non-colliding name and left `nwsimilar.ado` untouched). Computes all pairwise scores via one matrix multiply (`NB*NB'` for the shared-neighbor-count matrix, diagonal zeroed first to kill both self-loop and self-counting artifacts in a single step) rather than an O(n²) or O(n³) per-pair loop. Directed networks use the union-of-both-directions neighbor sense (`nwkcore`'s convention, not `nwaltergen`'s directional one — a shared-neighborhood question is direction-agnostic). Found and fixed a real implementation bug before writing any test: assumed Mata's elementwise operators broadcast a column vector against a row vector (`deg :+ deg'`) the way NumPy/R do; confirmed via an isolated minimal repro that this is a conformability error in Mata, not silently-wrong output — fixed via explicit outer-product-shaped expansion (`deg*J(1,n,1) :+ J(n,1,1)*deg'`). Adamic-Adar's well-known `log(1)` blowup is proven safe by construction (a node can only appear in a genuine two-distinct-node intersection term if its own degree is already ≥ 2) rather than merely worked around. Certified against a hand-built 4-node network across all 5 measures (matching hand-derived values exactly, including two isolate-node edge cases where the correct answer is genuinely undefined/missing, e.g. cosine's 0/0), a directed-network union-convention case, and name-collision/`replace` semantics. Found and fixed a real usability bug in the `replace` option along the way: naively copying `nw2project.ado`'s own `nw_validate`+`r(validname)` pattern silently auto-renamed even when `replace` was explicitly given, instead of reusing the exact requested name — fixed here to the correct, expected behavior (drop-then-recreate under the exact name when `replace` is given); `nw2project.ado` itself was not touched (out of scope for this unit, flagged below) since its own test suite never exercised this path either. Also surfaced a genuine fragility in `nw_helpwriter.ado`'s doc-certification mechanism (see its own row below for the fix). `test_nwsimindex.do`, both modes. |
| `nw_helpwriter.ado` `_rc`-check fix | ✅ | ✅ (manual repro + full regression sweep) | ✅ | n/a (tooling, not a network command) | A bare `do cscripts/test_X.do` reads Stata's ambient `_rc` after the file completes — but neither a successful `assert` nor a successful `di` resets `_rc`, so if a test file contains *any* `capture badcmd`-style error-path check anywhere (not just at the end), `_rc` stays polluted with that deliberately-triggered error code for the rest of the file, and the "last certified" footer is silently never written even though every assertion passed. Confirmed via two isolated minimal repros (one showing the false-negative, one showing `capture noisily do X` correctly reports 0 for a clean pass and the real error code for a genuine failure) before touching the real file. Fixed by wrapping the check in `capture noisily do ...` (the `noisily` keeps test output visible exactly as before). Re-ran `nw_helpwriter` for the 3 docs from this session's own earlier work that turned out to have been silently affected (`nwburt`, `nwbalance`, `nw2project` — none had a "last certified" line before this fix; all three do now, content otherwise unchanged). `nwkcore`/`nwaltergen`/`nwgeodesic`/`nwneighbor`/`nwsimindex`/`nwcommunity`/`nwmodularity` were unaffected (already had the line). The rest of the package's ~70 pre-existing `.sthlp` files have not been audited against this — see Pending. |
| `nw2project.ado`: `replace` bugfix + `r(nodes)`/`r(ties)` display bugfix | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (re-certified) | Two independent real bugs found and fixed while re-certifying this file's doc: (1) `replace` silently auto-renamed instead of reusing the exact requested name (same root cause as the `nwsimindex` fix — `nw_validate`'s `r(validname)` was taken unconditionally before the `replace` branch even ran); fixed identically. (2) The display banner's `` `r(nodes)' ``/`` `r(ties)' `` lines always printed blank — not because `r(nodes)`/`r(ties)` were wrong (they were correct, per `return scalar`), but because a bare backtick-quoted `` `r(nodes)' `` is parsed as a *local macro* named literally "r(nodes)" (which doesn't exist, so it silently expands to ""), not as an r-class reference. The naive fix, `` `=r(nodes)' ``, was *also* wrong: a `return scalar` only publishes to `r()` when the program itself exits, so `r(nodes)` reads as missing from inside the same program body that just set it — confirmed via an isolated repro before settling on the real fix, which references the underlying Stata scalar (`` `=nodes' ``, immediately readable) instead. While fixing (1), a genuine third bug was found and fixed in the *tooling*, not this file: a double-backtick markdown-style comment (`` `replace` ``) crashed `nw_helpwriter.ado`'s line-copy loop with "too few quotes" — see its own row above. Regression-tested against the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nw2project.do` (extended, not replaced) with new assertions for name-collision auto-increment, `replace` reusing the exact name, and `r(nodes)`/`r(ties)` reading correctly immediately after the call (checked before any other r-class command could clobber them — a real ordering trap hit while writing the test itself). Both modes. |
| `nwevcent` weighted option + eigenvalue-search bugfix | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Added `weighted` (calculate on tie values instead of always dichotomizing a valued network, matching Newman 2004/Bonacich 1972's weighted generalization) and `replace` was documented (already implemented in code but missing from the doc header — a pre-existing gap). Found and fixed a genuine bug while extending the Mata helper: the eigenvalue-index search loop used `rows(thisnw->get_nodes())` as its upper bound — `get_nodes()` returns a scalar, and `rows()` of a scalar is always 1, so the search never actually iterated past `i=1`. This was silently correct in every observed case only because Mata's `symeigensystem()` happens to return eigenvalues already sorted descending (confirmed empirically, not merely assumed) — fixed to genuinely search using the real node count, removing the reliance on that ordering holding for every possible input. First verified `nosym` was *not* a bug before touching anything: it uses Stata's built-in `no`-prefix boolean-option convention (`syntax [nosym]` populates a local named `sym`, not `nosym`) — confirmed via an isolated repro before concluding the code was already correct. Certified against a star network with an extreme tie-weight ratio (unweighted treats all three leaves identically; weighted makes the heavily-weighted leaf clearly dominate — both the qualitative direction and near-exact expected values verified), an unvalued network (`weighted` has no effect, exact match), and K5 (eigenvalue-search regression guard: all 5 nodes get an identical, correctly-nonzero score of 1/√5). Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwevcent.do`, both modes. |
| Programming-API documentation chapter (`nw_programming.sthlp`) | ✅ | n/a (pure doc, no executable code) | n/a | ✅ | New "[NW-5.2] Modern (class-based, sparse-backend) programming" section added, since the existing "Internal design" section documented only the pre-2016 global-macro/dense-matrix architecture (now relabeled "[NW-5.3] ... (legacy, pre-2016)" and explicitly cross-referenced, not deleted — still has historical/reference value). Documents: `nw_syntax`'s provided locals, the `calculate_*()`-method-on-`NWdef` + `tempname`/`st_store()` convention used by every command added this session, the sparse accessor API (`neighbors()`/`neighbors_in()`/`degree()`/`has_edge()`/`edge_weight()`/`edgelist()`) and when to prefer it over the dense matrix, the two different directed-network "neighbor" conventions this session settled on and why, a 10-item numbered list of genuine Mata/Stata pitfalls found and fixed this session (pass-by-reference quoting, `selectindex()` orientation, missing-value matrix-multiply poisoning, no column/row broadcasting in Mata, `r()` not published until program exit, undefined `r()` reads as missing not error, `_rc` not reset by a successful `assert`/`di`, invalid single-line interactive `mata: if`, the `nw_helpwriter` double-backtick crash, `matastrict` declaration discipline), and the dev/production dual-mode testing + `nw_helpwriter` certification workflow. No `.ado`/`.do` file was touched, so no regression sweep was needed for this unit. |
| `nwcug` (Conditional Uniform Graph test) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | New command. Compares an observed network statistic against `reps` random networks density-matched to the observed network via the already-existing `nwrandom, density()` conditioned-generation capability — a standard baseline null model (Anderson, Butts and Carley 1999). `stat()` is a full command *template* containing the literal token `##net##`, substituted with the observed network's name once and each random draw's name `reps` times — deliberately not a bare command name: a first draft hit a real bug where the second random draw errored ("variable already exists") because the templated command (e.g. `nwcomponents`) tries to create a fixed-name output variable every call without its own `replace`; the template design puts that responsibility on the caller instead of nwcug guessing at option compatibility across arbitrary commands. Reused the `nw2project` in-program `r()`-not-yet-published lesson from this same session directly (display block reads from locals/scalars, not `r()`, since it runs before this program exits). Found and fixed a second real robustness gap while writing the invalid-`rname()` test: `r(undefined-name)` does not error, it silently evaluates to missing — an empty-string-only guard on the fetched statistic would have missed this and let the whole test proceed treating a missing value as a legitimate observed statistic; the guard now also checks for `.`. Certified against a fully deterministic case (two disjoint 5-cliques, density far above the ER connectivity threshold for n=10, so all 500 random draws are provably 1 component while observed is 2 — `sd_null` exactly 0, `p_greater` exactly 0), a milder non-deterministic case checked only for statistical sanity bounds (p-values in [0,1], reps count matches), seed-based reproducibility (identical results across two runs with the same seed), `tail()` validation, and the missing-`rname()` robustness fix. `test_nwcug.do`, both modes. |

| `nwgenvar` dead-code fix | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (inherits `nwgen`'s doc) | Was a broken duplicate of `nwgenerate.ado` under a mismatched filename, never actually loadable as `nwgenvar`. Converted to a genuine thin wrapper matching `nwgen.ado`'s proven pattern. Verified working via `nwgenerate.ado`'s own certified test pattern. |
| `nwgenerate.ado` dead dispatch branches | ✅ (partial) | ✅ | ✅ | — | 8 silently-no-op'ing shortcut branches (`dyadprob`/`homophily`/`lattice`/`path`/`pref`/`ring`/`small`/`transpose`) now give a clear, immediate error instead of silently doing nothing. Full restoration (re-wiring each to its target command's current syntax) deferred - see ROADMAP.md Stage 1. |
| `nwbalance` docs+tests+bugfix | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Was undocumented (no `.sthlp`) and untested. Also fixed a real bug: an unconditional `save test, replace` wrote an unwanted `test.dta` into the user's working directory on every call (unused debug leftover) - removed. Certified against a hand-derived signed-K4 worked example (Cartwright-Harary strong balance). One genuine pre-existing limitation found and documented, not silently fixed: a network with zero closed triads errors (r(2000)) instead of reporting zero - flagged in ROADMAP.md as a real gap in the reshape-based triad enumeration pipeline. |

## Session 2, continued: nwdissimilar/nwsimilar/nwhierarchy chain (partial fix at the time - see harmonisation unit 20 below for the resolution)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwtomatafast.ado` dead-reference fix | ✅ | ✅ (isolated repro) | ✅ | — | Assumed the pre-2016 storage design, where each network had its own standalone global Mata matrix named `nw_mata<id>`. That convention no longer exists under the current class-based architecture, so every caller (`nwdissimilar`, `nwsimilar`, `nwhierarchy`) failed with "nw_mata1 not found". Fixed to return a dereferenced-pointer Mata expression (`(*netobj->get_matrix())`, matching `nw_tomata`'s own `r(adj)` convention) instead of a now-nonexistent named matrix — preserves every caller's existing contract of substituting `` `r(mata)' `` directly into a later `mata:` line. |
| `nwdissimilar.ado` / `nwsimilar.ado` `matastrict` declarations | ✅ | ✅ (isolated repro) | ✅ | — | `unw_core.do` turns on `set matastrict on` for the rest of the Stata session (never reset); these two files' inline Mata helper functions (5 in `nwdissimilar`, 4 in `nwsimilar` — the euclidean/manhattan/matches/jaccard/hamming (dis)similarity formulas) used loose, undeclared locals throughout, a pre-`matastrict` style, so they failed to compile with "variable S undeclared" etc. the moment `unw_core.do` had been sourced earlier in the same session. Fixed by adding `real matrix S, i_outvec, i_invec, j_outvec, j_invec` / `real scalar i, j` declarations to every function. Confirmed via `set matastrict on` + `do nwdissimilar.ado` / `do nwsimilar.ado` compiling cleanly before vs. after. |
| **`nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar`/`nwhierarchy` chain — still NOT functional end-to-end** | ⚠️ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | A third, unresolved bug remains, found and precisely reproduced but not fixed: calling `nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar` as a normal command still fails with "`euclidean_dissimilarity() not found`" (or the equivalent `nwsimilar` function), even after both fixes above. Root-caused as far as: (1) the same user-defined Mata function IS directly callable via a plain `mata: X = myfunc(...)` line moments after the defining command runs successfully; (2) the identical function reference, when it appears inside `nwset`'s own `mat()` option string (evaluated internally via `` mata: mode1 = cols(`mat') `` at `nwset.ado:337`), is reported as "not found" in the exact same session, immediately after — i.e. something about how `nwset.ado` evaluates its `mat()` argument specifically cannot see a Mata function the calling program itself just proved was loaded. Ruled out: adopath/filename collisions (confirmed via `which`, and via a same-directory differently-named copy), matastrict, function naming collision, `nwtomatafast`/`nwvalidate`/`_opts_oneof` individually (each tested standalone and works), `capture mata mata drop` (missing-colon) syntax, ado-file loading order/timing (tested calling the defining command first, then `nwset` separately — still fails), and `nwset`'s own trailing-mata-block loading (nwset was already loaded successfully earlier in the same session before the failing call). **Not fixed. Flagged for a dedicated follow-up** — see `docs/ROADMAP.md`. Until resolved, `nwdissimilar`, `nwsimilar`, and (since it depends on them for its default dissimilarity-matrix path) `nwhierarchy` should be considered broken for any caller that routes a Mata-function-call expression through `nwset`'s `mat()` option — which is how all three currently work. `nwhierarchy`'s `dismat()`/`disnet()` input modes, which bypass this path entirely, were not tested and may still work. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 2: nwkatz

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwkatz` — fixed 5 real bugs, found by the network-type audit's "not canonical Katz centrality" flag | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The audit flagged `nwkatz` as scientifically questionable (computes a shortest-path distance-decay sum, not literature-canonical walk-counting Katz centrality). Investigating that documentation issue surfaced the command had **never actually worked end to end** — zero prior test coverage. Fixed: (1) `replace` was declared via the trailing `*` catch-all instead of as a real option, so it was silently absorbed into an unused `options` local and never actually worked, and the existing-variable guard printed a warning but never called `error`/`exit`, so execution continued regardless — both fixed (`error 110` on collision, `replace` now genuinely declared). (2) The documented `geodesic_options` passthrough was never actually forwarded to the internal `nwgeodesic` call — fixed. (3) The core `alpha^distance` computation routed a Mata pointer-dereference expression (`*netobj->get_matrix()`) through `nwreplace`'s plain-arithmetic string-substitution parser (`nw_expnetexp.ado`), which naively treats every `*`/`-`/`>`/`^` character as a Stata operator to translate — this mangled the pointer/method-call syntax into invalid Mata on every single call, confirmed via direct `set trace on` tracing. Fixed by computing the transform directly in Mata and writing it back via the network's own `set_edge()` method instead of routing object syntax through a string-based expression translator at all. (4) `getmata`'s parenthetical-expression form doesn't accept a raw pointer-dereference expression either (a separate, adjacent discovery, confirmed via an isolated repro) — fixed by assigning to a plain Mata variable first. (5) The `_out`/`_in` directed-network outputs were swapped relative to this package's own row=source/column=target adjacency convention (confirmed against `get_outdegree()`/`get_indegree()` in `unw_core.do`) — a node's true out-reach was being reported as its in-reach and vice versa. Also fixed: dead `unconnected()` option (declared, given a default, never read anywhere in the body — removed); `sum \`generate_all'` referenced a never-assigned local at the end of the command (harmless cosmetic bug in the final display only, not the stored result — fixed). Documentation: added an explicit note that the implemented formula is not the literature's Katz centrality despite the name/citation (preserving the existing formula/values for backwards compatibility, making the citation's relationship to the actual computation explicit rather than implied), added a "Supported network types" section, fixed a `nwwbuse`→`nwwebuse` typo in the example, retitled from "Calculate Katz centrality" to "Calculate a Katz-inspired distance-decay centrality" (and its two topical-index entries) for honesty. Certified against a hand-computable star network (alpha=0.5: center=1.5, leaves=1.0 each, matching exactly) and a directed A→B,A→C network (out-reach/in-reach values verified by hand, catching the swap), plus the `replace` guard and `geodesic_options` passthrough. `test_nwkatz.do` (new), both modes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 3: nwtriads

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwtriads` undirected-input guard | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The audit flagged `nwtriads` as computing a directed-only 16-type MAN census with no guard for undirected input. Verified by direct computation (not assumed) on two different undirected test networks: 11 of the 12 asymmetric-dyad-dependent categories (`_012`/`_021D`/`_021U`/`_021C`/`_030T`/`_030C`/`_111D`/`_111U`/`_120D`/`_120U`/`_120C`) do reliably return exactly 0 for undirected input, as expected mathematically (an undirected network has no asymmetric dyads by construction). Added a runtime note printed whenever the command is called on an undirected network, plus a "Supported network types" doc section, rather than a hard error — preserves the ability to run the command on undirected data (some callers may genuinely want the 0/1/2/3-edge-triad counts, `_003`/`_102`/`_201`/`_300`, which remain meaningful) while ensuring the command never again *silently* proceeds without indicating the limitation. **Found a new, separate, unresolved bug in the process**: `_210` does *not* reliably return 0 on undirected input despite the identical mathematical argument applying to it (confirmed nonzero on two different undirected test networks) — likely a Mata `[symmetric]`-matrix-type edge case inside `calculate_triadcensus()`'s own asymmetric-dyad (C) matrix construction in `unw_core.do`, not yet root-caused. Not fixed in this pass (would require dedicated Mata-internals investigation, out of scope for the network-type-guard fix this unit targeted) — documented explicitly in `nwtriads.ado`'s own doc header and locked into `cscripts/test_nwtriads.do` as a regression guard on the current (known-incorrect) value, never asserted as correct. Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwtriads.do` (not a new file); its prior directed-network assertions still pass unchanged. Both modes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 4: nwcontext mode() terminology

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwcontext`'s `mode()` — `either` accepted as the canonical synonym for `both` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The audit found a real terminology inconsistency: `nwneighbor`'s `mode()` uses `outgoing/incoming/either` for the union-of-both-directions concept; `nwcontext`'s `mode()` used `outgoing/incoming/both` for the identical concept — same meaning, different word. Fixed per `NWCOMMANDS_COMMAND_STYLE.md`'s decision (`either` is canonical, `both` kept as a working legacy alias, not removed): `either` is now accepted by `_opts_oneof` and immediately normalized to `both` internally, so the rest of the command's logic (which checks the literal string `"both"`) needed no other changes and `both` continues to work exactly as before. Also fixed a genuine pre-existing doc typo found while editing this section: the `incoming` context was mislabeled `"; default"` in the option-value table when `outgoing` is actually the default (confirmed against the code's own `if "\`mode''==""  { local mode = "outgoing" }`). Certified with a regression test proving `mode(either)` and `mode(both)` produce bit-identical results, plus an invalid-mode-value error-path check. Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwcontext.do` (not a new file); all of its prior assertions still pass unchanged. Both modes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 5: nwpath r()-return doc/code parity

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwpath` — real r()-return doc/code mismatch fixed | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The audit flagged `nwpath`'s doc claiming `r(paths)`, `r(path_shortest)`, `r(ego)`, `r(alter)`, `r(paths_matrix)` that the code never actually set (it only ever returned `r(num_paths)` — a different name than documented — and `r(path_length)`). Fixed by making the code match the documented interface rather than watering the docs down to match the code, since every missing return was cheap and safe to add (no other `.ado` file in the repository reads any of these — confirmed via a repo-wide grep before renaming): renamed `r(num_paths)` → `r(paths)` (3 internal references updated, including two `forvalues i = 1/\`r(...)''` loops); added `r(ego)`/`r(alter)` (trivial — the node ids were already computed locals); added `r(path_shortest)` as an explicit alias of `r(path_length)`, documented honestly as an alias rather than a distinct value, since the command has no `length()` option to select a non-shortest path despite the pre-existing doc's "length of path specified in `length()`" text and an illustrative example's fictional "Selected length" display line implying one exists — both corrected rather than left describing a feature that was never built; added `r(paths_matrix)` (a genuinely new capability, built directly from the already-computed path matrix, set only when at least one path was found — matches the documented behavior exactly). Also fixed the illustrative example's "Ego : 9 (medici)" line, which claimed a numeric-id-plus-name display format the actual `di` code has never produced (it only prints the name). Added a "Supported network types" section (weighted: not applicable — any nonzero tie is traversable regardless of value, no weighted shortest-path variant exists in this command; see `nwgeodesic` instead). Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwpath.do` (not a new file) with assertions for every new/renamed return, covering both the unconnected case (`r(paths)=0`, `r(paths_matrix)` correctly left unset) and the connected case (matrix contents verified against the known path). Both modes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 6: nwdegree genuine netlist support

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwdegree` — implemented genuine `netlist` (multi-network) support | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The audit flagged `nwdegree`'s doc describing multi-network ("z networks at once") behavior the code didn't implement. On inspection this was worse than a doc/code mismatch: `nw_syntax ..., max(1)` actively capped the argument to exactly one network, and what looked like the start of a loop (`if networks > 1 { local k = 1 ... }`) never actually wrapped anything — the rest of the body ran once unconditionally and referenced an undefined `netname_temp` local throughout (a genuine, half-finished implementation, not merely undocumented). Rather than simply correct the docs to admit the feature doesn't exist, this was finished: `nwdegree` is exactly the model example `NWCOMMANDS_COMMAND_STYLE.md` itself cites for genuine `netlist` support (independent per-network degree calculation has obvious, useful semantics). Implemented via `max(9999)` + a real `foreach` loop (matching the established `nwcomponents`/`nwcommunity` pattern), with output variables suffixed `_<netname>` when more than one network is processed (`NWCOMMANDS_COMMAND_STYLE.md`'s established multi-network output-naming convention) — a single-network call is completely unaffected, keeping the exact plain default names as before. Found and fixed a real bug while implementing this: the outer `networks` count local gets clobbered by the inner per-iteration `nw_syntax` call (which always resets it to 1, since it parses one network name at a time) — captured into a separate `totalnetworks` local *before* the loop starts, matching how `nwcomponents`/`nwcommunity` already avoid this exact trap. Also investigated and *ruled out* an apparent second bug during certification: a multi-network test initially looked like it was storing one network's degree values into the wrong observations — traced to `nw_datasync`'s own documented behavior (it re-sorts the shared dataset to align "node i of whichever network was just synced" with "observation i"), not a defect; the test was rewritten to look values up by `_nwnode` rather than assume a fixed row position, and the underlying values were confirmed correct throughout. r()-returns (e.g. `r(dg_central)`) reflect whichever network was processed last, matching this package's established convention for other `netlist` commands (documented explicitly, not left implicit). Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwdegree.do` (not a new file); its prior single-network assertions still pass unchanged. Both modes. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 7: nwbetween genuine netlist support + dead-guard fix

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwbetween` — implemented genuine `netlist` support; fixed dead `replace` guard; removed dead `alpha()` option | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Same doc/code gap the audit flagged alongside `nwdegree`'s (unit 6): `nwbetween`'s doc has always claimed "In case, betweenness centrality is calculated for z networks at the same time... the command generates the variables varname_z" but `nw_syntax` was called with no `max()` override (defaulting to exactly one network), and `local k = 1` / `local generate_all ""` were vestigial scaffolding from an abandoned attempt, referenced nowhere else in the body. Fixed the same way as `nwdegree`: `max(9999)` + a real `foreach netname_temp in \`netname'` loop, with output suffixed `_<netname>` only when more than one network is processed; single-network calls are unaffected. Separately found and fixed a real, independent bug while reading the code closely for this fix: `capture drop \`generate'*` ran *unconditionally*, before the "already exists" confirm check — so the check could never actually find the variable (it had just been deleted by the wildcard drop), and there was no `replace` option in `syntax` at all despite the error message telling users to pass one. In effect `nwbetween` always silently overwrote any existing output variable, with a completely inert-looking guard giving the opposite impression. Fixed by adding a real `replace` option and making the guard gate the drop, matching the working convention already used by `nwdegree`/`nwkatz` elsewhere in this package. Also removed the dead `alpha(real 0)` option (parsed, never referenced anywhere in the body — a leftover, not connected to any weighted-betweenness variant, which doesn't exist in this command). Drive-by fix: a broken `{help nwbetweenness}` link (typo, no such help file exists) in `nwkatz.ado`'s and `nwevcent.ado`'s "See also" sections, corrected to `{help nwbetween}`; both `.sthlp` regenerated via `nw_helpwriter` to match (no functional code change in either file, comment-only). Extended `nwbetween.sthlp`'s "Supported network types" section: **W3** (explicit binary-only, already partly documented as "the command used the dichotomized network," now formalized), plus a newly-documented signed-network gotcha found while reading `calculate_betweenness()`'s Mata body — the adjacency-list build filters on `edge_weight(m,n)>0`, so a tie with weight <= 0, including a negative (e.g. antagonistic) tie, is silently treated as no tie at all, not as a distinct dichotomous edge; signed networks are not natively supported, now stated explicitly rather than left for a user to discover by reading Mata source. Also documented that the default (non-`nosym`) behavior symmetrizes *any* network, including an already-directed one, before computing betweenness at all — `nosym` is required for genuinely directed betweenness, previously only inferable from the option's one-line synopsis. Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwbetween.do` (not a new file) with netlist assertions (suffixed variable names, values checked against the same hand-verified path-graph betweenness values used by the existing single-network tests), a single-network-unaffected regression check, and a replace-guard test for both the single deliberate-overwrite case and the multi-network case. Passing in both dev mode and production mode (against the compiled `.mlib`). Full-suite regression sweep (78 files, both modes): matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly, no new failures; `test_nwkatz`/`test_nwevcent` confirmed unaffected by the drive-by doc fix; confirmed no other `.ado` calls `nwbetween` internally (only doc "see also" references exist elsewhere). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 8: nwconstraint — full documentation, tests, and packaging from scratch

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwconstraint` — wrote complete doc header, test suite, and packaging entries (all previously absent) | n/a (pre-existing computation, unchanged) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Flagged by the audit as a highest-priority target ("zero documentation of directed/two-mode behaviour despite being weight-driven by construction"), but on inspection the gap was larger than that: `nwconstraint.ado` had *no doc header at all* (not a thin one — literally none, just bare code), no `.sthlp` file existed, `nw_alphabetical.sthlp`/`nw_topical.sthlp` both explicitly said "no help file yet," there was zero test coverage, and the command was missing from all 4 packaging manifests (`_pkg_ado.txt`, `_pkg_hlp.txt`, `nwcommands-ado.pkg`, `nwcommands-hlp.pkg`) — not merely undocumented, effectively unshippable. The underlying Mata computation (`constraint()`, a standalone function, not touched) was read closely and confirmed to be exactly Burt's (1992) dyadic constraint formula `c_ij = (p_ij + sum_q(p_iq*p_qj))^2` with `p_ij = a_ij/rowsum(a_i.)`, computed on the raw (non-symmetrized) adjacency matrix and returned as a *new network* via `nwset, mat()` rather than a per-node Stata variable — an unusual but real, working design (the aggregate node-level constraint reported in the literature is then a row sum the user computes themselves, e.g. via `nwtomata`). Wrote a full SMCL doc header from scratch (title/syntax/description/formula/examples/"Supported network types"/references/see also) describing this exactly as it behaves, not as a hypothetical redesign. Verified the formula end-to-end with a hand-computed star-network test (`cscripts/test_nwconstraint.do`, new file): center-to-leaf constraint 1/9, leaf-to-center constraint 1, and — a genuine arithmetic correction made *during* test authoring, not before — leaf-to-leaf constraint is *also* 1/9 (not 0, as first assumed), because Burt's indirect term `sum_q(p_iq*p_qj)` picks up a length-2 path leaf→hub→leaf even though the two leaves have no direct tie; the aggregate check (hub's row sums to 1/3, matching the textbook "n mutually disconnected contacts ⇒ constraint 1/n" identity) still holds since it only touches the hub's own row, which is unaffected by this correction. Also tested a directed-only star (confirms the command's real, asymmetric treatment of directed input — no symmetrization, so a node with only incoming ties shows zero constraint) and a weighted star (confirms **W1**, tie weight used natively as the investment proportion, not as distance). Classified: Binary yes; Directed yes but asymmetric/not-symmetrized (now explicit, previously undiscoverable without reading Mata source); Weighted **W1**; Signed not supported (`p_ij` becomes mathematically meaningless — can exceed 1 or go negative — if any of a node's outgoing tie weights are negative, now stated explicitly rather than silently produced); Two-mode not checked. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and to both `nw_topical.sthlp` (moved from "Uncategorized" into the Analysis section) and `nw_alphabetical.sthlp` (placeholder "no help file yet" line replaced). Passing in both dev mode and production mode (against the compiled `.mlib`; a matching `lib/cscripts_prod/test_nwconstraint.do` was created, since none existed before). Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 9: nwqap — fixed a total crash, a display bug, and added a weight warning

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwqap` — fixed r(3000) crash on every call; fixed blank row labels; added explicit weighted-DV warning | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Started as a scoped follow-up to the audit's `nwqap` weight-handling flag ("silently dichotomizes any weighted DV/IV network... no warning"), but investigation found something much bigger: `nwqap` could not run at all. It uses the deprecated `_nwsyntax` wrapper (`program _nwsyntax { nw_syntax \`0' ; c_local netobj ... ; c_local id ... ; c_local netname ... ; c_local networks ... }`), which only re-exports 4 of the locals `nw_syntax` itself sets via `c_local` (`netobj`/`id`/`netname`/`networks`) — `nodes`/`directed`/`valued`/`is2mode`/`labs`/`datasync`/`selfloops` never reach the caller, since `c_local` only propagates one stack frame up and `_nwsyntax` doesn't forward them further. `nwqap.ado`'s very first Mata call (`J((\`nodes' * \`nodes'),\`vars',0)`) therefore always referenced an empty `nodes` local, crashing every single invocation with r(3000) "nothing found where subexp expected" - confirmed empirically before any fix (`nwqap dv iv1, permutations(5)` on a trivial hand-built network reproduced the crash exactly). Zero test coverage existed to have ever caught this. Fixed by switching to `nw_syntax` directly (also resolves this command's entry in the cross-cutting "legacy architecture" list in `docs/COMMAND_AUDIT.md`, upgrading that finding from "style nit" to "actually fatal in at least one case" - `nwcloseness`/`nworder` have not been checked for the same failure mode and are flagged as a priority follow-up). A second, independent, unrelated bug was found while verifying the first: `nwqap.ado` read `` `r(name)' `` after calling `nwname`, but `nwname` (via `nw_name.ado`) actually returns `r(netname)` - `r(name)` never existed, so the local used to build every printed row label was always empty, leaving every IV row blank in the results table (confirmed both by reading `nw_name.ado`'s own `return` list and by reproducing the bug with debug instrumentation, then confirming the fix with a working "iv1"-labeled table matching the raw `logit` output's own labeling). Note: this label bug never affected `e(b)`/`e(pvalues)` themselves, since those come directly from Stata's own regression command using the dataset's real variable names - only the cosmetic printed table was ever wrong. Also added an explicit, non-silent warning (this package's harmonisation standard: never silently mishandle weights) when the dependent network is valued and `type()` is one of the binary-outcome commands (`logit`, the default, plus `probit`/`cloglog`/`scobit`) - demonstrated empirically that this is a real, not hypothetical, gotcha: a weighted DV network and its exact binary equivalent (same tie pattern, weight 1 instead of arbitrary positive weights) give the bit-for-bit identical `logit` coefficient, proving tie strength is discarded; `type(regress)` was separately confirmed to use tie strength correctly via an exact hand-computable coefficient (`wdv = 2*iv1` element-wise gives coefficient exactly 2.0, intercept ~0). Wrote `cscripts/test_nwqap.do` (new file, zero coverage before) covering: the crash fix (basic run must not error), the exact-coefficient `type(regress)` check, the weighted-vs-binary `logit`-discards-weight demonstration, the variable-IV code path (separate from the network-IV path, smoke-tested), and a direct micro-test of the `nwname`/`r(netname)` contract to guard against ever reintroducing that specific confusion. A separate, pre-existing fragility was found (not fixed) while building the test: a QAP permutation can by chance produce a fully degenerate (no-variation) permuted dependent network, which the chosen regression command cannot fit, aborting the whole command with r(2000) instead of skipping/retrying that one permutation - avoided in the test suite via a fixed `set seed` and adequately dense test networks; logged in Pending below as a genuine robustness gap, separate from the correctness fixes made here. Also hand-added a "Supported network types" section directly to the pre-existing, hand-maintained `nwqap.sthlp` (this command's `.ado` has no `/*** ... ***/` doc header for `nw_helpwriter` to regenerate from - migrating it to the header-driven convention used by every other command touched this session was scoped out as a separate, larger, non-blocking task). Passing in both dev mode and production mode (new `lib/cscripts_prod/test_nwqap.do`). Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 10: _nwsyntax root-cause fix; nwcloseness and nworder repaired

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_nwsyntax.ado` — fixed the shared wrapper's own `netname` re-export bug; `nwcloseness`/`nworder` fixed as direct consequences, plus a third, independent multi-network bug in `nwcloseness` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Direct follow-up to unit 9's finding that `nwcloseness`/`nworder` (both still on the deprecated `_nwsyntax` wrapper) had not been checked for the same class of failure as `nwqap`. Investigation found a **different, more insidious** bug than `nwqap`'s: `_nwsyntax.ado`'s own re-export line reads `c_local netname \`name'` — but nothing in `_nwsyntax` or the `nw_syntax` call it wraps ever sets a local called `name`; `nw_syntax` sets one called `netname` (via its own `c_local` in `_nwsyntax`'s scope), which `_nwsyntax` should have re-exported by referencing `` `netname' ``, not `` `name' ``. Every single call to `_nwsyntax` therefore re-exported an *empty* `netname` to its caller - unlike `nwqap`'s crash, this produces **no error at all**: `nwcloseness net1` ran to completion with `_rc == 0`, printed a normal-looking (if blank-titled) results banner, and simply never created any of its `_closeness`/`_farness`/`_nearness` output variables, because its `foreach netname_temp in \`netname'` loop silently iterated zero times over an empty list. Confirmed via targeted debug instrumentation (a copy of `nwcloseness.ado` with `noi di` probes inserted at the loop boundary) that the loop body was never entered at all before the fix. Fixed by correcting the single `c_local` line in `_nwsyntax.ado` - a genuinely shared root-cause fix, since every other still-deprecated caller of `_nwsyntax` that relies on its `netname` export benefits immediately, not just `nwcloseness`. Fixing this exposed a **second**, previously-masked bug: once the loop actually ran, `nwcloseness` called `_nwsyntax_other`, which is incompatible with the modern network storage architecture (its `mata: _diag(nw_mata\`id', 0)` references the legacy `nw_mata<id>` global directly, which no longer exists) - it also turned out to be entirely absent from the working tree (tracked in git history, but deleted from disk, part of this repository's pre-existing, unrelated file-deletion drift; restored via `git checkout HEAD -- _nwsyntax_other.ado` since 4 other commands still depend on the file existing even though it wasn't the fix for `nwcloseness` itself). Fixed by replacing `nwcloseness`'s `_nwsyntax_other` call with a direct `nw_syntax` call and using its `nodes` export instead of `_nwsyntax_other`'s `othernodes` (same modernization pattern as `nwqap`'s fix in unit 9). A **third**, independent bug was found while writing netlist (multi-network) test coverage: `nwcloseness`'s per-network result-storage step (`mata: st_store((1::\`nodes'),...)`) assumed rows `1..nodes` of the shared dataset already corresponded to the current network's own nodes in order, but nothing established that alignment for any network after the first - confirmed by direct observation (a 2-network test produced correct values for only 4 of the expected 7 dataset rows, with the other 3 entirely missing/misaligned). Fixed by adding an explicit `nw_datasync` call before each network's results are stored, the same alignment mechanism already used by `nwdegree`'s own netlist support. All three fixes verified together with a from-scratch `cscripts/test_nwcloseness.do` (zero coverage before): single-network results checked against hand-computed Sabidussi closeness/farness/nearness values for a 4-node path graph, and a 2-network case checked by `_nwnode` lookup (not row position, per this package's established `nw_datasync` testing convention) against a second, independently hand-computed 3-node path graph. `nworder` had an entirely separate, additional bug, found and fixed in the same pass: it relied on `nw_syntax`'s `name()` option to export the resolved `_all` network list under a local literally called `allnets` - but `name()` is dead code in `nw_syntax.ado` itself (sets a local, never references it again anywhere in the file), so `allnets` was always empty, and the command crashed with r(111) "no variables defined" on every call. A second, unrelated collision was found in the same investigation: `nworder`'s own `syntax anything(name=netname)` line names the user's reorder specification `netname`, which the very next line's `_nwsyntax _all, ...` call then silently overwrote via its own `c_local netname` export - fixed by capturing the user's original specification into a separate local (`userorder`) before making that call, then restoring `netname` from it afterward. Verified with a from-scratch `cscripts/test_nworder.do` (zero coverage before): a 3-network reorder confirmed via `nwset, detail`'s `r(nets)'`, plus a data-integrity spot check (reordering must not corrupt any network's own matrix). All new/touched tests passing in both dev mode and production mode. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 11: _nwnodelab/_nwnodeid — crash fix plus a comma/space format bug

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_nwnodelab.ado`/`_nwnodeid.ado` — fixed the `_nwsyntax` crash class plus an independent comma/space label-format bug | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (internal, underscore-prefixed helpers, no `.sthlp`) | Follow-up to the triage prompted by unit 10's discovery that `nwqap`/`nwcloseness`/`nworder` were all broken via the deprecated `_nwsyntax` wrapper. `_nwnodelab` referenced `` `nodes' `` (never exported by `_nwsyntax`) at its bounds check, crashing every call with r(198) "invalid name" (an `if 2 > ` with a blank right-hand side); fixed by using `` `r(nodes)' `` from the `nwname` call already made on the previous line, rather than re-deriving it. `_nwnodeid` had the identical `` `nodes' `` reference in its numeric-nodelab branch, same fix applied. Fixing the crash exposed a second, completely independent, unrelated bug in **both** files: `nwname`'s `r(labs)` return is comma-separated (`nw_name.ado`'s own `invtokens(...,",")`), but `_nwnodelab` fed it directly into `` word N of `labs' `` and `_nwnodeid` fed it into `` foreach onelab in `labs' `` - both constructs expect a *space*-separated list, so the entire comma-joined string was treated as a single list item. `_nwnodelab` silently returned an empty label for any `nodeid` past 1 (no error at all); `_nwnodeid` reported every genuinely valid label as "not found" (r(6012)) - confirmed empirically for a network where node 2's label ("B") is unambiguously valid. Fixed by converting the comma-separated string to space-separated (`` subinstr local labs "," " ", all ``) in both files before use. Wrote `cscripts/test__nwnodelab.do` (new file, zero coverage before, covers both commands as a matched pair since they are exact inverses of each other): full nodeid<->label round trip for every node in a 3-node network, out-of-bounds/not-found error paths confirmed to still fire correctly now that the crash blocking them is gone, and the numeric-nodelab pass-through path (a third, separate code branch, also affected by the `nodes' bug) regression-guarded. Passing in both dev mode and production mode. Two other files (`nwdropnodes`, `nwkeepnodes`) call `_nwnodeid` and benefit from this fix, but were found during investigation to have their own, additional, deeper problems (see Pending below) - not fixed in this pass, scoped separately. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 12: nwutility — nine stacked bugs, never actually worked

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwutility` — fixed 9 distinct bugs across both code paths; corrected a stale `.sthlp` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Started as the next item in the `_nwsyntax` triage queue ("Medium" effort, self-contained, no chain dependency). On investigation it needed far more than a local-export swap — the command had apparently never worked end to end. Bugs found and fixed, in the order encountered: (1-2) the deprecated-`_nwsyntax`/unexported-`nodes` crash, in both the default and `intrvalue()`/`intrcost()` branches, fixed by switching to `nw_syntax` directly; (3) `intrvalue()`/`intrcost()` also called the separately-broken `_nwsyntax_other`, fixed the same way, which exposed a real local-name collision — calling `nw_syntax` a second time for `intrvalue()`/`intrcost()` silently overwrote the main network's own `netname`/`nodes` locals (the file already had a `local netname_backup` capturing this risk, unused, before the fix — the collision it anticipated was real, just never completed); fixed by capturing both the name and node count before any second call and restoring the name afterward. (4) A genuine copy-paste bug: the `intrcost()` branch pulled its matrix from `` `intrvalue' `` instead of `` `intrcost' ``. (5) The geodesic-distance step called `_nwgeodesic` (underscore-prefixed) - no such command exists, the real one is `nwgeodesic` - and routed the whole sub-command call through `nwgenerate`'s arithmetic-expression translator, which isn't valid usage of that translator (Mata "illegal arglist" - the same misuse pattern already found and fixed in `nwkatz` earlier this session); fixed by calling `nwgeodesic` directly with `name()`, the same pattern `nwcloseness` already uses. (6) `distance_distribution()`'s own Mata function sized its result matrix via `max(geonet)`, but the geodesic matrix's diagonal is missing by this package's convention and Mata's `max()` propagates a missing value as the maximum, crashing with a conformability error; fixed by zeroing the diagonal first (the function's own `dist>0` check already correctly excludes it from the actual tabulation). (7-8) `util_simple()`'s loop used `` i < cols(dd) `` instead of `` i <= cols(dd) ``, silently dropping the longest-distance bucket from every node's benefit sum - confirmed by hand computation before the fix (observed benefit values were exactly one distance-bucket short of the documented formula's expected total); `util_weighted()` had the identical off-by-one in both its loop bounds. (9) `util_weighted()`'s inner loop used `` geonet[i,j] >= 0 `` instead of `` > 0 `` - since the diagonal is 0 after fix (6), this double-counted the self term, which is already added back separately via `` + diagonal(w) ``, corrupting every row with a missing value whenever the intrinsic-value network's own diagonal was missing (the common case, see below). Also found, en route, a real Mata naming-collision landmine unrelated to any of the above: the weighted branch stored the analyzed network into a Mata matrix literally named `nw` - which is this package's own reserved global identifier (the `NWs` class instance holding all network state, from `unw_core.do`) - clobbering it and crashing the very next Mata statement with a class/real type mismatch; renamed to `netmat`. Verified BOTH code paths against the command's own documented formula (`U(i) = w_ii + sum_j[benefit^(d_ij) * w_ij] - sum_j[cost * y_ij]`, taken directly from `nwutility.sthlp`'s pre-existing "Description" section, which — reassuringly — was accurate even though the code implementing it was not) with hand-computed exact-integer test cases for both the default and weighted paths; every value matched exactly. Found and documented a real, previously-undocumented usability gotcha while testing the weighted path: `intrvalue()`'s w_ii (self-value) term is read from that network's own diagonal, which `nwset` leaves missing unless the network is built with the `selfloop` option - without it, `nwutility` now reports `_benefit`/`_util` as missing (loud, not silently wrong), which is itself now regression-tested. `nwutility.sthlp` was also found stale: it documented a single `value()` option that no longer exists in the code (the real options are `intrvalue()`/`intrcost()`, from an apparent unreflected refactor), plus a genuine typo ("Exonomic" for "Economic") and a missing `{p_end}` tag - all corrected; a "Supported network types" section was added per the harmonisation style standard. Wrote `cscripts/test_nwutility.do` (new file, zero coverage before): the default path against hand-computed values, the weighted path (with `selfloop`) against hand-computed values, and the no-`selfloop` missing-value case as an explicit regression guard. Passing in both dev mode and production mode. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 13: nwrecode fixed; nwreplacemat's same-size path fixed

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwrecode` — fixed the `_nwsyntax`/`_nwsyntax_other` crash class plus 2 more independent bugs; `nwreplacemat` — fixed its same-size code path (root cause: a legacy-global write the modern architecture never reads) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (both already had a `.sthlp`, neither needed doc changes) | Next item in the triage queue, tagged "self-contained, Medium effort" - turned out not to be self-contained at all: every code path in `nwrecode` ends by calling `nwreplacemat`, which was itself completely broken, so fixing `nwrecode` required also fixing `nwreplacemat`. `nwrecode` had the same unexported-`nodes`/`_nwsyntax_other` crash class as `nwqap`/`nwcloseness`/`nworder` (harmonisation units 9-10) in two places: the outer `_nwsyntax_other `` netname', max(9999)`` call, and a per-network `` _nwsyntax `onenet' `` call whose only purpose was to read `` `directed' `` - a local `_nwsyntax` never re-exports at all (unit 10's fix corrected the one broken `netname` re-export `_nwsyntax` does attempt; it never widened what the wrapper exports in the first place, so this reference was still dead even after that fix). Both replaced with `nw_syntax` calls (`nw_syntax`'s own `other()` option gives the exact `othernetname`/`othernodes`/etc. naming convention `_nwsyntax_other` used, a direct drop-in). Fixing the crash exposed two more, independent bugs: `` nwtoedge `onenet', forcedirected `` - `nwtoedge` has no such option; the real one that forces both `(i,j)` and `(j,i)` into the edgelist is `full` (confirmed via `nwtoedge.ado`'s own logic: "if the network is directed, `full` is enforced" - exactly the semantics `nwrecode` needs for a symmetric recode); and `` nwfromedge _fromid _toid `onenet', ... `` - `_fromid`/`_toid` never existed as variable names, `nwtoedge`'s actual default output variables are `_ego`/`_alter` (confirmed against `nwtoedge.ado`'s own `ego()`/`alter()` option defaults). With all four fixed, `nwrecode` still failed at its final step - every branch (in-place, `generate()`, `prefix()`) ends in a call to `nwreplacemat`, which was completely non-functional: its same-size path wrote the new matrix to `` nw_mata`id' `` and the directed/undirected flag to `` $nwdirected_`id' ``, both legacy pre-2016 globals the modern `netobj`/`NWdef` Mata class architecture never reads from - the call returned no error and appeared to succeed, but silently left the network completely unchanged. Fixed using the modern `` `netobj'->set_edge() ``/`` `netobj'->set_directed() `` methods instead (the same methods already used elsewhere this session, e.g. `nwkatz`'s own in-place matrix update) - plus `nwreplacemat`'s own separate `_nwsyntax` crash (same unexported-`nodes` bug) and a literal `erorr 6082` typo in its dimension-validation guard (would have crashed with "command erorr not found" instead of raising the intended error). Verified both files together end to end: `nwrecode`'s in-place and `generate()` paths both checked against hand-computed recoded values; `nwreplacemat` additionally checked directly for both a symmetric and an asymmetric replacement matrix (confirming the directed/undirected auto-detection survived the fix), plus the invalid-dimensions guard. `nwreplacemat`'s size-*changing* path (needed by `nwdropnodes`/`nwkeepnodes`, not by `nwrecode`, which never resizes) is **not** fixed by this unit - it remains entangled with further legacy globals (`` $nwsize_`id' ``/`` $nw_`id' ``/`` $nwlabs_`id' ``) not touched here; see Pending. Wrote `cscripts/test_nwrecode.do` and `cscripts/test_nwreplacemat.do` (both new files, zero coverage before). Passing in both dev mode and production mode. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 14: nwbalance zero-closed-triads fix

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwbalance` — fixed the zero-closed-triads r(2000) crash | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (`.sthlp` re-certified, content unchanged) | Root-caused the pre-existing, previously-documented-as-a-limitation gap: a network with zero closed triads (e.g. a path graph, no triangles) crashed with r(2000) "no observations" because the command's Stata-reshape-based triad enumeration pipeline fed a completely empty dataset into Stata's own `collapse` command at the very last step, which errors on zero observations unconditionally, regardless of what it's collapsing. Fixed with an explicit `qui count` guard right before the `collapse` call: when the filtered triad-level dataset is empty, every node genuinely has 0 closed triads (not an undefined count), so the guard builds that all-zero per-node result directly from the network's own node list (captured via `levelsof` before the command's own `preserve`) instead of attempting to collapse an empty dataset. The per-node balance ratio (0 balanced / 0 closed) is correctly left missing, not silently reported as 0 or 1 - `r(balance)` and `_balance` follow the same convention. Found and fixed a second, separate, more serious bug while building this fix: the first draft used a bare `clear` command to reset the scratch dataset for the zero-case, not realizing Stata's bare `clear` behaves like `clear all` - it wiped Mata memory too, including this package's own singleton network-state object, corrupting every command called afterward for the rest of the session (surfaced immediately as a `nws_create() not found` Mata error on the very next test block, `capture noisily do`-wrapped tests never even reached their real content). Fixed by using `drop _all` instead (dataset-only, leaves Mata state untouched) - caught before commit by running the *entire* pre-existing test file end to end, not just the new assertion in isolation, exactly the failure mode this session's own "always run the full suite, not just the new bit" regression-sweep discipline exists to catch. Replaced the pre-existing "known limitation, documented not fixed" test block in `cscripts/test_nwbalance.do` with real assertions (zero closed/balanced/unbalanced triads, missing per-node ratio) rather than adding a new file, and confirmed the rest of that file's pre-existing assertions (signed K4, all-positive, single-negative-tie, custom `generate()` names) all still pass unchanged. Passing in both dev mode and production mode. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 15: nwqap permutation-degeneracy robustness fix

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwqap` — permutations that produce a degenerate (no-variation) dependent network now retry instead of aborting the whole command | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (behavior change, not an interface change - no doc update needed) | Follow-up to the robustness gap flagged (not fixed) while building unit 9's `cscripts/test_nwqap.do`: a QAP permutation can by chance produce a fully degenerate dependent network (all ties one value), which the chosen regression command can't fit (e.g. `logit`'s "outcome does not vary") - previously this aborted the *entire* `nwqap` call with r(2000) on the very first degenerate draw, discarding every already-computed permutation. Fixed by wrapping the per-permutation regression call in `capture` and retrying with a fresh permutation draw on failure, up to 20 consecutive attempts before raising a clear error (a pathologically small/structured network could theoretically exhaust this, but no such case was found or is expected in practice). Verified the fix is real, not just theoretically sound, by instrumenting a debug copy of the command and confirming across several `set seed` values that permutations genuinely do hit this failure mode for small/sparse test networks (1-4 retries needed across 10 permutations, several seeds), and that every one of those runs now completes successfully where it would previously have crashed on the first bad draw. Extended `cscripts/test_nwqap.do` with a dedicated regression test using `set seed 5` and a small, sparse 4-node network pair confirmed (via that same instrumentation) to need 4 retries across its 10 permutations - asserts the command completes with `_rc == 0`. Also removed the now-stale "not fixed here" caveat from that test file's header comment. Passing in both dev mode and production mode. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 16: nw_helpwriter cascading-failure isolation + bulk .sthlp certification

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nw_helpwriter.ado` — isolated the line-copy crash so it can no longer cascade across a batch; bulk-certified 43 previously-stale/never-certified `.sthlp` files | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | n/a (tooling + doc-only, no `.sthlp` of its own) | A survey (prompted by this session's own standing harmonisation mandate to audit every help file) found 54 commands with a doc header but no "last certified" line - 52 with an existing test, 2 (`nwdyads`, `nwnoderename`) with no test at all. Ran a bulk `nw_helpwriter` pass across the 52; it broke immediately: `nwimport.ado` has genuine program-body content (not a doc-comment typo, unlike every prior instance of this bug found this session) that trips the already-known macro-mangling fragility and crashes with r(132) "too few quotes" - and since `nw_helpwriter` never closed its two open file handles on that path, every command processed *afterward* in the same batch failed too, with a cascading r(110) "file already open". Fixed by wrapping the copy loop in `capture noisily { ... }` and unconditionally closing both handles afterward (`capture file close`) - a pure error-isolation fix; the successful-copy path is byte-for-byte unchanged, and the underlying macro-mangling fragility itself is deliberately still not fixed (assessed as genuinely riskier than its "Small-Medium" label suggested: correctly escaping arbitrary Stata source lines without extensive edge-case testing could introduce *silent* corruption, which is worse than the current *loud* crash). Re-ran the batch clean: 43 of 52 now certified (their tests pass); `nwimport` correctly stays uncertified (its own test independently fails anyway - one of the 8 known baseline failures, unrelated to this fix); `nwplotjs` hit a third, distinct failure mode from the same underlying fragility - its heavily backtick-quoted embedded-JavaScript content eventually exceeds Stata's maximum macro/line length rather than erroring cleanly - confirmed via `git diff` to be completely unmodified (not silently truncated) and left uncertified. Verified every one of the 43 newly-certified files is a faithful, complete regeneration (not corrupted or truncated) by diffing each `.sthlp` against the exact `/*** ... ***/` block(s) of its own `.ado` source, whitespace differences aside - the git diffs against HEAD looked alarmingly large for several files (up to hundreds of lines) purely because HEAD's committed `.sthlp` predated a *later* `.ado` doc-header update that was never regenerated into docs before now (e.g. `nwname.ado`'s doc header is dated 2019, describing options - `newtitle()`, `new2mode()`, `newvalued()`, `newselfloop()` - the committed `.sthlp` never mentioned at all) - genuine, valuable doc/code staleness this pass fixes, not accidental data loss. A handful of pre-existing, already-known cosmetic artifacts survive in a few regenerated files (e.g. a doc example's `` `i' ``/`` `nets' `` loses its escaping backslash and collapses to an empty macro reference) - the same class of fragility, left as-is rather than hand-patched, consistent with not fixing the mechanism itself in this pass. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly - this is a documentation-only change, no `.ado` program-body logic was touched. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 17: Part I re-audit — nw2project/nwburt already done; a real nwconstraint gotcha found

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Part I re-audit of `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md`/`docs/ROADMAP.md` against current repo state; `nwburt`/`nw2project` given "Supported network types" sections; a real `nwconstraint` documentation gap found and fixed | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Prompted by the standing instruction's explicit warning not to trust the old feature audit/roadmap without re-verifying against the current, harmonised repository. Immediately validated: `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own "Top 20" list still showed `nw2project` (two-mode projection) and `nwburt` (Burt's structural-holes suite) as items 1-2, unchecked, described as a "phantom command" and "deleted during uncommitted-years drift" respectively — but both `nw2project.ado` and `nwburt.ado` actually exist, are committed (`1ba195e`/`531e018`), and their existing test files (`cscripts/test_nw2project.do`/`test_nwburt.do`) pass cleanly today; the roadmap checkmarks were simply never added when that work was completed (in an earlier part of this session, before the portion visible in this context window). Re-verified both directly rather than trusting the stale claim. Found one real, genuinely-missing piece of harmonisation for each: neither had a "Supported network types" section — added to both, classifying `nwburt` identically to `nwconstraint` (same underlying `p_ij` formula, same directed-asymmetric/signed-unsupported caveats) and `nw2project` as **T3** (explicit, user-requested projection - the correct, non-silent way to project in this package, exactly the harmonisation brief's own "never silently project" requirement). Cross-checking `nwburt`'s per-node `_constraint` against `nwconstraint`'s raw dyadic matrix (added in harmonisation unit 8, before `nwburt`'s existence was rediscovered) surfaced a real, subtle documentation gap: unit 8's own docs suggested a plain full-row sum of `nwconstraint`'s output gives the standard per-node aggregate constraint, but the standard literature definition sums only over a node's *actual direct neighbors* - confirmed empirically on the same 4-node star network already used for `nwconstraint`'s own certification: a leaf node's aggregate constraint is exactly 1 via `nwburt` (correctly neighbor-restricted) but 1.222 via a naive full-row sum of `nwconstraint`'s matrix (silently over-counting the nonzero indirect-only contribution to non-neighbors, `p2_ij`, for pairs with no direct tie at all). Fixed `nwconstraint.ado`'s own doc header to state the restriction precisely (with a corrected worked example using an explicit tie-mask rather than a bare `rowsum()`) and cross-reference `nwburt` as the tool that already does this correctly, rather than leaving users to derive the restriction themselves. Also updated `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md`'s status legend to formally define the **F** category (implemented but not yet certified/documented to current harmonisation standards) the standing instruction requested, and reconciled several other stale claims found in the same pass (`nwkatz`'s already-completed correctness audit, `nwbalance`'s already-completed documentation/test/bugfix work, `nwqap`'s already-completed crash fixes and test coverage, `nwcug`'s already-completed CUG-test implementation) that the original audit predates. All touched `.sthlp` files regenerated via `nw_helpwriter` (clean, self-certified); `test_nwburt.do`/`test_nw2project.do`/`test_nwconstraint.do` all re-verified passing after the doc changes (doc-only, no functional code touched in any of the three commands). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 18: nwbetween weighted (Dijkstra) betweenness

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwbetween, weighted alpha()` — genuine Dijkstra-based weighted betweenness, alongside the unchanged unweighted default | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | First concrete implementation item after the Part I re-audit (unit 17) reconciled the standing feature audit against the current repository. Chosen as the next target because it directly closes an explicitly-flagged "silently ignores weights" gap (`docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md`'s own executive summary: "betweenness centrality silently ignores weights (plain BFS, not Dijkstra, despite the Brandes' algorithm structure)"), was independently flagged "Medium value, Small effort" in `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own priority ranking, and had a dead, never-implemented `calculate_betweenness_weighted()` stub already sitting in `unw_core.do` (commented-out, syntactically incomplete) marking exactly where it belonged. Implemented as a Dijkstra generalization of the existing, already-verified-correct unweighted `calculate_betweenness()` (Brandes 2001's two-phase algorithm: single-source shortest-path-DAG discovery, then back-propagation of dependencies) - the FIFO dequeue becomes a linear-scan extract-min over unsettled nodes (Mata has no built-in priority queue with decrease-key), and the unweighted hop-relaxation `D[w]==D[v]+1` becomes the real-valued `D[w]==D[v]+cost(v,w)`.
