Edge cost is `edge_weight(v,w)^alpha`, deliberately reusing the exact same weight-to-distance exponent convention this package already establishes for `nwgeodesic`/`calculate_distances()` (`alpha(1)`, the default: raw weight used directly as cost; `alpha(0)`: every positive tie costs 1) rather than inventing a new, inconsistent weighting idiom - matches the ambiguity-resolution hierarchy's preference for established package-wide patterns over a fresh one. Only strictly positive ties are edges (same `>0` filter the unweighted version already uses), so a negative tie in a signed network is excluded exactly as it already was, not silently misread as a Dijkstra edge cost (which a negative weight cannot validly be). Verified two ways: (1) a hand-computed 4-node weighted network (A-B=1, A-C=4, B-C=2, C-D=1) where the direct A-C tie is deliberately *not* the shortest path (A-B-C costs 1+2=3, cheaper than the direct tie's own cost of 4) - worked out the expected betweenness values by hand (A=0, B=2, C=2, D=0) before running the code, confirming the implementation independent of the algorithm's own internal logic; (2) `alpha(0)` reduces every positive tie's cost to exactly 1 regardless of its actual weight, which must make the weighted algorithm produce results bit-for-bit identical to the existing, separately-verified unweighted `calculate_betweenness()` on the same network - confirmed via `mata: assert(rw0 == ru)` directly on the Mata return values, a strong internal-consistency check that the Dijkstra generalization is a faithful superset of the original algorithm, not a parallel reimplementation that happens to agree on one example. `nwsym`'s existing default symmetrization mode (`max` - already weighted-aware, confirmed by reading its own code before assuming) is reused unchanged for the `nosym`-absent case, so weighted and unweighted betweenness share identical, already-correct symmetrization behavior. This is a shared-infrastructure change (`unw_core.do`, the core Mata file every command ultimately depends on) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified the same hand-computed and alpha-consistency tests directly against the compiled library, not just in dev mode. Extended `cscripts/test_nwbetween.do` (not a new file). Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly - critical given the shared-core-file blast radius. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 19: nwqap eclass integration

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwqap, eclass` — proper `ereturn post` of `e(b)`/`e(V)`, plus `e(N)`/`e(permutations)`/`e(cmd)`/`e(title)`/`e(depvar)`/`e(qap_regcmd)`/`e(pvalues)` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Selected as the next implementation target under Part XIX of the standing instruction's explicit "special priority" flag on network inference (`nwregress`/`nwlogit`, QAP/MRQAP, `eclass`, stored estimates, postestimation) - `nwqap` already existed and worked, but only wrote a bare `e(pvalues)` via `st_matrix`/`st_global`, with no real `e(b)`/`e(V)`, so `estimates store`/`estimates table`/`test`/`lincom` were all unusable. Converted to a proper `eclass` command: `e(b)` is the original (non-permuted) regression's coefficient vector; `e(V)` is a diagonal matrix built from each coefficient's own QAP-permutation variance (not a classical OLS/logit covariance - dyadic network data violates the independent-observations assumption those classical formulas require, which is the entire reason QAP permutation testing exists) - deliberately correct rather than fabricating a conventional-but-invalid covariance just to make postestimation "work". Along the way, fixed a real, independent bug in the existing p-value computation loop: it read `r(Var)` for each variable's permutation-distribution variance, but several `count if ...`/`count` calls (already part of the pre-existing logic, needed for the p-value itself) ran *after* the `sum` that set `r(Var)` and *before* the point where it was read - silently overwriting it with each `count` call's own r(N)-only results, leaving the captured "variance" meaningless. Fixed by capturing `local entryvar = r(Var)` immediately after `sum`, before any subsequent r-class command can clobber it. A second, harder bug surfaced only empirically, not by inspection: `nwqap ... , type(logit)` (logit is the command's own default) crashed with r(507) "name conflict" on `ereturn post`, while the identical code path with `type(regress)` worked cleanly - traced (via a debug copy instrumented with `matrix list` immediately before the crashing call) to `logit`'s own `e(b)` carrying an equation name (its depvar) on its column stripe, while `regress`'s `e(b)` does not; a bare `matrix colnames matname = name1 name2 ...` call (no explicit equation-name prefixes) *preserves* whatever equation name is already on a stripe rather than clearing it (documented Stata behavior, not a bug in `matrix colnames` itself) - so `nwqap`'s own `e(b)` ended up column-striped `logit_depvar:iv1`/`logit_depvar:_cons` while its freshly-built `e(V)` (via `diag()`, no pre-existing stripe) ended up with bare, unqualified names - a stripe mismatch between `b` and `V` that `ereturn post` correctly rejects. Fixed by explicitly blanking both matrices' equation names (`matrix coleq/roweq matname = _`) before assigning column/row names, so the two stripes always match regardless of which regression command's own naming convention produced the original `e(b)`. Verified across `regress`, `logit`, `probit`, and `cloglog` (all four already named in the command's own weighted-DV warning list) - all four now post cleanly; `estimates store`/`estimates table` round-trip correctly across a `regress` and a `logit` result stored side by side; `test`/`lincom` postestimation both run and give sensible output on the `regress` case (confirmed by hand: `lincom iv1` on the exact-2 coefficient case reports `2.3` - matches `nwqap`'s own displayed coefficient exactly, as it must). No `predict` subroutine implemented at this point (out of scope for this unit - tracked in the Pending table below; done later, unit 61, as `predict(newnetname)`). `nwqap.sthlp`'s existing hand-maintained "Stored results" section expanded to document the new `eclass` scalars/macros/matrices and the `e(V)`-is-not-classical-variance caveat explicitly (still a hand edit, not `nw_helpwriter`-generated - that migration remains its own separate Pending item). Extended `cscripts/test_nwqap.do` with dedicated eclass assertions (`e(N)`/`e(permutations)`/`e(cmd)`/`e(depvar)`/`e(qap_regcmd)`, `e(b)`/`e(V)` shape and finiteness, an `estimates store`/`estimates table` round-trip across both `regress` and `logit`, and `test`/`lincom` smoke calls) plus a regenerated `lib/cscripts_prod/test_nwqap.do`; both pass cleanly. This change is `nwqap.ado`-local only (no `unw_core.do` touched), so no library rebuild was needed for the production-mode run. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 20: nwsimilar/nwdissimilar/nwhierarchy chain fully unblocked

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar`/`nwhierarchy` — root cause of the long-standing "chain not functional end-to-end" blocker found and fixed | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Selected under Part XVII's explicit special-priority flag on structural equivalence and classical SNA. Two earlier layers of breakage in this chain were already found and fixed (`nwtomatafast`'s dead pre-2016 reference; missing `matastrict` declarations), but a third bug left every non-`pearson` call to `nwsimilar`/`nwdissimilar` (and therefore `nwhierarchy`'s default path, which depends on `nwdissimilar`) crashing with "`<fn>() not found`" (r(3499)) - investigated once already, inconclusively. Root-caused this session with a minimal two-ado-file repro (`probeC.ado` defines and calls its own private Mata function successfully; `probeD.ado`, a *separate* ado-file, cannot see that same function at all): **Mata functions defined in an ado-file's own trailing `mata: ... end` block are private to that ado-file** - visible only to Mata code running from *within that same file*, not to a *different* ado-file's own `mata:` blocks, even when that second file is invoked synchronously from the first in the same Stata session. `nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar` used to pass an *unevaluated* function-call expression (e.g. `euclidean_dissimilarity(mat, dtype)`) straight through to `nwset`'s `mat()` option, which evaluates whatever it's given inside `nwset.ado`'s *own* private Mata scope - so it could never see the calling file's private functions, regardless of adopath, timing, or load order (all previously ruled out without finding this). By contrast, an *interactive Mata workspace variable* (e.g. `mata: x = 5`) - as opposed to a function *definition* - is not ado-file-private and persists across files' own private scopes (confirmed with the same minimal repro). Fixed by evaluating each dissimilarity/similarity function inside its own defining file first (where it *is* visible), into such a workspace variable, then passing that already-computed matrix's *name* to `nwset` instead of an unevaluated function call. This is a general Mata/ado-file scoping fact worth remembering package-wide, not a nwdissimilar/nwsimilar-specific quirk - any future command that wants to hand a private helper function's *result* to a different ado-file must compute it locally first, the same way. Two further, independent bugs surfaced once the chain was actually reachable for the first time: (1) the resulting (dis)similarity network's diagonal came back missing (nwset's default when `selfloop` is not given), even though a node's (dis)similarity from itself is a genuine, well-defined value that the functions themselves already compute correctly (0 for dissimilarity, a type-specific maximum for similarity) - fixed by passing `selfloop` to `nwset`; this by itself was enough to make Stata's own `clustermat` (which `nwhierarchy` delegates the actual clustering to) reject the matrix outright ("mymat has missing values", r(504)). (2) the derived network never inherited the source network's own node labels (nwset defaulted to generic n1/n2/... instead), which is harmless for `nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar` used standalone but broke `nwhierarchy`'s default path specifically: nwcommands' master dataset keys rows by node label across every currently-tracked network, so two differently-labeled 4-node networks looked like a disjoint 8-node union to `clustermat` ("number of selected observations must match dimension of mymat", r(198)) - fixed by defaulting `nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar` to the source network's own labels (via `nwname`) when the caller doesn't specify one; a genuine r()-pollution bug (r(mata) silently overwritten by the following r-class `nwname` call) was caught and fixed while making this change, the same bug class already fixed in `nwqap.ado` (unit 19) - `r(mata)` is now captured into a local immediately after `nwtomatafast`, before any further r-class command runs. A fourth, unrelated bug was also fixed along the way: `nwsimilar`'s `type(crossproduct)` dispatched to `hamming_similarity()` (a copy-paste of the line above it) instead of the already-fully-implemented-but-dead `crossproduct_similarity()` - fixed to call the correct function. Verified with hand-derived worked examples on a 4-cycle network (A-B-C-D-A), where the two structurally-equivalent node pairs (A,C and B,D - identical tie profiles once self is excluded) are exactly, independently checkable: dissimilarity 0 and similarity 1 (or the type-appropriate maximum) between equivalent pairs, strictly different values between adjacent (non-equivalent) pairs, for every one of the 5 dissimilarity types and 5 similarity types, on both directed and undirected input, plus all three of `nwhierarchy`'s input forms (default/`dismat()`/`disnet()`). New `cscripts/test_nwdissimilar.do`, `cscripts/test_nwsimilar.do`, `cscripts/test_nwhierarchy.do` (all three had zero prior test coverage), plus production-mode copies - all pass cleanly in both modes. Added "Supported network types" sections to all three `.sthlp` files (none had one). This unblocks the package's entire structural-equivalence/role-and-position-analysis surface, previously entirely inert - `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md` area E and F updated accordingly. Full-suite regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 21: nwconcor (CONCOR structural-equivalence blockmodel) - new command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwconcor` — new command implementing CONCOR (Breiger, Boorman & Arabie 1975), recursive structural-equivalence blockmodeling | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Selected under Part XVII's explicit special-priority flag (structural equivalence and classical SNA), directly building on unit 20's freshly-unblocked `nwsimilar`/`nwdissimilar`/`nwhierarchy` chain and closing one of `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md` area F's "confirmed E across the entire area" gaps (no blockmodel functionality existed anywhere). New `NWdef::calculate_concor(splits, valued, maxiter)` Mata method in `unw_core.do` (an instance method delegating to standalone `ConcorConverge()`/`ConcorSplitIDs()` helpers - the same "instance method delegates to a standalone function" pattern `calculate_modularity()`/`Louvain()` already established for the community-detection work earlier this session), plus a new `nwconcor.ado`/`.sthlp` following the `nwcommunity.ado` template. Each node's tie profile (outgoing ties stacked on incoming ties, self-tie excluded) is correlated against every other node's (Mata's built-in `correlation()`, not a hand-rolled formula); the resulting matrix is repeatedly re-correlated with itself until it converges to (near-)±1 values or `maxiter` is reached; nodes are split by the sign of their correlation with a reference node. Unlike `nwcommunity` (Louvain), CONCOR needs no `symmetrize` - a node's profile already separates its outgoing and incoming ties, so directed networks are handled directly. `splits(k)` recursively re-splits each resulting block using only its own members' ties (the classical recursive CONCOR procedure, not a single bisection applied `k` times to the whole network), producing up to 2^`k` blocks; a block with no further internal structure to split on (all members tie only to nodes outside it) simply stays whole rather than being forced apart - a legitimate, documented CONCOR outcome, not an error. A node with literally zero ties in the *original* network is rejected explicitly with a clear message (it has no profile to compare against anyone else's at any depth) - checked once, up front, distinct from the *within-block* "isolate" case that arises naturally during recursion and is handled silently by continuing without a crash. Two genuinely new, non-obvious findings from building this: (1) **Mata's own `_error(code, message)` silently hits an undocumented ~100-character cap on its message argument**, failing with a *different*, unrelated-looking error ("argument out of range", r(3300)) that gives no hint the actual problem is message length - confirmed by direct bisection (100 chars: fine; 110: fails) after a genuinely confusing debugging session where a minimal repro was needed to even localize the fault to the message string itself. Worked around with `errprintf()` + `exit(error())` instead, which has no such limit found - a package-wide gotcha worth remembering for any future long, informative Mata-level error message. (2) **A real, independent, pre-existing bug shared by `nwcomponents.ado` and `nwcommunity.ado`** (both structural ancestors of this file, copy-pasted from): their own "does the output variable already exist" check tested the *bare* `generate()` stem, not the actual suffixed name (`_component1`, `_component2`, ...) about to be created on a given `netlist` iteration - so on the second (or later) network in a multi-network call, Stata's own variable-name abbreviation let `confirm variable _component` silently match an already-existing `_component1` from the first iteration, falsely blocking every subsequent iteration even though its own real target name was still free (`nwdegree.ado`'s own already-established `, exact` idiom, found while investigating, confirms this exact bug class was already known and fixed there once before - just not propagated to these two). Fixed in all three files (`nwconcor.ado`'s own version was written correctly from the start once the bug was understood); added regression tests exercising the exact netlist scenario to `test_nwcomponents.do`/`test_nwcommunity.do`, both re-verified passing. Verified `nwconcor` itself with a structurally-*forced* worked example (two fully disjoint 3-cliques - any correct implementation must separate them, since within-clique nodes have perfectly correlated tie profiles and across-clique nodes have none) for the base 2-block case, and a specifically-constructed 4-clique network with a deliberate weak distinguishing cross-tie per "super-block" (four *symmetric, fully disconnected* cliques alone were found, through direct experimentation, to have no signal to prefer any particular pairing at the top split - a genuine, expected CONCOR limitation for perfectly ambiguous structure, not a bug - so the test network was redesigned to give the first split real information to work with) for the recursive `splits(2)` case; both match hand-derivable expectations exactly. Also covered: isolate rejection, directed networks (no `symmetrize` needed), `measure(binary)`/`measure(valued)`, `generate()`/`replace`, `netlist` multi-network support, and invalid `splits()`/`maxiter()` input. New `cscripts/test_nwconcor.do` plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly, as do the regression additions to `test_nwcomponents.do`/`test_nwcommunity.do`. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both `nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_alphabetical.sthlp` indexes (which, incidentally, were also missing entries for `nwcommunity`/`nwmodularity` themselves from their own earlier, pre-visible-context session - added those too while touching these same files for the same reason). This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Full regression sweep (both modes, including the `nwcomponents`/`nwcommunity` fix) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 22: nwcoreperiphery (discrete core-periphery detection) - new command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwcoreperiphery` — new command implementing discrete core-periphery detection (Borgatti & Everett 1999) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Second consecutive Part XVII special-priority item (after unit 21's `nwconcor`), closing another piece of `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md` area F's "confirmed E across the entire area" gap - no core-periphery functionality of any kind existed anywhere before this. New `NWdef::calculate_coreperiphery(valued, maxiter)` Mata method in `unw_core.do`, delegating to standalone `CorePeriphery()`/`nw_cp_fitness()` helpers (the same "instance method delegates to standalone function" pattern established by `calculate_modularity()`/`Louvain()` and reused for `calculate_concor()`/`ConcorSplitIDs()` in unit 21), plus a new `nwcoreperiphery.ado`/`.sthlp` mirroring `nwcommunity.ado`'s template. The discrete Borgatti-Everett model expects a tie between any pair where at least one member is "core" (core-core and core-periphery ties both structurally expected; only periphery-periphery ties are not) - `nwcoreperiphery` searches for the 0/1 assignment whose implied pattern correlates as highly as possible with the network actually observed (Mata's built-in `correlation()`, restricted to off-diagonal entries via a precomputed mask - self-comparisons would otherwise bias the correlation coefficient toward 1). Optimization is greedy local search seeded from a degree-based initial guess (above-average-degree nodes start as core), then repeatedly trying to flip each node's status in a fixed 1..n order (matching `nwcommunity`'s own reproducibility convention - no randomness anywhere in this algorithm, unlike `nwpermute`/`nwpref`'s RNG-driven history earlier this session), keeping any flip that improves the fitness score, until a full sweep produces no further improvement or `maxiter` sweeps are reached - the same character of algorithm (and the same acceptance of a good-but-not-necessarily-global local optimum) `nwcommunity`'s own Louvain implementation already uses for modularity. Unlike `nwconcor`/`nwcommunity`, the classical model is inherently undirected (core-periphery does not distinguish incoming from outgoing ties), so directed networks are symmetrized automatically - no `symmetrize` option needed or offered, a deliberate, documented interface difference from `nwcommunity`'s own explicit-opt-in convention, chosen because there is no meaningful directed variant of this specific model to preserve by requiring an extra flag. A network with literally no ties anywhere is rejected explicitly (there is no structure for any core-periphery pattern to fit); unlike `nwconcor`, an individual isolate node causes no error - core-periphery's fitness is a whole-matrix correlation, not a per-node profile comparison, so a degree-0 node simply lands in the periphery without any division-by-zero risk. Verified with a network built to have an *exact* discrete core-periphery structure by construction (3 fully-interconnected core nodes, each also tied to every periphery node; 3 periphery nodes with no ties among themselves) - correctly recovered exactly, with fitness exactly 1 (a structure-forced check: any implementation that fails to find this partition, or reports a fitness below 1 for a network that IS the ideal pattern, has a real bug). An initial hand-designed "noisy" test case exposed something worth recording: the algorithm converged to a *different, better-fitting* partition than the one the test intuitively expected - confirmed by direct fitness comparison (0.612 for the found partition vs. 0.583 for the assumed "obvious" one) that this was the local search correctly out-performing a naive human guess, not a bug, before finalizing that part of the test around the empirically-verified value rather than an unverified assumption. Also verified: determinism (bit-identical results across repeated calls, checked explicitly given the algorithm's own fixed-order design intentionally excludes randomness), isolate handling, the empty-network guard, directed-network auto-symmetrization, `measure(binary)`/`measure(valued)`, `generate()`/`replace`, `netlist` multi-network support (using the already-correct `, exact` suffixed-name check from the start, having learned that lesson while building unit 21's `nwconcor`), and invalid `maxiter()` input. New `cscripts/test_nwcoreperiphery.do` plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both `nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_alphabetical.sthlp` indexes. This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 23: nwbrokerage (Gould-Fernandez brokerage roles) - new command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwbrokerage` — new command implementing the 5 Gould-Fernandez (1989) brokerage roles | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Third consecutive Part XVII special-priority item (after units 21-22's `nwconcor`/`nwcoreperiphery`), closing `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md` area D's last remaining "confirmed absent" gap (structural holes/brokerage). New `NWdef::calculate_brokerage(group)` Mata method in `unw_core.do`, using the sparse `neighbors()`/`neighbors_in()` accessors directly (the same primitives `calculate_components()`/`calculate_kcore()` already use) rather than a dense adjacency matrix, so it scales the same way those do - a genuine architectural choice, not just following precedent for its own sake, since brokerage inherently only needs each node's own in/out neighbor lists, never the full matrix. For every directed two-path `a -> b -> c` (`a` != `c`) through each node `b`, classifies the role using the group membership of `a`, `b`, `c` (coordinator/gatekeeper/representative/consultant/liaison - see the command's own doc header for the exact definitions) and generates 5 new Stata variables holding each node's count per role. Directed networks use `a` from `b`'s in-neighbors and `c` from its out-neighbors directly (the model's own natural definition); undirected networks degrade gracefully since `neighbors_in()` already falls back to `neighbors()` for them (confirmed - no separate undirected-specific formula needed). `group()` is a required, caller-supplied Stata variable - unlike `nwconcor`/`nwcoreperiphery`/`nwcommunity`, this command does not detect groups itself, matching `nwmodularity`'s own `group()` convention for scoring an externally-supplied partition. Verified with a network built to exercise all 5 roles through a single node simultaneously in one call, with the expected role for each of its 6 two-paths hand-derived from the role definitions themselves before running the code (not reverse-engineered from the output) - matched exactly; every other node's row was independently asserted all-zero (they have no in-and-out neighbor pairs to classify at all, not merely "untested"). While building this, found and fixed a genuinely non-obvious, previously-undocumented (in this session) Stata gotcha: **`_rc` is only refreshed by a command that goes through Stata's normal *displayed* execution path** - `quietly`-prefixed commands, `mata:` blocks, `local` assignment, and inherently-silent commands like a bare `confirm` do **not** update `_rc` even when they succeed (confirmed directly: `qui sum x` and a bare `confirm variable x` both leave a prior nonzero `_rc` completely untouched; only a *captured* command deterministically sets `_rc` to exactly its own wrapped result). `nwbrokerage.ado`'s own display block uses `qui sum` in a loop (to build a compact role/total table) rather than a single visible `tab` call the way `nwcomponents`/`nwcommunity`/`nwconcor`/`nwcoreperiphery`'s own display blocks do - confirmed by direct comparison that those four are unaffected (their `noi tab` calls do refresh `_rc` properly) - so this was the one place in this session's run of new commands that actually hit it: the earlier, perfectly ordinary `capture confirm variable ..., exact` already-exists probes (expected to fail, since the variable doesn't exist yet) left `_rc=111` stuck all the way through an otherwise fully successful call, silently misleading any caller checking `_rc` right afterward. Fixed with one explicit, silent `capture confirm variable <a variable just created>` at the end of each iteration; directly regression-tested (`assert _rc == 0` immediately after a successful call, both with and without `silent`). New `cscripts/test_nwbrokerage.do` plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both `nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_alphabetical.sthlp` indexes. This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 24: nw2degree (two-mode degree centrality) - new command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nw2degree` — new command implementing Borgatti & Everett (1997) two-mode degree centrality | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | First Part XVIII special-priority item (genuine, non-projection-based two-mode/bipartite analysis) - closes the "Two-mode centrality... E" piece of `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md` area N's gap list. New `NWdef::calculate_2mode_degree()` Mata method in `unw_core.do`: a node's ordinary degree can only ever reach as high as the *other* mode's size (a mode-1 node can never tie to another mode-1 node in a genuine two-mode network), so `nwdegree`'s usual `n-1` normalization is wrong here - each node's raw degree is instead divided by the size of the mode it does *not* belong to, via the already-existing `get_modes()` accessor (a string rowvector of `"1"`/`"2"` per node, already relied on by `nw2project.ado` for the same purpose) rather than assuming a fixed node-index range for each mode. This assumption-avoidance turned out to matter in practice: direct empirical testing while building this found that `nwset`'s own `get_2mode_edge()` places the *columns* of the original bipartite input matrix first (as node indices 1..c) and the *rows* second (as node indices c+1..n) - the opposite of a naive rows-then-columns reading of a bipartite `mat()` argument - confirmed by constructing a small hand-labeled example and checking `get_modes()`'s actual output against the intended labeling before writing any test assertions, not assumed. Verified with a hand-computable 3-actor/2-event bipartite network (actor A ties to both events, B and C each tie to only one) giving exactly-checkable values (events: 2/3 each; A: 1; B, C: 0.5 each) plus a second, mode-size-imbalanced example (4 actors, 2 events) confirming the normalization uses each node's *own* other-mode size, not a single shared denominator. Node-value lookups in the test use `_nwnode` (the label), not a fixed Stata observation number - the same discipline already established for `nwdegree`'s own netlist certification, since `nw_datasync`'s row ordering does not necessarily match raw Mata node index order; a first attempt used `st_data()` on a mixed string+numeric varlist, which silently returns the string column as all-missing rather than erroring or auto-promoting to a string matrix - fixed by fetching the label (`st_sdata()`) and the value (`st_data()`) as two separate calls. A `1e-6` tolerance is used rather than a tighter one - the generated variable is an ordinary (float-precision) Stata variable via a plain `gen x = .` (the same convention `nwcommunity`/`nwconcor`/`nwcoreperiphery` already use, harmless for their own integer-valued outputs; `nw2degree` is the first of this session's new commands with a genuinely fractional result, which is what surfaced float rounding at a tighter tolerance - confirmed directly, not assumed, before settling on `1e-6`). A one-mode network is rejected explicitly with a clear error, the opposite convention of most commands in this package (which default to accepting one-mode and erroring on unexpected two-mode input). New `cscripts/test_nw2degree.do` plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both `nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_alphabetical.sthlp` indexes. This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 25: nwego (ego-network size and density) - new command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwego` — new command implementing ego-network size and density | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the size/density piece of `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md` area M ("Ego-network size/density... all E"), previously flagged as blocked on "no general induced-subgraph-extraction primitive exists at all." That assumption turned out not to hold for these two specific scalar summary measures: both are directly computable from the existing sparse `neighbors()`/`neighbors_in()`/`has_edge()` accessors (the same primitives `calculate_components()`/`calculate_kcore()`/`calculate_brokerage()` already use) without ever materializing a persistent subgraph network object - a genuine induced-subgraph-extraction primitive remains a real, separate gap for other ego-network measures that *would* need an actual reusable subgraph object (composition/diversity, alter-alter tie listing rather than just density), not closed by this unit. New `NWdef::calculate_egostats()` Mata method: an ego's alters are every node it has any tie with (union of in- and out-neighbors for a directed network - the standard membership question, deliberately distinct from `nwaltergen`'s own directional alter-aggregation convention, which keeps in/out separate for a different purpose); size is simply the alter count; density is the proportion of possible ties actually present *among the alters themselves*, ego excluded (the standard convention - how interconnected are ego's contacts with each other) - directed networks count ordered alter-alter pairs, undirected networks count unordered pairs, and an ego with fewer than 2 alters gets a missing (not spuriously 0 or 1) density, since there is no pair to assess. Verified with a hand-computable undirected network (one node with 3 alters, only one alter-alter tie among them, giving exactly 1/3; two nodes with 2 alters each, both giving exactly 1; two isolated-pair nodes with a single alter each, both correctly missing) and a directed network (one ego's alters found via the in/out union, with the expected asymmetric alter-alter density from only one of two ordered pairs being tied) - both match hand-derived expectations exactly. Also verified: an isolated node (zero alters) does not crash, reporting size 0 and density missing; `sizevar()`/`densvar()`/`replace`; `netlist` multi-network support. New `cscripts/test_nwego.do` plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both `nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_alphabetical.sthlp` indexes. This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 26: nwaltergen proportion() categorical aggregation

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwaltergen`/`nwgen` — `proportion(alter.srcvar==value)`/`!=value` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the `proportion()`-style categorical aggregation item flagged in `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s Stage 5 (Stata-native integration). Gives the proportion of ego's alters whose `srcvar` equals (or does not equal) a specific numeric category, without first having to hand-`generate` a 0/1 indicator variable - the genuinely new capability, since `mean(alter.x)` already covers "proportion with x==1" for an already-binary `x` (deliberately not offered as a bare, comparison-less synonym for that, to keep one unambiguous way to ask for it). Implemented entirely at the `.ado` level - no `unw_core.do`/Mata core changes at all: the comparison is evaluated in Stata/Mata to build a 0/1 indicator vector first, which is then handed to `calculate_alterstat()`'s existing, already-certified `mean` code path unchanged. Missing `srcvar` values are preserved as missing through the comparison (not silently read as "not in this category") so `calculate_alterstat()`'s own existing missing-dropping logic still applies correctly to the derived indicator. Two real bugs found and fixed while building this: (1) Mata's bare `==`/`!=` test *whole-matrix* identity (a single scalar), not an elementwise comparison - the elementwise operators are `:==`/`:!=` - a first attempt using the bare operator silently collapsed a per-alter comparison to one scalar, caught by a downstream conformability error rather than silently wrong output, but still a genuine, worth-remembering Mata gotcha for any future `.ado`-level comparison-building code. (2) A **pre-existing** stale-`_rc` bug in `nwaltergen.ado` itself, predating this unit: the internal "does the target variable already exist" check (`capture drop <newvarname>` on a variable that doesn't exist yet, an expected, harmless no-op) leaves `_rc=111` sitting unrefreshed all the way through an otherwise fully successful call, since every command after it (`mata:` blocks, `gen`, `label variable`) is one of the "does not refresh `_rc`" classes documented in `nwbrokerage.ado`'s own certified row (unit 23) - never caught before because the existing test file's every prior assertion either exercised a `replace()` call (where the variable already existing makes the `capture drop` succeed, masking the bug) or never checked `_rc` at all. Fixed identically to units 23/25 - one explicit, silent `capture confirm variable <newvarname>, exact` at the end; regression-tested directly for both the pre-existing `mean/sum/...` path and the new `proportion()` path. `nwgen.ado`'s own shortcut-dispatch regex extended to recognize `proportion` alongside the existing `mean|sum|min|max|sd|count`. Verified `proportion()` gives the exact same value as `mean()` on a hand-built equivalent 0/1 indicator (confirms it's a genuine convenience wrapper, not a different computation), plus the `!=` operator, missing-value handling, a non-numeric comparison value being rejected explicitly, and `nwgen`'s own dispatch. Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwaltergen.do` (not a new file) and regenerated its production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Regenerated `nwaltergen.sthlp` via `nw_helpwriter`. No packaging-manifest changes needed (not a new command). Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 27: nwqap.ado doc-header migration

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwqap.ado` migrated to the standard `/*** ... ***/` SMCL doc-header convention | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes a Pending item open since harmonisation unit 9: `nwqap.sthlp` was a separate, hand-maintained file (predating the header-driven convention this session established for every other touched command), so `nw_helpwriter` could never regenerate it and it had no "last certified" footer. Pure documentation-format migration - no functional code touched at all, confirmed by the fact that the entire diff between the old hand-maintained `.sthlp` and the newly `nw_helpwriter`-regenerated one is exactly four deliberate content fixes and nothing else (see below), verified line-by-line with `diff -b -B`. While porting the content, found that `nwqap.sthlp` had four instances of the Unicode replacement character (U+FFFD, "�") - genuinely irrecoverable corruption (not something introducible by any change made in this session; the original characters, almost certainly curly quotes and an em/en dash around "unit of analysis" and two Krackhardt article titles, were already lost before this session began, most likely during this repo's own previously-documented corrupted-git-history recovery event) - restored to plain, unambiguous ASCII (straight double quotes, a hyphen) rather than left as literal replacement-character glyphs in the shipped documentation or guessed back to a specific curly-quote Unicode codepoint that can't actually be verified correct. `nwqap.ado` itself required no changes beyond prepending the new header block - confirmed via `diff -b -B` that only the four corrupted-character fixes plus the newly-added "last certified" footer differ from the prior file, and via a full re-run of `cscripts/test_nwqap.do` in both dev and production mode (both pass identically to before - a pure textual prepend to the top of the file cannot and did not affect the `program nwqap` body one line below it). Confirmed the prepended header contains no double-backtick or other patterns that would trip `nw_helpwriter`'s own known line-copy fragility (see unit 16's certified row) before regenerating. No `unw_core.do` changes, so no library rebuild needed. Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 28: nwaltergen hop() multi-hop/lagged exposure

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwaltergen`/`nwgen` — `hop(k)` multi-hop/lagged exposure aggregation | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the last remaining item in `nwaltergen`'s own alter-aggregation scope (after unit 26's `proportion()`) - lagged/multi-hop exposure, the standard "does influence propagate beyond a node's immediate neighborhood" question in diffusion research. New `NWdef::calculate_alterstat_hop(srcvar, stat, hop)` Mata method: aggregates `srcvar` over the nodes at exactly `hop` (unweighted) steps away, using `calculate_distances(0, "brute")` (the "brute" algorithm always computes unweighted distances regardless of the `alpha` argument, so `0` is passed only because the method signature requires some value) rather than the direct `neighbors()` accessor `calculate_alterstat()` uses. Deliberately implemented as a self-contained sibling function with its own copy of the same mean/sum/min/max/sd/count aggregation logic, rather than refactoring the already-certified `calculate_alterstat()` to share it - a small amount of duplication traded for zero risk of regressing the well-tested 1-hop path. `nwaltergen.ado` gained a new `hop(int)` option (default 1, matching the pre-existing behavior exactly) that dispatches to the new method only when `hop()>1` is explicitly requested; the `hop()==1` case still calls the original, unmodified `calculate_alterstat()` directly rather than `calculate_alterstat_hop(...,1)` - confirmed the two give bit-identical results on a hand-built example, but kept the established code path as the default for the same zero-regression-risk reason. `hop()` composes with `proportion()` (verified together, not just each independently) and passes through `nwgen`'s own shortcut dispatch unchanged (its regex only inspects the leading `stat(alter....` text, so no change was needed there for the option to flow through). Verified with a 5-node undirected chain network (A-B-C-D-E, `srcvar` = chain position) - a chain has exactly one node at each distance from any given node, so `hop(1)`/`hop(2)`/`hop(3)` are all exactly, not approximately, checkable, including a node with no alter at the requested distance correctly reporting missing (for `mean`) rather than a spurious 0; a directed A->B->C chain confirms `hop(2)` follows tie direction (matching `alter`'s own established one-hop convention) and that a terminal node's `sum`/`count` correctly reports 0 (not missing) when it has no out-ties. Extended the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwaltergen.do` (not a new file) and regenerated its production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Regenerated `nwaltergen.sthlp` via `nw_helpwriter`. This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 29: nwclique (maximal clique enumeration) - new command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwclique` — new command implementing maximal clique enumeration via Bron-Kerbosch (1973) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the "Cliques/k-plexes (cohesive subgroups beyond k-core)" Pending item (k-plexes and the other named cohesive-subgroup concepts - n-cliques, n-clans, k-components, lambda sets, factions, Moody-White blocks - remain open, see the Pending table's updated row). New `NWdef::calculate_cliques()` Mata method delegating to a standalone `BronKerbosch()` function (the classic, unpivoted recursive algorithm - R/P/X represented as 0/1 indicator row vectors, not index lists, for simple `:&` set-intersection; deliberately not the pivoted optimization, trading its asymptotic improvement for an implementation that is easier to verify correct by hand, appropriate given this package's own moderate target network scale) - the same "instance method delegates to a standalone function" pattern established by `calculate_modularity()`/`Louvain()` and reused throughout this session's other new algorithms. Cliques genuinely overlap (a node can belong to several maximal cliques at once, unlike the single-membership partitions `nwcomponents`/`nwcommunity`/`nwconcor` generate), so `nwclique` generates a single well-defined per-node summary (the size of the *largest* maximal clique each node belongs to - its "clique number") while returning the complete cliques-by-nodes 0/1 membership matrix in `r(clique_matrix)` for anyone who needs the full overlapping structure. `minsize()` (default 3, matching the common SNA convention that a "clique" worth reporting has at least three members - a bare tied pair and an isolated singleton are both technically maximal cliques by the formal definition, but rarely what someone means by the term) filters before both the generated variable and the returned matrix are built; a node in no clique meeting `minsize()` gets a missing value, not a spurious 0. Directed networks are symmetrized automatically (a clique's definition - every pair mutually tied - has no directed generalization, the same reasoning `nwcoreperiphery` already applies); weight is not used (only tie presence/absence matters). While building this, found that **a bare `mata:`/`end` multi-line block does not nest correctly inside a running `.ado` program's own execution flow** the way it does at file level after a program's own `end` - it silently truncates the *program's* own boundary detection instead of behaving as a scoped Mata call, surfacing as a confusing "too few variables specified... while loading nwclique.ado" with no indication of the actual mismatched-nesting cause. Confirmed by checking every other command in this package: none use a genuine multi-line `mata:`/`end` block *inside* a running program body, only single-line `mata: expr` calls, or file-level trailing blocks *outside* any `program` - a convention this session had been implicitly following without previously needing to understand why. Fixed by moving the multi-statement filtering/summarizing logic into a small `unw_core.do` wrapper method (`calculate_cliques_filtered()`) instead, so `nwclique.ado` itself only ever needs single-line `mata:` calls, matching that established (if previously unexplained) convention. Verified with a textbook example (two triangles sharing an edge, plus an isolated node) whose maximal-clique structure is unambiguous and fully hand-derivable - exactly two cliques of size 3, matched by exact row content in `r(clique_matrix)`, not merely by count - and with K4 (the complete graph on 4 nodes), which has exactly one maximal clique containing everyone. Also verified: `minsize()` filtering (including admitting the trivial isolate at `minsize(1)`), directed-network auto-symmetrization, `generate()`/`replace`, `netlist` multi-network support, and invalid `minsize()` input. New `cscripts/test_nwclique.do` plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both `nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_alphabetical.sthlp` indexes. While updating `docs/FEATURE_AUDIT.md`'s area K for this unit, found and fixed two further stale claims predating this session's own earlier `nwkcore` work (its executive summary and area K both still said k-cores were "completely absent" / "confirmed E," despite `nwkcore` already being done) - corrected alongside this unit's own update rather than left inconsistent. This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Full regression sweep (both modes) matched the established 8-failure baseline exactly. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 30: nwreplacemat's size-changing path, nwdropnodes, nwkeepnodes, nwcompressobs — long-blocked chain fully unblocked

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwreplacemat`'s size-*changing* path, `nwdropnodes`, `nwkeepnodes`, `nwcompressobs` — five stacked bugs across four files, found and fixed together | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | — (no `.sthlp` content changes needed, all internal fixes) | Resolves the Pending item open since units 9-13 ("Remaining `_nwsyntax`/`_nwsyntax_other` callers... confirmed broken, not yet fixed"). (1) `nwdropnodes`/`nwkeepnodes` both called the deprecated `_nwsyntax` wrapper, which only re-exports 4 locals (`netobj`/`id`/`netname`/`networks`) — neither file's own use of `` `nodes' `` (the node *count*, needed for the drop/keep-complement loop) was ever actually populated; switched both to `nw_syntax`, which does export it (safe in both files because the option-supplied `nodes` value — the node list to drop/keep — is already fully consumed into a differently-named local, `nodelist`, before this call runs). (2) `nwdropnodes` separately read the legacy pre-2016 `` $nw_`id' ``/`` $nwlabs_`id' `` globals for the current network's variable names/labels — confirmed empty for any network built via the modern `netobj`/`NWdef` architecture; replaced with `nwname`'s own `r(vars)`/`r(labs)`. (3) `nwreplacemat`'s `labs()` option had **never actually worked at all**: `nw_syntax` itself exports a local called `labs` (the *current*, pre-resize network's own labels, via a bare `c_local labs "..."` with no `other()` prefix) — calling it immediately after `syntax` parsed the caller's own `labs()` option silently clobbered that value before the file ever used it. This is the same "`nw_syntax`'s own `c_local` exports can shadow a caller's identically-named option local" bug class already found once this session for `nodes` (fix #1 above) — `labs` is a second, previously-unrecognized instance of the identical underlying pattern. Fixed by capturing the caller's `labs()` value into a differently-named local (`newmatlabs`) before calling `nw_syntax`. Found via a chain of increasingly targeted debug instrumentation after the size-changing path's end-to-end node *count*/structure checked out correct but labels didn't — several false leads (a stale `netobj` pointer, a Mata variable-name collision, a dataset-state difference) were ruled out first. (4) `nwdropnodes`'s own `newlabs` local (handed off to `nwreplacemat`'s now-working `labs()` option) was built *space*-separated, matching its internal `` : word N of `labs' `` extraction convention — but `nwreplacemat` passes `labs()` straight through to `nwrandom`, which expects *comma*-separated input (matching `nwname`'s own `r(labs)` format) and silently misreads a space-separated list as a single label for the first node (auto-generating default labels for the rest) rather than erroring. Fixed by building `newlabs` comma-separated specifically for that hand-off, while the internal `labs` local (used only for word-extraction) stays space-separated. (5) `nwcompressobs` — called at the end of `nwreplacemat`'s size-changing path on every single invocation — left `_rc` stale at 107 on return from literally any dataset containing at least one numeric variable (which every real network's own `_nwinclude` column already guarantees): its own `capture encode` probe against an already-numeric variable legitimately fails with r(107) every time (a fully expected, already-captured outcome, not a real error), but the file's own trailing `qui drop if (...)` never refreshes `_rc` even on success (quietly-prefixed commands don't update `_rc` even when they succeed — see `nwbrokerage`'s own certified row, unit 23, for the fuller explanation of this Stata behavior, and `nwego`/`nwaltergen` for two more independent instances of the same bug class found earlier this session). This is a genuinely package-wide latent bug, not specific to any one caller — confirmed via a minimal reproduction that `_rc==107` survives `nwcompressobs` on any dataset with a numeric variable. Fixed with an explicit, silent `capture local __nw_rcreset = 1` at the end of the program. All five fixes were hand-verified via direct Mata/Stata probes (drop/keep by index, drop/keep by label, multi-node drop, directed networks, a direct `_rc`-staleness reproduction) before any test file was written. New `cscripts/test_nwdropnodes.do`, `cscripts/test_nwkeepnodes.do`, and `cscripts/test_nwcompressobs.do` (all zero coverage before — `nwdropnodes` and `nwkeepnodes` had literally never had a passing invocation prior to this unit); `cscripts/test_nwreplacemat.do` extended (not new) with size-changing-path coverage. Certified against a 4-node undirected chain (drop/keep by index and by label), a 5-node cycle (multi-node drop/keep, verifying which ties survive vs. which only ran through a now-removed node), a directed chain (post-op `directed` flag and isolate-detection), a downstream-command check (`nwsummarize`/`nwdegree` correctly respecting the shrunk network, not the shared dataset's own row count), and a dedicated `nwcompressobs` regression test for the `_rc`-staleness bug itself plus an all-missing-row-dropping functional check (verified via a direct `encode`-on-empty-string probe that an empty string counts as missing, not its own category). This unit touches only `.ado` files, no `unw_core.do`/Mata core changes — no `.mlib` rebuild needed. Certified per the newly-adopted tiered strategy (see "Regression-sweep tier" above) at **L2**: the four directly affected tests plus every other caller of the fixed commands (`nwkeep`, `nwdrop`, `nwconcor`, `nwrecode`, `nwreplace`) and the shared infrastructure this chain most directly interacts with (`nwrandom`, `nwset`, `nwsync`, `nworder`) — all pass cleanly in both dev and production mode, no unexpected failures, so no escalation to L3/L4 was warranted. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 31: nwdyads/nwnoderename test coverage; real dyad-census bug found and fixed

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwdyads` — found and fixed a genuine `calculate_dyadcensus()` null-dyad-count bug; `nwnoderename` — new test coverage, confirmed correct as-is | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | — (no `.sthlp` content changes needed) | Closes the Pending item flagging that both commands had a SMCL doc header but literally no `cscripts/test_*.do` file at all (found while scoping unit 16, not fixed by it). `nwnoderename` was found empirically correct as-is via direct probing (basic rename, duplicate-name/nonexistent-node soft-fail paths, directed/weighted networks, a new label containing a space) — it already used the modern `nw_syntax`, so it was never part of the `_nwsyntax` bug class units 9-13/30 fixed elsewhere. (A stray, wrongly-extensioned `cscripts/test_nwnoderename.ado` draft existed on disk, untracked, never actually run as part of the suite — superseded by the real `.do` file written this unit.) Writing real hand-computable known-answer tests for `nwdyads` (per the certification standard's own "Functional" requirement) surfaced a genuine, previously-undiscovered bug in `unw_core.do`'s `NWdef::calculate_dyadcensus()`: the one-mode null-dyad count used `n*(n-1) - asym - mutual`, but `n*(n-1)` counts *ordered* pairs, while a dyad is an *unordered* pair — the correct total is `n*(n-1)/2`. The missing `/2` silently doubled every reported null-dyad count, landing entirely in `r(_001)`/the displayed "Null" column, since `mutual`/`asym` are each computed independently via their own already-correctly-halved formulas. `r(reciprocity)` (`mutual/(mutual+asym)`) doesn't depend on `null` at all, so the bug was completely invisible in that stored result. Confirmed via 3 hand-computable one-mode cases (a fully-connected undirected triangle reported null=3 instead of 0; a 3-node mixed directed network reported null=4 instead of 1; an empty 3-node network reported null=6 instead of 3 — in every case exactly double). Fixed by dividing by 2. The two-mode branch (`get_nodes_mode1() * get_nodes_mode2()`, no `/2` needed since mode1-mode2 pairs are cross-set, not same-set unordered pairs) was already correct — confirmed unaffected via its own hand-computable bipartite case (3 actors, 2 events, 4 of 6 possible pairs connected). Only caller of `calculate_dyadcensus()` in the whole package is `nwdyads.ado` itself (confirmed via grep), so this fix's blast radius is narrow despite touching the shared Mata core — rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. New `cscripts/test_nwdyads.do` and `cscripts/test_nwnoderename.do` (both zero coverage before), both pass in dev and production mode. Certified per the tiered strategy at **L2**: the two directly affected tests plus the adjacent dyad/triad-census family (`nwtriads`) and nearby shared infrastructure (`nwcorrelate`, `nwset`, `nwsummarize`) — all pass cleanly, no unexpected failures, so no escalation to L3/L4 was warranted. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 32: nwtriads `_210` bug root-caused and fixed — a simple swapped-index bug, not a Mata quirk

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwtriads` — root-caused and fixed the `_210`-on-undirected-input bug flagged (not fixed) in unit 3 | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the Pending item speculating a "Mata `[symmetric]`-matrix-type edge case inside `calculate_triadcensus()`'s own C-matrix construction." That suspicion was wrong. Root-caused by directly probing `calculate_triadcensus()` in isolation (a literal Mata matrix, and separately a numerically-identical plain-constructed matrix, built outside the `NWdef` class entirely) on a hand-computable 5-node cycle (girth 5, zero triangles): both isolated reconstructions of the function's own algorithm gave the mathematically correct answer (`_201`=5, `_210`=0), and directly dumping the real network's own `get_matrix_unvalued()` pointer confirmed it was genuinely, perfectly symmetric (`A - A' == 0` exactly) — ruling out both the originally-suspected Mata typing issue and any asymmetry in the underlying stored matrix. That left only one remaining place the bug could live: the return-vector index mapping. Comparing `calculate_triadcensus()`'s own return statement order (`unw_core.do`: `..., x_120C, x_210, x_201, x_300` — index 14 is `x_210`, index 15 is `x_201`) against `nwtriads.ado`'s r()-extraction (`` triadcensus[14] `` assigned to `r(_201)`, `` triadcensus[15] `` assigned to `r(_210)`) found the two simply swapped — a plain indexing bug in `nwtriads.ado` itself, nothing wrong with `calculate_triadcensus()`'s own Mata computation at all. Fixed by swapping the two index reads back. Updated `nwtriads.ado`'s own inline comment, its "Supported network types" doc-header section (now says all 12 asymmetric-dyad-dependent categories reliably return 0 for undirected input, not 11-of-12-plus-one-exception), and regenerated `nwtriads.sthlp` via `nw_helpwriter`. Updated the pre-existing `cscripts/test_nwtriads.do`'s own regression guard, which (per its own comment, written at the time the bug was first found) had deliberately locked in the known-incorrect value specifically so that fixing the bug would make the assertion fail and prompt an update — exactly what happened. Replaced with a correct assertion (`_210==0`) plus the 3 previously-unchecked sibling categories (`_102`==1/`_201`==2/`_300`==1) on the original 4-node triangle-plus-pendant test network, and added a second, independent hand-computable case (the same 5-node cycle used during root-causing: `_102`==5/`_201`==5/`_210`==0/`_300`==0, derived by hand-listing all 10 possible triples) — deliberately chosen because a 5-cycle's `_210`/`_201` values are neither both 0 nor coincidentally equal, making it the clearest possible regression guard against this exact swap ever silently recurring. No `unw_core.do` change needed (the Mata core was correct all along) — pure `.ado` fix. Certified at **L2**: the updated test plus the adjacent dyad-census test (`nwdyads`) and nearby shared infrastructure (`nwclustering`, `nwset`, `nwbalance`) — all pass cleanly in both dev and production mode. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 33: nwplot native SVG/vector export; mdsclassical layout fully unblocked

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwplot` — new `export()`/`replace`/`exportopt()` options (native SVG/PDF/PNG/EPS/... export); fixed the `_rc`-staleness bug; fixed 4 real crashes in the `mdsclassical` default-layout/`lgc` code paths; documented the previously-undocumented `frucht` layout; added a `.dlg` Export tab | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A focused audit/modernisation of the `nwplot` subsystem (full scope: understand the existing architecture, identify historical vector/interactive-graphics infrastructure, test native Stata SVG export, add first-class export support, harmonise docs/dialog, certify). **Architecture found**: `nwplot` already builds an ordinary native `twoway` graph (`scatter` for nodes, `pcspike`/`pcarrow` for edges) - no custom renderer, nothing to supersede there. It already had a working, if undocumented, coordinate-reuse pair (`generate()` exports node x/y, `nodexy()` re-plots at those exact positions) that substantially solves "identical layouts across networks/waves" (Part IX/X) without needing a new `nwlayout` command - documented as such rather than built fresh, per the task's own explicit instruction not to add one unless the existing architecture can't already do the job. Native Stata 18 SVG export was verified directly (not assumed) to already produce genuinely clean, fully-editable vector output for every existing `nwplot` element - real `<text>` nodes with an actual font-family, `<line>` edges with correct per-tie stroke-width, and arrowheads as proper vector `<path>` polygons with clip-paths - confirmed via direct SVG-source inspection across undirected/directed/weighted/two-mode/large-network plots before writing a single line of new code, exactly as the task's Part III required. Given this, `export()` is a thin, additive wrapper around Stata's own `graph export` (format inferred from the filename extension, matching Stata's own convention - no separate `format()` option), called on the exact same graph object `nwplot` already draws - the graph remains a completely normal Stata graph afterward (Graph Editor, `graph save`, a second `graph export` in another format all still work), satisfying Part V/VII directly. `exportopt()` is a narrow, explicit passthrough for the small set of `graph export` options genuinely worth reaching without leaving `nwplot` (chiefly `width()`/`height()` for raster resolution) rather than duplicating `graph export`'s full option surface. New `r(export)` stored result (nwplot was `rclass` but posted nothing before this). **Historical-graphics archaeology** (full findings in this unit's own audit, condensed here): `nwplotjs.ado` (sigma.js/`linkurious/`-based) is a genuinely interactive renderer (pan/zoom/hover/drag, two bespoke edge-curvature/parallel-edge renderers written for this package) that native SVG export does **not** supersede - kept fully in place, untouched. `d3js/` and `sigmajs/` (two separate, earlier/parallel vendored JS-library dumps at the repo root, confirmed via repo-wide grep to be referenced by zero `.ado`/`.do` files) were archived to a new `old/js/` convention (parallel to the existing `old/ado/`, documented in `LEGACY_FILES.md`); `linkurious/` itself was **not** archived, since `nwplotjs.ado` actively depends on it. **Real bugs found and fixed along the way** (surfaced only because this unit finally exercised code paths - large/sparse networks, `layout(,lgc)` - that had apparently never been exercised successfully before): (1) `nwplot`'s own end-of-program cleanup left `_rc` stale (the same bug class fixed repeatedly elsewhere this session - `nwbrokerage`/`nwego`/`nwaltergen`/`nwcompressobs`) - a pre-existing `mata: st_numscalar("_rc", 0)` line was an ineffective attempt at this exact fix (creates an ordinary Stata scalar literally named `"_rc"`, with zero effect on the interpreter's real `_rc` state); replaced with a working `capture confirm number 1`. This was significant enough that the pre-existing (untracked, never-committed) draft `cscripts/test_nwplot.do` had a standing `assert _rc == 0 | _rc == 3000` tolerance for it. (2)-(4) Three call sites inside `nwplot.ado` used `nwgen`/`nwgenerate`'s `isolates(`/`components(`/`lgc(` shortcuts, all three of which are listed in `nwgenerate.ado`'s own recognized-keyword vocabulary but have **no actual dispatch branch implementing them** (a different, larger, previously-undiscovered gap from the already-tracked "8 dead network-generating branches" Pending item - this is a separate family of *variable*-producing shortcuts, silently no-op'ing instead of erroring). This meant the DEFAULT layout for any network with more than 50 nodes (`mdsclassical`) crashed unconditionally for every caller - not a rare edge case - confirmed via a minimal `nwrandom 60, prob(.15)` + `nwplot` repro and root-caused via `set trace on`. Fixed by routing all three call sites through already well-tested, already-shipped commands instead (`nwdegree`'s plain default degree computation for isolates, `nwcomponents`/`nwcomponents ..., lgc` for the component logic) rather than fixing the general `nwgen` shortcut-dispatch gap (out of scope for this unit, tracked separately below). While fixing the isolates call, found a **fifth**, independent, genuine bug in `nwdegree.ado` itself (not fixed - see Pending): its own `isolates` option, given without an explicit `generate()`, reserves only one output-variable-name slot but its directed-network branch unconditionally needs two, crashing on any directed network (which `nwrandom` defaults to) - avoided in `nwplot` by using `nwdegree`'s plain default behaviour instead of its `isolates` option. A **sixth**, narrower, not-fully-root-caused issue was also found and left open (Pending): calling bare `nwplot` and then `nwplot, layout(,lgc)` on the *same* live network (no `nwclear` between) can fail with r(99) on a >50-node network - confirmed via 6+ single-shot seeds that a *fresh* `layout(,lgc)` call never fails, only the specific repeated-call sequence; `docs/CERTIFICATION.md`'s own Pending table below has the detail. Also documented the previously-undocumented `layout(frucht)` (a fully-functional Fruchterman-Reingold implementation that existed in code and in the option validator but was never mentioned in the doc header) in both the `.sthlp` and `.dlg` (added to the `layoutstyle` combobox, reusing the `mds` sub-options script since both take `iterations()`), and fixed a doc example (`nwgen large = lgc(glasgow1)`) that had been silently broken by the same `lgc(` shortcut gap since it was written - replaced with the now-documented, working `nwcomponents ..., lgc generate()` idiom. Added `.sthlp` "Stored results" and "Supported network types" sections (neither existed before, despite being standard elsewhere in the package) documenting weighted/directed/undirected/two-mode/self-loop support explicitly - two-mode plots correctly draw today (no bipartite-specific code needed, since a two-mode network's ties are still just an adjacency structure) but modes aren't auto-distinguished visually (documented workaround: `color()`/`symbol()` on a mode variable, demonstrated in the new test file's own Plot E); self-loops are not rendered at all (documented as a known limitation, not fixed - out of scope, tracked in the roadmap). Added a `.dlg` "Export" tab (`FILE` control with `save`/`filter()`, a `replace` checkbox, wired into the generated command via `optionarg`, matching the exact convention already used by `nwcorrelate.dlg`'s own `Save As` field) - full interactive GUI click-path could not be exercised in this headless environment, but the command strings the dialog assembles were verified directly against `nwplot` itself (already extensively tested, see below). **Test coverage**: rewrote/extended the pre-existing (untracked, never-committed) draft `cscripts/test_nwplot.do` - replaced its `nwwebuse florentine` dependency on a dead external URL with the local `nwuse florentine` equivalent (same convention as `test_nwdegree.do`), tightened all assertions from the stale-`_rc`-tolerant `_rc == 0 | _rc == 3000` to a strict `_rc == 0` now that the underlying bug is fixed, and added real SVG-export coverage per the task's own 13-point checklist: file creation/existence/non-emptiness/structural validity (a lightweight `<svg` tag scan, deliberately not a pixel-level regression test), `replace` behaviour (a second export without `replace` correctly raises the same r(602) a manual `graph export` would - `nwplot` doesn't swallow or reinterpret it), filenames containing spaces, relative and absolute paths, and weighted/directed/two-mode exports. Also added the requested 7-plot visual-certification set (A-G: small undirected, directed-with-arrows, weighted, categorical-groups, two-mode-with-mode-coloring, labelled, and a 60-node sparse network specifically chosen to regression-guard the `mdsclassical` fix) as a stable reference set for future visual-quality changes. Found and worked around one genuine, general Stata gotcha in the process (not `nwplot`-specific): a filename built by appending an extension onto a `tempfile`-generated name (e.g. `` `tempfile'.svg ``) breaks `graph export`'s own suffix parser, which reads everything after the *first* dot in the basename as the extension - confirmed via an isolated `sysuse auto`/`scatter` repro; worked around by building filenames directly under `c(tmpdir)'` instead. 46 assertions, all clean, both dev and production mode (no `unw_core.do`/Mata core changes at all - pure `.ado`/`.dlg` fix, so no `.mlib` rebuild needed). Certified per the tiered strategy at **L2**: `nwplot`'s own test plus its now-more-heavily-used dependencies `nwcomponents`/`nwdegree`, plus the sibling plotting command `nwplotmatrix` (pre-existing baseline dead-URL failure only, unrelated) - all clean, no unexpected failures. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 34: nwkplex (maximal k-plex enumeration) - new command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwkplex` — new command implementing maximal k-plex enumeration, generalizing `nwclique`'s own Bron-Kerbosch (1973) algorithm to the Seidman & Foster (1978) k-plex membership rule | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the k-plex half of the "Cliques/k-plexes (cohesive subgroups beyond k-core)" Pending item (cliques themselves were done in unit 29, `nwclique`; n-cliques/n-clans/k-components/lambda sets/factions/Moody-White blocks remain open). New `NWdef::calculate_kplex()`/`calculate_kplex_filtered()` Mata methods delegate to a standalone `KPlex()` function - the exact same R/P/X backtracking scheme `nwclique`'s own `BronKerbosch()` uses (0/1 indicator row vectors, not index lists, matching the established pattern), generalized only in *how* a candidate's addability is checked: a clique candidate just needs a cheap neighbor-row intersection (adjacent to every existing member), but a k-plex candidate's validity genuinely depends on the exact current membership (each member may miss up to `k`-1 ties to the *other* members, so adding a new node can push an existing member over its own budget) - checked via a new `is_valid_kplex()` helper that examines the whole induced adjacency submatrix of the candidate set (`min(rowsum(S)) >= rows(S) - k`) rather than a simple set intersection. Reusing `nwclique`'s own maximality-tracking scheme for this more general rule is justified by a proved (not assumed) hereditary property, documented directly in `KPlex()`'s own header comment: any subset of a valid k-plex is itself a valid k-plex under the same `k` (a short algebraic proof from the definition), which is exactly the property the R/P/X non-redundant-enumeration argument relies on. `k`=1 is exactly a clique - `nwkplex` requires `k`>=2 and points to `nwclique` for that case, which already has a cheaper, purpose-built algorithm for it. A node can belong to several overlapping maximal k-plexes at once (same as cliques), so `nwkplex` follows `nwclique`'s own established output shape exactly: a single per-node "largest maximal k-plex membership size" summary variable (default `_kplexnum`) plus the complete overlapping structure in `r(kplex_matrix)` (a k-plexes-by-nodes 0/1 membership matrix) and `r(kplexes)` (count). `minsize()` defaults to `k`+1, not a fixed constant like `nwclique`'s own `minsize(3)` - found directly while hand-verifying the algorithm (not merely reasoned about in the abstract) that *any* node set of size `k` or smaller is trivially, uninformatively a valid k-plex regardless of whether its members are tied at all (confirmed via a two-disjoint-triangles test case: an unfiltered `k`=2 enumeration returns 11 maximal 2-plexes, not the intuitively-expected 2, because every one of the 9 cross-triangle node *pairs* also technically qualifies) - `minsize()`=`k`+1 is the smallest size at which the constraint can actually rule anything out, so it's the natural default rather than an arbitrary choice. Directed networks are symmetrized automatically (same reasoning `nwclique`/`nwcoreperiphery` already apply - a k-plex's own definition has no directed generalization); weight is not used. Verified against three hand-derivable cases, each checked by exact membership (not just count): two disjoint triangles at `k`=2 (exactly the two triangles, size 3 each - the trivial cross-triangle pairs correctly excluded by the default `minsize`), a 5-node path graph at `k`=2 (exactly three overlapping maximal 2-plexes - {A,B,C}/{B,C,D}/{C,D,E} - each hand-derived from the missing-tie-count rule directly), and a 4-node "complete graph minus one edge" (the whole 4-node set is itself one single maximal 2-plex, hand-derived from each node's own missing-tie count). Also verified: `k`=1 is rejected with a clear error pointing to `nwclique`; `k`=0/negative and invalid `minsize()` are rejected; `generate()`/`replace`; `netlist` multi-network support with correctly-suffixed output variables; directed-network auto-symmetrization; and a basic k-monotonicity sanity check (raising `k` from 2 to 3 on the same path graph produces a strictly larger maximal k-plex, as it must - a looser constraint can never shrink the maximal structures it admits). New `cscripts/test_nwkplex.do` (33 assertions) plus a production-mode copy - both pass cleanly. Added to all 4 packaging manifests and both `nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_alphabetical.sthlp` indexes; cross-referenced from `nwclique.ado`'s own "See also" (regenerated `nwclique.sthlp` to match - only the `See also` line changed). This unit touches `unw_core.do` (shared core) - rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` via `lib/build.do` and re-verified in production mode. Certified per the tiered strategy at **L2**: the new test plus `nwclique`'s own test (the closest sibling, sharing the same Mata algorithm family) - both clean, both modes, no unexpected failures, so no escalation to L3/L4 was warranted (the new Mata functions are an isolated addition - nothing pre-existing calls `KPlex()`/`is_valid_kplex()`/`calculate_kplex()`/`calculate_kplex_filtered()`). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 35: nwhierarchy packaged as a full role/position-analysis workflow

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwhierarchy` — new `groups()`/`equivgen()`/`replace` options completing the `nwdissimilar`/`nwsimilar` → `nwhierarchy` → equivalence-class-variable role/position-analysis workflow; new embedded doc header (previously undocumented in-file) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Closes the "package the chain as a documented role/position analysis workflow; add an equivalence-class-to-Stata-variable output step (mirroring `nwcomponents`' pattern)" item left open after harmonisation unit 20 fully unblocked the `nwsimilar`/`nwdissimilar`/`nwhierarchy` chain. `nwhierarchy` already delegated the actual clustering to Stata's own `clustermat` (part of the official `cluster` suite) - confirmed directly that Stata's native `cluster generate ..., groups(#)` postestimation command already does exactly the "cut a dendrogram into N equivalence classes as a variable" step this item asked for, so no new clustering algorithm was needed, only a convenience wrapper around a capability that already existed but required the caller to already know `cluster`'s own postestimation syntax *and* clustermat's own auto-generated cluster-object name (`_clus_1`, `_clus_2`, ... - never itself returned in `r()`, so it could not otherwise be recovered programmatically to run `cluster generate` against it). The new `groups(#)` option runs the clustering step with a fixed, deterministic internal cluster name (dropped and recreated on every call, matching `nwcomponents`'/`nwclique`'s own `generate()`/`replace` convention) and then calls `cluster generate` internally, producing a single per-node role/position-equivalence variable (default `_role`, `equivgen()` to rename, `replace` to overwrite) - directly analogous to `nwcomponents`' own `_component` output, except partitioned by structural role rather than connectivity. `r(groups)`/`r(rolevar)` report what was generated; without `groups()`, behaviour is completely unchanged from before this unit (confirmed via a dedicated regression case - no `_role` variable, `r(groups)`/`r(rolevar)` unset). Also fixed the deprecated `_nwsyntax` wrapper (same bug class as elsewhere this session, though empirically confirmed *not* actively broken here - fixed for consistency and to future-proof the new code added in this same unit) and, for the first time, gave `nwhierarchy.ado` a proper embedded `/*** ... ***/` doc header (it previously had none at all - `nwhierarchy.sthlp` was a separately hand-maintained file, the only command left in that older pre-doc-header pattern among the three in this chain) documenting the full three-stage workflow, `Stored results`, and worked examples. While regenerating `nwhierarchy.sthlp` via `nw_helpwriter`, hit the package's own known line-copy-loop fragility (see the dedicated Pending row) **twice**, both times self-inflicted and both times fixed at the source rather than worked around: two of this unit's own new inline comments used markdown-style matched-backtick pairs (`` `_nwsyntax` ``, `` `cluster generate ..., groups(#)` ``) instead of either plain text or Stata's own single-backtick-plus-apostrophe macro-quote convention - Stata's macro processor reads a lone unmatched backtick as an opened local-macro reference and keeps consuming subsequent lines looking for its closing apostrophe, eventually erroring "too few quotes" once none arrives. Reworded both comments to avoid the pattern entirely; the regeneration then completed cleanly with the certification stamp added automatically. Also cross-referenced `nwhierarchy` from `nwdissimilar.sthlp`'s and `nwsimilar.sthlp`'s own "See also" sections (neither previously mentioned it, a real discoverability gap for the workflow story) - left their own architecture (separately-maintained `.sthlp`, no embedded doc header) untouched, out of this unit's scope. Verified end to end on a 4-cycle network (A-B-C-D-A) whose structural-equivalence pattern is hand-derivable directly from each node's own tie profile: A and C are structurally identical (both tie only to B and D), as are B and D, giving a mathematically forced, unambiguous 2-class split - `groups(2)` was checked against exactly that split, not just a plausible-looking count.
