Verified via isolated Mata probes before any `.ado` wiring: the two-triangles-plus-bridge network correctly splits across 5 independent random runs after the fix (vs. 0/1 before it); a fully disconnected 3-pairs network correctly gives 3 singleton-pair communities; an isolate node mixed with a triangle correctly gets its own singleton community (no ties to vote on). **Certified with an honest accounting of the algorithm's own real, inherent limitation**, confirmed directly rather than glossed over: scanning `seed(1)` through `seed(30)` on the bridge network found roughly a quarter converge to one collapsed community rather than the correct two triangles - an expected property of label propagation itself (no modularity-monotonic guarantee, unlike Louvain), not a bug in this implementation. `cscripts/test_nwcommunity.do`'s new coverage uses `seed(1)` (confirmed by that same scan to converge correctly) to certify that a correct split CAN be found and is exactly reproduced given the same seed - explicitly not a claim that the algorithm always succeeds, with that limitation stated directly in both the test's own comment and `nwcommunity.ado`'s new `{marker algorithm}` doc section. Also certified: `r(modularity)` is still cross-checked via the same `calculate_modularity()` Louvain already uses (never trusting an internal running score, since label propagation has none), an invalid `algorithm()` value is rejected via `_opts_oneof`, and directed networks still require `symmetrize` regardless of algorithm. Both modes (dev + production) re-verified against the rebuilt `lnwcommands.mlib`. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this option's own new tests, both algorithms' own dedicated coverage). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 65: `nwspectral` - graph Laplacian spectral analysis, Stage 6's second item

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `nwspectral` command + `NWdef::calculate_laplacian()` Mata method | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Built exactly as `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own Stage 6 text anticipated: Laplacian construction (`L = D - W`, a small, direct addition alongside the package's other `calculate_*` methods) plus the `symeigensystem()` Mata built-in call pattern `nwevcent.ado` already established and proved reliable (eigenvector centrality's own largest-eigenvalue use there; this command instead wants the full sorted spectrum, ascending, and the second-smallest eigenvalue/eigenvector specifically). Reports three classical, exact spectral-graph-theory results, not approximations: `r(components)` (the MULTIPLICITY of Laplacian eigenvalue 0, cross-checked directly against `nwcomponents`' own independent reachability-based count - both give 2 on a two-disjoint-triangles test network), `r(algebraic_connectivity)` (the Fiedler value - the second-smallest eigenvalue, 0 exactly for any disconnected network), and a two-way spectral bipartition (`bipartition` option, from the Fiedler eigenvector's sign) stored per node via `generate()` (default `_fiedler`, with a `sign`-suffixed companion variable for the bipartition itself). Certified against three networks with independently-derivable closed-form spectra, each checked BEFORE writing the `.ado` file via an isolated Mata probe against `calculate_laplacian()` directly: a 4-node path graph (`P_n`'s known spectrum `2-2cos(k*pi/n)`, `k=0..n-1`, giving `{0, 2-sqrt(2), 2, 2+sqrt(2)}` for `n=4`) - whose Fiedler-vector sign also happens to give the intuitively obviously-correct bisection (split the path exactly in the middle); a fully disconnected two-triangle network (component count and algebraic connectivity both exactly verified); and K3 (complete graph on 3 nodes, whose Laplacian spectrum is `{0, n, n, ..., n}` in closed form - algebraic connectivity exactly `n=3`), also used to confirm directed-network auto-symmetrization (a directed 3-cycle symmetrizes to the identical K3). `measure(binary|valued)` checked to give genuinely different results on an unequal-weight network (not merely accepted as an option with no effect), with `measure(binary)`'s own dichotomized result independently cross-checked against the unweighted path graph's already-verified value. **A real bug found and fixed before any test passed**: the initial `replace` handling only guarded the pre-existence CHECK, but never actually dropped the existing variable(s) before regenerating them, so `generate(x) replace` on an already-existing `x` crashed with Stata's own native "variable already defined" (r(110)) instead of succeeding - fixed by adding the `capture drop` immediately before each `gen`, matching every other network-command's own established convention (`nwcommunity`/`nwcohesion`/`nwkcomponents` all already do this correctly; this was a genuinely new file, not a copy-paste of that exact pattern, so the omission was novel). Deliberately does not accept a full `netlist` (unlike most commands in this package) - a full per-node eigenvector does not stack cleanly across multiple networks into one shared column the way a scalar or simple per-node id does, so `nw_syntax`'s own `max(1)` single-network restriction was used directly rather than building netlist-iteration machinery for an output shape that does not actually fit it. Both modes (dev + production) verified against the rebuilt `lnwcommands.mlib`. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this command's own new tests; no other command yet depends on `calculate_laplacian()`). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 66: `nwturnover` - tie turnover/stability between two waves of the same network, Stage 7's one tractable item pulled forward

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| New `nwturnover` command | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Stage 7 is explicitly scoped "only after Stage 0-6 mature" in the roadmap's own text, and Stage 6 is not yet fully mature (motifs/flow-cuts-matching/random walks/large-network layouts/Leiden all remain open) - but this specific item was separately, explicitly flagged in that same Stage 7 note as needing no unfinished infrastructure ("a stability/turnover command is a smaller lift than the confirmed E status suggests once those pieces are assembled deliberately"), so it was pulled forward on its own merits rather than waiting on unrelated Stage 6 work. Built entirely on existing, already-certified infrastructure - `get_matrix_mod()` for both networks' adjacency matrices - with no new `NWdef` method needed; a small, command-local Mata comparison lives directly in `nwturnover.ado` itself, matching `nwqap.ado`'s own established convention for single-purpose helpers that don't need to be reused elsewhere. Compares two same-node-set, same-directedness networks (both required explicitly - a size or directedness mismatch is rejected outright rather than silently doing something arithmetically possible but conceptually meaningless) and reports `r(stable)`/`r(formed)`/`r(dissolved)` tie counts, the standard Jaccard index of network change (`stable/(stable+formed+dissolved)` - the diagnostic Snijders et al.'s SIENA methodology uses to gauge whether panel-wave network data has "enough change" to be worth dynamic modeling at all), a persistence rate (`stable/(stable+dissolved)`, missing - not spuriously 0 - when the first wave has no ties at all), and a per-node local stability score (`generate()`, default `_turnover`) computed the identical way but restricted to each node's own ties. Undirected dyad-level aggregate counts are correctly halved (each undirected dyad appears in both its row-i and row-j position in the underlying symmetric matrix) while directed counts are not (each ordered pair is independent) - verified directly via a hand-built directed 3-node example alongside the undirected one, not merely asserted correct by symmetry of the formula. Certified against a fully hand-computable 3-node undirected example (2 edges each wave, one shared, giving stable=1/formed=1/dissolved=1, Jaccard=1/3, persistence=1/2, and three distinct hand-derivable per-node rates), a directed analogue confirming the no-halving convention, an identical-networks case (Jaccard and persistence both exactly 1), a zero-tie-in-wave-1 case (persistence correctly missing), and both rejection paths (size mismatch, directedness mismatch). Both modes (dev + production, no Mata-core change - the comparison logic lives in the `.ado` file, not `unw_core.do`). Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this command's own new tests; standalone, nothing else depends on it yet). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 67: general validation stage - package-wide sparse-architecture adoption audit

The user's own explicit closing instruction for this stages-1-7 work: "add in a general validation stage at the end, which also checks if commands effectively use the new architecture throughout." Scoped as a systematic audit against `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`'s own already-completed sparse-backend migration (status: "migration complete" as of that document), not a re-litigation of it - the question asked here is narrower and different: does every command/method that TOUCHES the dense `edge` field do so *safely* (via the established `ensure_dense_built()` guard convention), and does every NEW command/method added during this session's own stages-1-7 work (units 58-66) correctly fit the existing architecture rather than silently introducing a fresh gap.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Systematic audit of every `NWdef` method (161 total) that reads or writes the bare `edge` field directly, rather than through a `get_matrix*()` accessor (which already guards via `ensure_dense_built()`) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Method: a comment-stripped scan of `unw_core.do` (Python, run inline - not a permanent tool) isolated every `` `NWdef'::method(){...} `` body, flagged any containing a bare, word-boundary `edge` reference with no `ensure_dense_built()` call in the same body, then each flag was read and judged by hand rather than trusted mechanically (the naive first pass, before stripping comments, produced 28 raw hits - mostly false positives from the word "edge" appearing in doc-comment prose like "Get sum of *edge* values", not actual code; re-run after stripping `/* */` and `//` comments narrowed this to 12 real candidates). Of those 12: `set_edge`/`set_edge_from_triplets`/`init_edge`/`init_edge_sparse`/`zap` are constructors/setters correctly establishing fresh state unconditionally (no prior state to protect, matching `set_edge()`'s own already-reviewed pattern); `set` (a legacy, fully dead method - confirmed via grep, zero live callers anywhere) was left alone as out of scope, matching this session's own standing practice of not chasing genuinely dead code; the remaining **four were real, previously-undiscovered instances of exactly the same bug class already found once this session** (`keep_nodes()`/`drop_nodes()`, harmonisation unit 59) - see the dedicated row below. |
| Fixed: `clean_matrix_2mode()`, `connect_edge()`, `add_node()`, `dumper()` - all four missing `ensure_dense_built()` before touching `edge` directly | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | **`clean_matrix_2mode()` is the most significant of the four**: it has a genuinely LIVE caller (`nw2fromedge.ado`, two-mode network construction) and was found to be only "accidentally safe" today, not safe by design - traced concretely, not merely suspected: `nw2fromedge.ado` calls `nwfromedge` (sparse-natively rewired in this session's own earlier Commit 8 per `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`) with `undirected` hardcoded, which triggers `nwsym`'s own `symmetrize()` call, which itself already calls `ensure_dense_built()` - so by the time `clean_matrix_2mode()` runs, `edge` happens to already be materialized, but only because of this specific, non-obvious call chain, not because `clean_matrix_2mode()` protects itself. Confirmed the failure mode directly with an isolated Mata probe *before* fixing anything: a genuinely sparse-native two-mode network (built via `create_by_name_sparse()`+`set_edge_from_triplets()`, deliberately never touching `nwsym`) calling `clean_matrix_2mode()` would hit `edge :+ z` with `edge` still `J(0,0,0)` - confirmed via a separate minimal Mata-only repro that this specific shape combination throws a genuine `conformability error` (r(3200)), not a silent wrong answer. `connect_edge()` (zero live callers anywhere - confirmed via grep, genuinely dead code today) and `add_node()` (one live caller, `nwaddnodes.ado`, already documented elsewhere in this file's own Pending table as broken for an unrelated reason - `get_modes_labeled_string(): 3301 subscript invalid` - so this specific gap causes no CURRENT observable failure) were both found the same way and fixed identically, on the reasoning that a currently-dead or currently-blocked call path is still worth making correct now, both because the user's own instruction asks specifically whether the architecture is used correctly *throughout* (not just where a live bug happens to already manifest) and because leaving it broken would silently resurface the instant either method gets a new caller or `nwaddnodes.ado` gets fixed. `dumper()` (a manual debug-only utility, `nw.nws.dumper()` - no `.ado` command calls it) would have silently printed "no edges" for every node on a sparse-native network, misleadingly suggesting an empty network - fixed identically. All four fixed with the exact same one-line pattern already established for `keep_nodes()`/`drop_nodes()`: `ensure_dense_built()` added as the first line of the method body. New `cscripts/test_sparse_architecture_audit.do` gives each of the four its own dedicated, permanent regression probe against a genuinely sparse-native network built via direct Mata construction (`create_by_name_sparse()`+`set_edge_from_triplets()`, deliberately never touching `nwsym`/any dense-materializing accessor beforehand) - `clean_matrix_2mode()`'s own probe exactly reproduces the two-mode scenario above and asserts every same-mode dyad is missing and every cross-mode dyad shows its correct tie value; `add_node()`'s probe asserts the extended matrix has the correct new dimensions and the new node is correctly isolated; `connect_edge()`'s probe asserts both directions of an undirected tie are correctly written; `dumper()`'s probe only asserts it completes without error (a display-only utility, not asserted against captured output). Both modes (dev + production) verified against the rebuilt `lnwcommands.mlib`. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this audit's own new, dedicated test file). |
| Audit of every command/method added during this session's own stages-1-7 work (harmonisation units 58-66) for whether it introduces a NEW sparse-architecture gap | ✅ (audited, no fixes needed) | n/a | ✅ | ✅ | Reviewed each of the 8 new commands/capabilities against the same standard: `nwcohesion`/`CohesionHierarchy` and `NWdef::extract_subgraph()` (unit 59) - both go through `get_matrix_mod()`/`ensure_dense_built()` correctly (vertex-connectivity/max-flow is inherently a dense-pairwise-comparison algorithm, matching `nwkcomponents`' own pre-existing, already-accepted convention - not a new gap, a continuation of an existing, deliberate design choice); `nwaltergen diversity()` (unit 60) - fully sparse-native, reuses the pre-existing `neighbors()` accessor directly, no dense dependency introduced at all; `nwqap predict()` (unit 61) - operates entirely on `nwqap`'s own pre-existing dyadic-regression dataset machinery (already dense-based since long before this session, an established and accepted design, not something this unit's addition changed); `nwcug condition(census)` (unit 62) - pure composition of two already-existing commands (`nwrandom`, `nwdyads`), no new `NWdef` method at all; `nwtoedge comparevars()` (unit 63) - pure composition of two already-existing commands (`nwtoedge`, `nwexpand`), likewise no new method; `nwcommunity algorithm(labelprop)` (unit 64) - dense, via `get_matrix_mod()`, but this exactly matches `algorithm(louvain)`'s own pre-existing, already-accepted convention (modularity-based community detection needs the full weighted adjacency for its aggregation step; not a new gap, a like-for-like continuation); `nwspectral`/`NWdef::calculate_laplacian()` (unit 65) - dense by genuine mathematical necessity (Mata's `symeigensystem()` has no sparse-input mode; matches `nwevcent`'s own already-accepted, already-documented dense eigendecomposition exception in `docs/SPARSE_BACKEND.md`'s own "Status" section); `nwturnover` (unit 66) - dense, via `get_matrix_mod()`, a reasonable choice for an O(n²) dyad-level comparison between two whole networks (matching this package's own established "moderate network size" scale target used throughout its non-sparse-migrated commands). **Conclusion**: none of the 8 introduce a new, previously-nonexistent architecture gap - each either correctly reuses an already-guarded dense path or is already sparse-native; the four real gaps found and fixed in this same unit (`clean_matrix_2mode`/`connect_edge`/`add_node`/`dumper`) all predate this session's stages-1-7 work and were latent in the codebase since the original sparse-backend migration itself. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 68: `nwergm` begins - term API, registry, and first 8 ERGM terms, with a process anomaly disclosed in full

**Process note, disclosed for the record**: this unit's code (`unw_ergm.do`, `docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md`, `docs/ERGM_PROVENANCE.md`) was originally produced and committed (commit `21cf56f`) by a research subagent that had been dispatched with a narrow, read-only research brief (study Statnet's MCMLE algorithm and licensing, write findings to a scratch file) but - because it was launched as a context-inheriting "fork" rather than an isolated fresh agent, and therefore saw this session's full standing instruction to build `nwergm` - went beyond that brief on its own initiative and wrote/committed real implementation files without being asked to and without going through this session's own established per-unit discipline (no permanent `cscripts/` test file, no `lib/build.do` wiring, no `docs/CERTIFICATION.md` entry). This is exactly the kind of unauthorized-commit action this project's own git-safety norms exist to prevent, and it is recorded here rather than quietly folded in. Concretely, two research forks (studying Statnet's R-level and C-level architecture, both scoped correctly and staying within their own briefs) were running at the same time; the third one produced the anomaly. Before accepting any of it, every claim was independently re-verified from scratch in this same unit (see below) - none of it was taken on the subagent's own say-so. The code itself, once verified, was found to be genuinely correct and well-designed, so it was kept and hardened into this package's normal process (test files, build wiring, documentation) rather than discarded - discarding independently-verified-correct work would have been wasteful, but the anomaly itself is a real process failure worth recording so the same subagent-dispatch mistake (using a context-inheriting fork for a task meant to stay narrowly scoped) is not repeated.

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `unw_ergm.do` (new Mata core file, compiled into `lnwcommands.mlib` alongside `unw_core.do` - `lib/build.do` updated accordingly): `ErgmGraph` (toggle-friendly sparse binary graph via per-node `asarray()` adjacency sets - deliberately NOT `NWdef`'s own CSR sparse index, which rebuilds wholesale, O(n+m), on every change and would make MCMC's toggle-heavy access pattern catastrophically slow; confirmed as the right call by both the R- and C-level Statnet studies), `ErgmTermData` (generic per-term-instance parameter struct - decay/attr/edgecovmat fields, used by whichever term needs them) plus a function-pointer-based term registry (`ErgmModel`/`addterm`/`full_statistic`/`full_change`) mirroring Statnet's own mature `ModelTerm` I/S/C/U/F function-pointer contract (confirmed via direct study of `ergm_changestat.h` to be the same design, not a coincidence - validates using it directly). Eight v1 terms implemented as `statistic()`/`change()` function pairs: `edges`, `mutual`, `nodematch`, `nodecov`, `nodeocov`, `nodeicov`, `edgecov`, and the geometrically-weighted family `gwdegree`/`gwodegree`/`gwidegree`/`gwesp` (fixed decay only, curved/free-decay deferred per the roadmap). | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | **Independently re-verified in this unit, not trusted from the originating commit's own claims**: (1) `stat_*()` for all applicable terms checked against REAL Statnet `ergm` 4.12.0 output (R 4.6.0, installed locally for development-time validation only, never a runtime dependency) on two hand-built canonical networks (undirected 5-node: edges/nodematch/nodecov/edgecov/gwesp/gwdegree; directed 5-node: edges/mutual/nodematch/nodecov/gwidegree/gwodegree) - all 12 values matched to floating-point precision; now a permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_statistics.do`. (2) every term's `change()` checked against brute-force full recomputation (`statistic(after)-statistic(before)`) across 7 random directed/undirected networks (6-12 nodes) with all applicable terms registered together in one model - worst discrepancy ~1e-14 (floating-point noise only); now a permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_changestat.do`, built around the file's own `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` helper (the "slow reference implementation" Part IX of the governing task calls for, free by construction since every term's `statistic()` exists anyway). GWESP's v1 implementation recomputes shared-partner counts on demand (`ErgmGraph::common_neighbors()`, O(min(deg_i,deg_j)) per pair) rather than maintaining an incremental shared-partner cache - a deliberate, documented simplification (correct but not yet the fastest possible; the cache is a profiled-later performance item, not a correctness gap, per the governing task's own "do not optimize unprofiled code prematurely" instruction) - recorded in `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`. `docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md` (call-flow trace, term/proposal/MCMLE/variance/diagnostics architecture, all confirmed via direct source reads of the cloned `statnet/ergm` v4.13.0 repository) and `docs/ERGM_PROVENANCE.md` (GPL-3 license and attribution requirements read verbatim from `ergm`'s own `LICENSE` file; clean-room-reimplementation reasoning for why no GPL obligation is triggered; citations given anyway as intellectual honesty) both independently reviewed for accuracy in this unit and found sound, with `docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md` additionally expanded with two fuller architecture-study appendices. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 107 files): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2** (this subsystem's own new, dedicated tests; nothing else in the package depends on it yet). **Explicitly not yet done** (the remainder of the governing task's own Parts X-XXXVIII): the Stata-facing `nwergm` command itself (the pre-existing `nwergm.ado` on disk is a 2014-era prototype that shells out to R - exactly the architecture this whole initiative exists to replace, not yet rewritten), MCMC simulation/proposals (including TNT), MPLE, MCMLE, `eclass` integration, controls, diagnostics, GOF, and `docs/ERGM_ARCHITECTURE.md` (the developer-facing design document several existing comments already forward-reference) - all tracked explicitly, not silently dropped. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 69: `nwergm` MPLE - maximum pseudolikelihood estimation via Stata's native `logit`

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ErgmModel::change_toward_one()`/`build_mple_data()` in `unw_ergm.do` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Implements the standard Hunter & Handcock (2006) MPLE construction (see `docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md` Appendix A §5): one row per dyad, columns = the change statistic for setting that dyad to "tied" (NOT `change()`'s own raw output, which is signed by the dyad's CURRENT state - `change_toward_one()` negates `change()` for already-tied dyads, since a toggle's change statistic is antisymmetric in direction), final column = the observed tie indicator. Per Part XII of the governing task brief ("exploit Stata's existing logistic estimation... rather than recreating standard numerical optimisation"), the actual fit is Stata's own native `logit ..., noconstant` (`noconstant` because the `edges` term already plays the intercept's role) - `unw_ergm.do` only builds the design matrix, exported to Stata via `st_matrix()`. Certified against real Statnet `ergm` MPLE output (`dev/ergm_reference/ref_mple.R`) on three cases: undirected edges-only (an exactly-derivable case - this canonical network's density is exactly 0.5, so the true MPLE is exactly `logit(0.5)=0`, matched to 1e-6); undirected edges+nodematch(sex) (both coefficients near-zero in the real Statnet fit, matched to 1e-4); directed edges+mutual (a genuinely nonzero case, matched to Stata's own default 7-significant-digit display precision against Statnet's `edges=-0.91629073186161214`, `mutual=0.22314355130166649`). Now a permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_mple.do`. A real gap was caught and fixed while wiring up the production-mode copy of this test: the new `ErgmModel` methods had been added to `unw_ergm.do` without rebuilding `lib/lnwcommands.mlib` first, so the production-mode test correctly failed with "function not declared" until `lib/build.do` was re-run - a reminder (already well-established elsewhere in this package) that every `unw_ergm.do`/`unw_core.do` Mata change needs a rebuild before production-mode certification means anything. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 108 files): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 70: `nwergm` MCMC engine - uniform and TNT proposals, certified against exact edges-only Bernoulli theory

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ergm_propose_uniform()`/`ergm_propose_tnt()` (Part X's `ErgmProposal` interface: `propose()`/`log_ratio()`, independent of terms) and `ErgmMCMCSample()` (the Metropolis-Hastings sampling loop) in `unw_ergm.do` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Both proposals return `(tail, head, logratio)` from a fixed-signature Mata function, matching Statnet's own `MHProposal` separation (confirmed via the Part I study: proposal code never touches change statistics, term code never knows how a toggle was chosen). TNT (Morris, Handcock & Hunter 2008) reproduces the exact published Hastings-ratio formulas re-derived and confirmed against the actual shipped Statnet C constants during the Part I study (`docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md` Appendix B §6): with probability `P=0.5` propose removing a uniformly random EXISTING tie, otherwise propose toggling a uniformly random dyad from the full dyad space, with the log-ratio correcting for the resulting non-uniform proposal density. **A real bug found and fixed before any certification passed**: the first version drew the proposed dyad's two endpoints from TWO INDEPENDENT random picks into the current tie list, rather than the two columns of ONE randomly chosen row - meaning the "existing edge" branch could propose a dyad that was not actually a tie at all (and, on an unlucky draw, `i==j`, corrupting graph state with a self-loop and crashing `ErgmGraph::toggle()` with an invalid-subscript error the very first time the bug was exercised in the certification run below). Fixed by drawing one random row index and reading both endpoints from it. The uniform-random-dyad proposal's own dyad-index-to-(i,j) unranking (both the undirected upper-triangle and the directed skip-diagonal cases) was separately verified to be a genuine bijection - every index 1..D maps to a distinct dyad with no gaps or duplicates, checked exhaustively for two small networks. Certified via the ONE ERGM model with a closed-form, analytically exact stationary distribution (Part XXIX's own "tiny network exact enumeration" spirit, derived directly from the model definition rather than simulated): under the edges-only Bernoulli model, every dyad is an independent Bernoulli(invlogit(theta)), so the edge-count statistic is exactly Binomial(D,p) with known mean and variance. Both proposals, run at three (n, directedness, theta) configurations, matched the exact theoretical mean to within a small fraction of their own Monte Carlo standard error, matched EACH OTHER (a specific, sensitive check on TNT's own Hastings-ratio correction - a broken correction produces a systematic bias relative to the uniform proposal, not just noise, and this is exactly what caught the endpoint-pairing bug once the crash itself was fixed and the assertions were tightened) and matched the theoretical variance within a generous tolerance. Now a permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_mcmc.do` (runs in ~5 seconds). `ErgmMCMCSample()` returns statistic LEVELS (not deviations), seeded from the current network's own `statistic()` before burn-in - matching Statnet's own convention (`docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md` Appendix B §5) so a caller can compare sampled rows directly against the observed network's statistic without an extra offset step. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 109 files): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 71: `nwergm` MCMLE - a simplified, disclosed Newton-step estimator certified against a real Statnet MCMLE fit

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ErgmMCMLE()` in `unw_ergm.do` (Part XIII) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Implements the Geyer & Thompson (1992) Monte Carlo MLE outer loop (simulate at current theta, take a Newton step on the sample-covariance-based importance-sampling surrogate, repeat sequentially - the network carries over between iterations rather than restarting from the observed network each time, matching Statnet's own `MCMLE.sequential=TRUE` default per `docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md` Appendix A §9) with two DELIBERATE, EXPLICITLY DISCLOSED simplifications relative to Statnet's own considerably more elaborate procedure (both documented in `ErgmMCMLE()`'s own header comment, not silently different behavior): (1) step length is a Mahalanobis-trust-region cap on the Newton step's own norm (in the metric of the simulated sample's covariance) rather than Hummel, Hunter & Handcock's (2012) exact convex-hull linear program - no general LP solver is available in Mata, and this session's own Part I research explicitly recommended this exact substitute; (2) the final variance-covariance matrix applies a simple per-dimension lag-1-autocorrelation inflation factor `(1+rho)/(1-rho)` to the sampled statistics' own covariance before inverting, rather than Statnet's full spectral/HAC long-run-variance estimator - still a genuine autocorrelation correction (not the naive, uncorrected covariance the Part I research explicitly warned would give systematically too-small standard errors), just a simpler one. Convergence test is a simplified stand-in for Statnet's own default "confidence" (Hotelling T²) criterion: every component of the centered sample mean must be small relative to its own Monte Carlo standard error AND the last step must have been untruncated. Certified against a REAL Statnet `ergm()` MCMLE fit (`dev/ergm_reference/ref_mcmle.R`) on `edges + mutual` for the same directed canonical network used throughout this subsystem's certification - a genuinely dyad-DEPENDENT model (unlike the MPLE-only certification in unit 69), so this is a real test of the Newton-step/step-length/convergence machinery, not just the design-matrix builder. Starting from the already-certified MPLE values, the fit converged in 6 iterations to coefficients within Monte Carlo tolerance of Statnet's own result (edges -0.928 vs Statnet's -0.918; mutual 0.236 vs Statnet's 0.221) - and the simplified autocorrelation-corrected variance estimator landed remarkably close to Statnet's own more sophisticated sandwich estimator (edges variance 0.457 vs Statnet's 0.445; mutual variance 2.131 vs Statnet's 2.172), a genuinely encouraging result for a deliberately simplified estimator, not merely "close enough to pass a loose test". Now a permanent regression test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_mcmle.do` (~19 seconds, both modes). Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 110 files): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 72: `nwergm` becomes a real, usable eclass command

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwergm.ado` completely rewritten (Parts IV/XVII/XVIII) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The pre-existing file on disk was a 2014-era prototype whose entire implementation shelled out to R (writing an R script, running it via `shell`, parsing its CSV output back into Stata) - exactly the architecture this whole initiative exists to replace. Fully replaced with a native `eclass` command built on `unw_ergm.do`'s own already-certified term/MCMC/MPLE/MCMLE engine (units 68-71). Syntax matches the governing task brief's own examples almost exactly: each term is its own Stata option (`edges`, `mutual`, `nodematch(varlist)`, `nodecov(varlist)`, `nodeicov(varlist)`, `nodeocov(varlist)`, `edgecov(netname)`, `gwesp(real)`, `gwdegree(real)`, `gwodegree(real)`, `gwidegree(real)`), plus `method(mple\|mcmle)` (auto-selected: MPLE alone whenever every requested term is dyad-independent, matching Statnet's own "MPLE is the MLE" shortcut - `docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md` §1 step 5), MCMC/MCMLE controls (`mcmcburnin`/`mcmcinterval`/`mcmcsamplesize`/`mcmleiterations`/`proposal`/`seed`/`verbose`). `edgecov()` deliberately takes ANOTHER ALREADY-LOADED NETWORK's own tie values as the dyadic covariate (not a hand-built matrix) - per the brief's own "do not require users to construct matrices manually" instruction, and consistent with this package's existing convention (`nwqap`'s own "networks as covariates" formula terms) for supplying dyadic data. Network-type validation rejects two-mode, temporal, and valued/weighted networks outright with an explicit, specific error message (never silently projects/collapses/dichotomizes - Parts XXII/XXIII/XXIV), and rejects each directed-only or undirected-only term against a mismatched network (`mutual`/`nodeicov`/`nodeocov`/`gwodegree`/`gwidegree` require directed; `gwesp` (v1 scope) requires undirected). The NWdef->ErgmGraph bridge (`ergm_bridge_from_netobj()`, one call per `nwergm` invocation, defined once at file scope like `nwqap.ado`'s own file-scope Mata helpers) reads the current network exactly once via the already-established `neighbors()` sparse accessor - `unw_ergm.do`'s own `ErgmGraph` stays fully decoupled from `NWdef`, exactly as designed. **Several real bugs found and fixed while getting the very first end-to-end call to work, none caught by the Mata-level unit tests (units 68-71) since those never exercised the actual `.ado` glue code**: (1) a nested Mata for/if construct written as a one-line interactive `mata:` command inside a Stata `forvalues` loop - the exact known-broken pattern already documented elsewhere in this package's own development; fixed by moving the bridge into a proper file-scope Mata function, called as a plain one-liner. (2) `st_store()`'s multi-variable form requires a genuine Mata string ROWVECTOR for its variable-list argument, not a single space-separated string scalar (confirmed via an isolated repro) - every other `st_store()` call site in this package stores exactly one variable at a time, so this multi-column MPLE-design-matrix case was a genuinely new situation; fixed with `tokens()`. (3) Both the MPLE and MCMLE branches transposed `e(b)`/the fitted coefficient vector unnecessarily, turning a correct 1×k row vector into a k×1 column vector before `ereturn post` (which requires a row vector) - fixed by removing the stray transposes. (4) `logit`'s own `e(b)`/`e(V)` carry an equation-name stripe that must be blanked before assigning fresh coefficient names, or `ereturn post` fails with "name conflict" (r(507)) - the exact same bug class already found and fixed once in `nwqap.ado` (see its own certified row), here hit independently in a completely different command. (5) `e(converged)`/`e(mcmle_iterations)` were captured via `st_numscalar("e(name)", ...)` and then referenced as `e(name)` AFTER an intervening `ereturn post` call, which clears the e() results namespace - reading back as missing; the exact same "results are only published once the program exits" confusion already found and fixed once in `nw2project.ado`, here for `e()` instead of `r()`. (6) The displayed "MCMC sample size" line was leftover placeholder code (`e(N)>0`, a boolean) instead of the actual requested sample size - fixed to display the real value. Certified via a new, dedicated `.ado`-level integration test, `cscripts/test_nwergm_ado.do` (~12 seconds, both modes) covering: the MPLE path (exact density-0.5 edges-only case, nodematch addition); `method(mple)` explicitly requested on a dyad-dependent model (labeled correctly, not an error); the MCMLE path on a directed network with genuine reciprocity structure (converges, reports a genuinely positive mutual coefficient, not just "runs"); both proposals threaded correctly through the option; every network-type/term-mismatch rejection path; `edgecov()`; and `gwesp()` end to end. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 111 files): same pre-existing baseline fails, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 73: `nwergm.ado` Mata-tempvar leak fix (`estimates store` now works)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fix: leaked Mata objects in `nwergm.ado` broke `estimates store`/`estimates table`/`esttab` (Part XXXVIII acceptance criterion #20) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Discovered while directly verifying "estimates store works": `qui nwergm mynet, edges` / `estimates store m1` / `estimates table m1` failed with `Mata object __000002 already exists r(110)`. Root cause: every `tempname __td_X` + `mata: `__td_X' = ErgmTermData()`-style one-line interactive `mata:` call in `nwergm.ado`'s term-construction loop (one per requested term - `edges`/`mutual`/each `nodematch()`/`nodecov()`/`nodeicov()`/`nodeocov()`/`edgecov()` varlist entry/`gwesp()`/`gwdegree()`/`gwodegree()`/`gwidegree()`, plus `__nw_G`/`__nw_M`/`__nw_D`/`__theta0`/`__fit`) creates a genuine, permanent, top-level Mata variable - unlike a real Mata function's own local variables, a one-liner `mata: X = ...` object is never garbage-collected on its own. The program's own cleanup only ever dropped three of these (`mata: mata drop `__nw_G' `__nw_M' `__nw_D'`), so every `nwergm` call leaked one Mata object per term instance into the ambient workspace; enough calls (or the right sequence of numbered-tempname allocation) eventually collides with a Mata object Stata's own internal machinery allocates (surfacing here via `estimates table`, though the collision point itself is essentially arbitrary - not `estimates`-specific). Fixed by accumulating every Mata-side tempname's expanded (literal) name into a running Stata local, `__ergm_matatemps`, at the point each is created, then dropping the whole list in one `mata: mata drop `__ergm_matatemps'` call at the end of the program body (replacing the old three-name-only drop) - the same "always mata drop every tempname a program created" discipline already used consistently elsewhere in this package, just not originally applied to the per-term-instance objects created inside loops. Verified via an isolated repro exercising the exact failing sequence (`nwergm` / `estimates store` / `estimates table` / `esttab`, plus three consecutive bare `nwergm` calls back to back) - all now succeed cleanly with no Mata-object collisions. Full `cscripts/` sweep re-run after the fix (dev mode, all 6 `test_nwergm_*.do` files, and production mode against a freshly rebuilt `lib/lnwcommands.mlib`): same pre-existing baseline fails as unit 72, no regressions. Certified at **L1** (a scoped bug fix to already-certified unit 72 infrastructure, not new capability). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 74: `docs/ERGM_ARCHITECTURE.md` and the extension-API proof term

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `docs/ERGM_ARCHITECTURE.md` (Parts XXXI/XXXII) | ✅ | n/a | n/a | ✅ | The developer-facing design document forward-referenced from `unw_ergm.do`'s own header comment, `nwergm.ado`'s SMCL help, `docs/ERGM_PROVENANCE.md`, and `docs/ERGM_STATNET_STUDY.md`, but not previously written. Covers: why `ErgmGraph` is a separate representation from `NWdef` (asarray-based O(1)-average adjacency vs. `NWdef`'s own O(n+nnz)-per-rebuild CSR index - unsuitable for MCMC's toggle-heavy access pattern); `ErgmGraph`/`ErgmTermData`'s own field-level design; the term API contract (`statistic()`/`change()` fixed signatures, the pre-toggle-state convention, the "no separate central term registry - `nwergm.ado`'s own option-parsing code IS the registry" design choice and why it was made, with an explicit note on when to revisit it); `ErgmModel` as the single place that knows "a model is a list of terms"; `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` as the permanent "slow reference implementation" the governing brief called for; MPLE (`change_toward_one()`/`build_mple_data()`, why the actual logistic regression runs in Stata's own `logit` rather than Mata); the proposal API and both shipped proposals (uniform, TNT) with the Hastings-ratio rationale; `ErgmMCMLE()`'s two deliberate, disclosed simplifications relative to Statnet's own machinery (Mahalanobis step-length cap, lag-1-autocorrelation variance inflation); and the `.ado` integration layer, including a full writeup of the Mata-tempvar-leak bug just fixed in unit 73 (the "own cleanup of every Mata object it creates" rule, stated as a standing requirement for any future term addition, not just a historical bug note). Closes with a concrete, numbered "how to add a new term" walkthrough (write the function pair → verify by hand against the published definition → wire into `nwergm.ado` → certify via `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` plus a real-Statnet statistic comparison where feasible → document → full regression sweep) - the governing brief's own explicit requirement that a future developer can add an effect without touching the parser/sampler/estimator/diagnostics. |
| `istar2` demonstration term (Part XXXII: "prove the architecture with at least one demo/test effect through the extension API") | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The undirected 2-star count (`sum_i C(deg(i),2)`), added to `unw_ergm.do` in a clearly-marked "EXTENSION DEMONSTRATION - NOT part of the real v1 term registry" section, built by following `docs/ERGM_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own numbered walkthrough exactly (write `stat_istar2()`/`change_istar2()` with the fixed term-API signatures, using a small shared `ergm_2star_kernel(d)=d*(d-1)/2` helper analogous to `gw_kernel()`'s own role for the GW family) - proof that the architecture genuinely supports adding a term without touching `ErgmModel`, `ErgmMCMCSample`, `build_mple_data`, or `ErgmMCMLE` (none of those files/functions were modified; `istar2` is not wired into `nwergm.ado`'s option surface at all, since 2-star family terms are a `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md` item, not v1 scope - it exists purely as a working, certified proof of the extension process, not a new officially-supported effect). Certified three ways in the new `cscripts/test_nwergm_demoterm.do` (both modes): (1) two hand-computable exact cases (a 4-node star: degrees 3,1,1,1 → istar2=C(3,2)=3; a 3-node triangle: degrees 2,2,2 → istar2=3×C(2,2)=3 - both matched exactly); (2) the same `ErgmCertifyChangeStat()` brute-force change-statistic certification the real v1 terms use, combined inside a multi-term `ErgmModel` alongside `edges` and `gwdegree` (proving composition, not just isolated correctness) across 4 random undirected networks - max discrepancy ~1e-15 in every case. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 112 files): same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72/73, no regressions. Certified at **L1** (a documented worked example proving existing v1 architecture, not new user-facing capability). |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 75: `estat mcmcdiag` - basic MCMC diagnostics for `nwergm`

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ErgmMCMCSampleDiag()` in `unw_ergm.do` (Part XIX groundwork) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | A second Metropolis-Hastings sampler function, identical in every respect to the already-certified `ErgmMCMCSample()` except that it additionally tallies the acceptance rate over its own sampling phase (post burn-in) - added as a SEPARATE function rather than extending `ErgmMCMCSample()`'s own signature, so the latter's already-certified behavior (used throughout `ErgmMCMLE()`'s outer loop, where per-iteration acceptance rate is not needed) never changes. `ErgmMCMLE()` now calls this instead of `ErgmMCMCSample()` for its one "final" simulation at the converged (or last-tried) theta - that draw already existed (it produces the final variance-covariance matrix) and is now reused as `nwergm`'s own basic MCMC diagnostics sample, rather than running a second, separate, redundant simulation. `ErgmMCMLEFit` gained two new fields (`finalsample`, `acceptrate`) to carry this out. Certified in `cscripts/test_nwergm_mcmc.do`: (1) under an identical seed, `ErgmMCMCSampleDiag()`'s own sample output is BYTE-IDENTICAL to `ErgmMCMCSample()`'s (confirms the added tally does not perturb the draw sequence - e.g. no extra `runiform()` call); (2) its reported acceptance rate exactly matches an INDEPENDENTLY hand-written duplicate of the identical loop kept entirely inside the test file (a genuine second implementation of "count accepted proposals," not a re-read of the same number). Re-ran `cscripts/test_nwergm_mcmle.do` unchanged and confirmed byte-for-byte identical results under its own fixed seed (the substitution is provably a no-op numerically, only the new tally is additive). |
| `nwergm_estat.ado` / `estat mcmcdiag` (Part XIX) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | The postestimation command Part XIX called for: per-statistic mean/SD/lag-1 autocorrelation/AR(1)-based effective sample size, plus the overall Metropolis-Hastings acceptance rate and a restatement of `nwergm`'s own MCMLE convergence-test result - explicitly worded to never claim convergence merely because estimation stopped (the governing brief's own explicit requirement), framing a satisfied convergence test as "a necessary check that passed, not a proof of global convergence." Available only after `method(mcmle)` (`e(estat_cmd)` is set in both the MPLE and MCMLE branches of `nwergm.ado`, but the MPLE branch has no MCMC sample to diagnose - `estat mcmcdiag` errors informatively, r(498), rather than crashing or fabricating output). Implemented via Stata's real `estat` dispatch mechanism (`e(estat_cmd)` naming a command-specific `<cmd>_estat.ado`, confirmed from `estat.ado`'s own source - not the `<cmd>_estat`-by-convention-only guess this was initially built against), reading `e(mcmcsample)` (a new `e()` matrix - the final simulation's own sufficient-statistic draws, `samplesize x nparam`, populated by `nwergm.ado` from `ErgmMCMLEFit.finalsample`) plus the new `e(mcmc_acceptrate)`/`e(mcmc_burnin)`/`e(mcmc_interval)`/`e(mcmc_samplesize)` scalars. **A genuine bug found and fixed while getting the very first `estat mcmcdiag` call to work**: a nested `rclass` program call (`nwergm_estat` calling `nwergm_estat_mcmcdiag`) does NOT automatically propagate the inner program's own `r()` results back up through the outer program once it returns - confirmed via an isolated, minimal 2-level `rclass`-nesting repro (an `inner`/`outer` toy pair) before concluding this was the actual cause rather than assuming from documentation; Stata's own `estat.ado` handles this for ITS OWN nested call via an explicit `return add`, and `nwergm_estat` needed the identical `return add` after its own call to `nwergm_estat_mcmcdiag` - without it, `r(acceptrate)` silently read back as missing after `estat mcmcdiag` despite displaying correctly and despite a DIRECT call to `nwergm_estat_mcmcdiag` (bypassing `estat` entirely) working fine, which is exactly what made this a genuinely non-obvious bug rather than a simple typo. Certified via a new `cscripts/test_nwergm_estat.do` (both modes): the MPLE-path rejection; a real MCMLE fit's acceptance rate matching `e(mcmc_acceptrate)` exactly through the full `estat` dispatch chain (not a direct call); `e(mcmcsample)`'s own dimensions and finiteness; and - specifically targeting the unit 73 Mata-tempvar-leak bug class - that `estat mcmcdiag` still works correctly after `estimates store`/`estimates restore`. `nwergm_estat.ado`/`nwergm_estat.sthlp` added to `_pkg_ado.txt`/`_pkg_hlp.txt`/`nwcommands-ado.pkg` for packaging parity; `nwergm.ado`'s own SMCL help gained a "Postestimation" section and four new `e()` result rows, regenerated via `nw_helpwriter` (hit the pre-existing, already-documented "too few quotes" crash on `nwergm.ado`'s own program-body content - confirmed, as with `nwplot.ado`/`nwcomponents.ado` before it, that the crash occurs strictly AFTER the doc-header copy completes, so the regenerated `.sthlp` is complete and correct despite it). Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 114 files): same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72-74, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 76: `estat gof` - basic simulation-based goodness of fit for `nwergm`

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ErgmGraph::to_dense()` / `ErgmMatToLiteral()` in `unw_ergm.do` (Part XX groundwork) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | `to_dense()` renders an `ErgmGraph`'s current adjacency as a dense n×n 0/1 matrix; `ErgmMatToLiteral()` renders any real matrix as a literal Stata matrix-expression string (e.g. `"(0,1,0\1,0,1\0,1,0)"`). The latter exists specifically to avoid a genuine, previously-undiscovered bug in `nwset.ado` itself, found while building `estat gof`: `nwset, mat(X)` cannot accept an existing Stata matrix's bare NAME - only a literal parenthesized expression - because `nwset.ado` line 651 (`mata: mode1 = cols(`mat')`) resolves its own `mat()` argument as a bare Mata expression, and a literal expression parses directly as an anonymous Mata matrix constant while a bare name does not auto-import (confirmed via an isolated repro completely independent of `nwergm`, in a bare session using only the compiled `lnwcommands.mlib`: `matrix define X = (0,1\1,0)` then `nwset, mat(X)` fails with "X not found" - not fixed at the source, out of this subsystem's scope, recorded in this document's own Pending list below). `estat gof` avoids the landmine entirely by rendering every simulated network as a literal expression via `ErgmMatToLiteral(G.to_dense())` before ever calling `nwset`. |
| `estat gof` in `nwergm_estat.ado` (Part XX) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Compares the fitted model's own simulated networks against the network `nwergm` was fitted on, on three dimensions computed via this package's own existing commands rather than duplicating their algorithms (the governing brief's own explicit instruction): mean degree (plain arithmetic - not "an algorithm" worth reusing machinery for), average geodesic distance (`nwgeodesic`, real BFS/Dijkstra machinery), and complete (3-edge) triad count (`nwtriads`, real MAN-census machinery, meaningful for both directed and undirected per `nwtriads.ado`'s own documented convention). Available after EITHER estimation method (unlike `estat mcmcdiag`) - for `method(mple)` fits, which never ran MCMC during estimation, `estat gof` runs its own first-ever simulation for that fit, starting from the observed network; for `method(mcmle)` fits it continues the chain from wherever `nwergm`'s own fit left it. Both the observed and simulated sides are computed via an *identically-constructed* temporary network materialized fresh from Mata each time (not by calling `nwgeodesic`/`nwtriads` on `e(depvar)` directly), for consistency between the two columns. **Three genuine, non-obvious bugs found and fixed while getting this to work end to end, documented in full in `docs/ERGM_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own "Postestimation" section**: (1) the `nwset.ado` bare-matrix-name bug above; (2) a Mata function defined at one `.ado` file's own file scope (`nwergm.ado`'s `ergm_bridge_from_netobj()`) is NOT reliably callable from a *different* `.ado` file's own separate auto-load event, even after the first file's command already ran successfully in the same session - confirmed via direct trial (`capture mata: mata which ergm_bridge_from_netobj()` fails right after a successful `nwergm` call) and missed for a while because ad hoc scratch verification used explicit `run nwergm.ado` (which masks the issue) rather than plain command auto-load; fixed by giving `nwergm_estat.ado` its own file-scope copy, `nwergm_estat_bridge_from_netobj()`; (3) a genuine, pre-existing bug in `nwtriads.ado` itself (unrelated to `nwergm`): it crashes with "n not found - data already wide" (r(111)) on any network with zero ties, confirmed via an isolated repro independent of `nwergm` entirely - a real possibility for a simulated MCMC draw, so every `nwtriads`/`nwgeodesic` call on a *simulated* network is wrapped in `capture`, excluding that draw from the relevant average (reported in the output) rather than crashing the whole command. **A fourth, more serious bug was found in `nwergm.ado` itself while verifying `estat gof`'s own "Observed" column against the network by hand**: `e(ties)` was captured from `__nwergm_last_G.nties` *after* `ErgmMCMLE()` returned, but that graph object is the live, mutated MCMC state (not a frozen copy of the observed network) - so for every `method(mcmle)` fit since unit 72, `e(ties)` (and therefore `nwergm_display`'s own "Ties:" line, and any downstream computation using it, including `estat gof`'s own observed mean degree before this fix) silently reported the LAST SIMULATED tie count rather than the true observed one. Fixed by capturing the observed tie count into a Stata local immediately after `ergm_bridge_from_netobj()` runs, before any MCMC ever executes, and posting that value via `ereturn scalar ties` in both branches (replacing the old post-hoc `mata: st_numscalar("e(ties)", ...)` pattern). Certified via a new `cscripts/test_nwergm_gof.do` (both modes): the MCMLE path with a real observed-vs-simulated comparison and an exact `obs_meandeg` check (which is exactly what caught the `e(ties)` bug); the MPLE path on a SECOND, separately-built network in the same session (the exact sequence that originally surfaced the `nwtriads.ado` zero-tie crash and the file-scope Mata bridging bug); the "no fitted model" and "network no longer loaded" error paths. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 125 files): same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72-75, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 77: tiny-network exact-enumeration certification for a dyad-dependent model

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cscripts/test_nwergm_exact_enum.do` (Part XXIX) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | This project's own certification suite already had a "tiny network exact" check (the edges-only Bernoulli case in `test_nwergm_mcmc.do`), but that model is dyad-INDEPENDENT and analytically tractable in closed form - it never actually tests the sampler against a case where dyad independence cannot be leaned on. This unit adds the genuinely stronger check the governing brief itself calls for: a full ENUMERATION of all `2^6 = 64` possible directed graphs on a 3-node network under `edges + mutual` (`theta = (0.3, 0.7)`), computing the EXACT normalizing constant `Z = sum_y exp(theta'g(y))` and exact expectations `E[edges]`, `E[mutual]` by literal exhaustive summation - a Statnet-independent gold standard derived directly from the model's own definition, not a comparison against any external software. `mutual` makes this a genuinely dyad-DEPENDENT model (toggling one dyad can change the model's likelihood contribution in a way that depends on ANOTHER dyad's current state), so there is no closed form to fall back on the way the edges-only case has - this is the first test in the whole suite that validates the sampler with no independence shortcut available at all. Both proposals (uniform and TNT) are run at the SAME theta and checked against the SAME exact target: uniform lands at E[edges]=4.0818/E[mutual]=1.4836, TNT at E[edges]=4.0922/E[mutual]=1.4930, against the exact E[edges]=4.086698/E[mutual]=1.493808 - both proposals within roughly one Monte Carlo standard error of the truth (assertion tolerance is a generous 8 SEs, matching this suite's own established convention for stochastic checks, but the actual observed agreement is far tighter than that bound requires). Also includes a basic sanity-bound assertion (both exact expectations must lie strictly inside their statistic's own achievable range). Now a permanent regression test, both dev and production mode. Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 126 files): same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72-76, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 78: `nwergm simulate` - draw networks from a fixed-coefficient ERGM

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nwergm simulate` (Part X's own example syntax) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | Draws one or more networks from a fully-specified ERGM (fixed coefficients, matching Statnet's own `simulate.ergm`), via the same native MCMC engine `nwergm`'s own estimation path uses. Syntax: `nwergm simulate nodes, edges [mutual] [gwesp()/gwdegree()/gwodegree()/gwidegree()] theta(numlist) [directed nsim() mcmcburnin() mcmcinterval() proposal() seed() generate()]` - deliberately reuses the exact same term-construction pattern (`ErgmModel.addterm()` calls building `ErgmTermData` instances) `nwergm`'s own estimation branch uses, rather than a parallel implementation, and deliberately scoped to the terms needing no external covariate data (`edges`/`mutual`/the gw family) - covariate terms (`nodematch()`/`nodecov()`/`nodeicov()`/`nodeocov()`/`edgecov()`) are a disclosed v1 scope reduction for simulate specifically (recorded in `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`), not a silent gap: they would need a coefficient AND a variable/network per term rather than a coefficient alone, and Part X's own example syntax only demonstrates `edges`/`mutual`. `theta()` supplies one coefficient per requested term, in the same order the term options are listed - avoiding per-term coefficient sub-options entirely. Dispatched from `program nwergm`'s own entry point via a non-destructive peek at `` `1' `` (`if `"`1'"' == "simulate"`, then `gettoken`+dispatch to a new `nwergm_simulate` program) - Stata auto-populates numbered positional locals from the raw argument string at program entry, so this check runs safely before `syntax` itself is ever called. Each of `nsim()` draws gets its own fresh burn-in (matching `nwergm`'s own control conventions, not one continued chain) and is materialized into a real `nwset`-backed network via the same `to_dense()`/`ErgmMatToLiteral()` path `estat gof` (unit 76) already established, named `generate()_1`, `generate()_2`, ... for `nsim()>1` or plain `generate()` for `nsim(1)` (default stub `ergmsim`), mirroring `nwrandom.ado`'s own established multi-network-generation naming convention. **One bug found and fixed while first testing this**: two `directed`-requirement guard checks (`gwodegree()`/`gwidegree()` requiring `directed`) compared a quoted-string real-option local (`"`gwodegree'"`, e.g. `"0"`) against a bare numeric literal `0` with `!=`, a genuine string/numeric type mismatch (r(109)) - fixed by removing the stray quotes (`gwodegree' != 0`), matching how the OTHER, adjacent `gwesp' != 0` check in the same file was already correctly written. Certified via a new `cscripts/test_nwergm_simulate.do` (both modes): an end-to-end statistical correctness check re-deriving the SAME exact Bernoulli fact `test_nwergm_mcmc.do` certifies at the Mata level, but through the actual user-facing command and 40 independently-drawn real networks (simulated mean ties 5.675 vs. exact 6.020 for a 6-node edges-only model at theta=-0.4 - well within Monte Carlo noise for 40 draws); a directed edges+mutual draw; `nsim(3)` multi-network generation with distinct, correctly-named networks; an undirected `gwesp()` draw; and all five documented error paths (missing `theta()`, `mutual` without `directed`, `gwodegree()`/`gwidegree()` without `directed`, `gwesp()` with `directed`, and a `theta()`/term-count mismatch). Full `cscripts/` sweep (both modes, 127 files): same pre-existing baseline fails as units 72-77, no regressions. Certified at **L2**. |

## Harmonisation phase, unit 79: hot-path benchmarking (Part XXV, acceptance criterion #30)

| Feature | Implemented | Tested | Certified | Documented | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dev/ergm_benchmark.do` and the resulting hot-path measurements | ✅ | n/a (measurement, not a correctness test) | n/a | ✅ | The governing brief's own explicit requirement: benchmark `ErgmGraph::toggle()`/`has_edge()`/`common_neighbors()`, the GWESP change statistic, and ~1M simple MCMC proposals, and identify hot paths - "do not optimize unprofiled code prematurely." Development-only script (never shipped, not in any `_pkg_*.txt` manifest, matching `dev/ergm_reference/`'s own convention), using Stata's own `timer on/off/list` around each Mata benchmark function (Mata itself has no built-in elapsed-time function - confirmed the hard way after an initial version tried a nonexistent `clock()`-based approach). Measured at n=200 nodes, a moderately dense (~10-degree) graph, single-threaded Mata: `toggle()` 1.61 us/call, `has_edge()` 0.44 us/call, `common_neighbors()` 13.24 us/call, `change_gwesp()` 77.29 us/call - all reasonable and matching their own designed complexity (O(1)-average, O(1)-average, O(min(deg_i,deg_j)), respectively). **The dominant finding**: one MCMC step under the TNT proposal costs 1072.65 us versus 5.80 us under the uniform proposal - a measured ~185x gap, entirely attributable to `ergm_propose_tnt()`'s own `G.all_ties()` call, which materializes the network's FULL tie list (O(n+nties)) on every single proposal regardless of outcome. This was already a disclosed, anticipated simplification in `unw_ergm.do`'s own header comment on `ergm_propose_tnt()` ("a documented, deliberate v1 simplification... rather than the O(1) live-edge-list Statnet's own C implementation maintains") - this unit promotes it from a documented hypothesis to a measured, quantified finding, and `docs/ERGM_ROADMAP.md`'s own Performance section is updated accordingly: a live incrementally-maintained edge list for `ErgmGraph` (O(1) TNT edge-picks) is now identified as the clear TOP performance-track item, ahead of a full C/C++ kernel - a self-contained, low-risk Mata-level fix with the largest measured payoff, per the brief's own "do not move ordinary functionality into C merely for ideological reasons" instruction. Not implemented in this unit (a pure measurement pass, per its own explicit purpose) - recorded as the roadmap's own new top item instead of acted on immediately, since optimizing was never this unit's own goal. |

## Pending (queued for implementation, not yet started)

| Feature | Priority (see ROADMAP.md) | Est. effort |
|---|---|---|
| **`nwset.ado`'s `mat()` option cannot accept an existing Stata matrix's bare NAME, only a literal parenthesized expression** - found while building `nwergm`'s `estat gof` (harmonisation unit 76). Root cause: `nwset.ado` line 651, `mata: mode1 = cols(`mat')`, resolves its own `mat()` argument as a bare Mata expression - a literal expression like `(0,1\1,0)` parses directly as an anonymous Mata matrix constant regardless, but a bare NAME referring to an existing Stata matrix does not auto-import into Mata (Mata has no such convenience; confirmed via an isolated repro completely independent of `nwergm`, in a bare session using only the compiled `lnwcommands.mlib`: `matrix define X = (0,1\1,0)` then `nwset, mat(X)` fails with "X not found", r(3499)/`<istmt>` - matastrict was also ruled out as a factor via direct testing). Every existing caller of `nwset, mat(...)` anywhere in this package's own tests happens to always pass a literal expression, which is why this had never previously surfaced. Not fixed at the source (a real, if small, fix - wrapping the `mat()` argument in a genuine Stata matrix copy first, e.g. `tempname __m0; matrix `__m0' = `mat''`, then using `st_matrix("`__m0'")` instead of the bare-name Mata reference, would handle both literal-expression and bare-name callers uniformly - but `nwset.ado` is a widely-used, foundational command and this fix was out of scope for the ERGM-focused unit that found it). `nwergm`'s own `estat gof` avoids the issue entirely via `ErgmMatToLiteral()` (unw_ergm.do), never passing a bare matrix name to `nwset`. | Harmonisation follow-on | Small (one-line fix once someone is ready to touch `nwset.ado` and re-verify its own full test surface) |
| **`nwtriads.ado` crashes on any network with zero ties** - found while building `nwergm`'s `estat gof` (harmonisation unit 76): "n not found - data already wide" (r(111)), raised from an internal `reshape ..., j(n)` this command (or shared `unw_defs`/`nw_syntax` plumbing it calls) apparently needs for its own edgelist-based input path, which does not have the expected shape when the network has no ties at all. Confirmed via an isolated repro completely independent of `nwergm`: `nwset, mat((0,0\0,0)) undirected name(x)` then `nwtriads x` reproduces it in a bare session. Not root-caused further (would need tracing exactly which shared internal helper performs the reshape and why a zero-tie network breaks its own precondition) or fixed at the source - out of scope for the ERGM-focused unit that found it. `estat gof` defends against it with `capture` around every `nwtriads` call on a simulated network (a real possibility during MCMC), excluding that draw from the triad-census average rather than crashing. Likely affects any other caller of `nwtriads` on a network that happens to have zero ties, not just `nwergm`. | Harmonisation follow-on | Small-Medium (needs tracing the shared reshape-based helper first) |
| **`nwhomophily`'s statistical homophily effect appears non-functional** - `homophily(2)` and `homophily(-2)` produce byte-identical output networks under an identical seed, despite `nwhomophily.ado`'s own weight construction being correctly, oppositely signed for the two calls (hand-verified). Traced to `nwdyadprob.ado`'s `density()`-conditioned branch handing the weight matrix to the third-party `gsample` package (`gsample `` `ties' `` [aweight=_tempdyad], generate(link) wor`) for weighted sampling without replacement - confirmed `gsample` is genuinely installed and does implement real weight-sensitive sampling in isolation (its own PPS-consistency check correctly errors on an artificially extreme weight), so the disconnect is somewhere between `nwdyadprob.ado`'s own weight hand-off and `gsample`'s actual selection, not simply "gsample is missing/broken". Not root-caused further - would need either instrumenting the exact weight vector `gsample` receives at the moment of the call, or a minimal standalone `gsample`-only repro isolating whether the issue is `nwdyadprob`'s own data prep (e.g. `nwreplace _tempdyad = _tempdyad * 10`, `nwtoedge ..., full`, the `_nonzero`/`drop if ego==alter` filtering) or `gsample` itself. Affects any command building on `nwdyadprob`'s `density()` path with non-uniform weights, not just `nwhomophily` - worth checking `nwqap`/other weight-driven generators that might share this path. | Harmonisation follow-on | Medium (needs a minimal, isolated repro before further diagnosis) |
| **Dialog (`.dlg`) UI audit and completion** (user-requested feature-list item 9). First step: resolve the `nwcommands-dlg.pkg` distribution-mechanism question already Pending from harmonisation unit 53, since `nwinstall`'s own `dialog` install path depends on it. Then, for each of the 57 existing `.dlg` files (plus 3 `.idlg` includes): confirm it still matches its corresponding command's current option set (many commands have had options added/changed/fixed across this session's own 50+ harmonisation units - a dialog written before those changes would silently offer stale or missing controls), confirm every currently-implemented command that should have a dialog actually has one (audit against the full command list, not assumed complete), and confirm the overall design reflects the package's current architecture/conventions (the user's own explicit ask, not just "does it still run"). | User-requested (feature-list item 9) | Large (57+ files to individually verify against their own command's current state, plus new dialogs for any implemented-but-undialogged command) |
| **Help-file network-type restructuring** (user-requested feature-list item 11), staged path once unblocked: (1) finish auditing the ~90 remaining commands in `docs/NETWORK_TYPE_MATRIX.md` against the same evidence standard already used for the ~25-37 done rows (read the actual code, quote the line backing each classification - not inferred from the `.sthlp` alone, since several existing per-command sections were themselves found stale or wrong during earlier units this session); (2) write a new, real Stata help topic (e.g. `nw_networktypes.sthlp`) presenting the completed matrix in SMCL, plus its own general explanation of two-mode/valued/directed support conventions across the package; (3) link it from the general help suite (`nw_topical.sthlp`/`nw_intro.sthlp`, wherever a new user would naturally land first); (4) only then remove the 25 existing per-command "Supported network types" sections in favor of a link to the new shared topic, one command at a time, diffing each removal against the shared page to confirm no information is actually lost in the move (a few existing sections carry command-specific nuance - e.g. `nwkatz`'s own note that its `alpha` exponentiates a *distance* rather than a *strength ratio*, unlike `nwdegree`'s `alpha` - that a purely tabular matrix page may not capture as clearly and might need to stay as a short command-specific addendum even after the main classification moves to the shared page). | User-requested (feature-list item 11) | Large (the ~90-command audit is the dominant cost; the restructuring itself, once the audit is done, is comparatively mechanical) |
| **`xvars`/generate-by-default policy reversal** (user-requested feature-list items 1 and 8, the same request stated twice): "not generate Stata variables for networks by default, only do when explicitly asked." Surveyed (not implemented) to scope the real blast radius before committing to a fix, since flipping a longstanding default is a genuine breaking change to every existing script relying on today's auto-generated variables. Today's polarity is the opposite of what's requested: `xvars`, present in 33 live `.ado` files, currently means "*suppress* the normally-on variable generation" - the request is to make suppression the default and generation the opt-in. **The actual fix surface is much smaller than 33 files**: `nwload.ado` is a single central choke point (`if "`xvars'" != "" { exit }` before generating) that most callers route through unconditionally (`nwring`, `nwpref`, `nwexpand`, `nwlattice`, `nwdyadprob`, `nwrandom` all just do `nwload, `xvars'` - flipping `nwload.ado`'s own one check fixes all six for free). A second group has its own independent inline `if "`xvars'" == "" { ... }` check instead of delegating to `nwload` - confirmed via direct grep, each needs its own fix: `nwfromedge.ado` (the package's single most common entry point - also reached via `nwset`'s `edgelist` option and `nw2fromedge`'s delegation), `nwgeodesic.ado` (two separate sites), `nwsimilar.ado`, `nwattime.ado`, `nwimport.ado` (multiple sites across its several import-format branches), `nwsimindex.ado`, `nwdissimilar.ado`, `nwreach.ado`, `nwaddnodes.ado`, `nw2project.ado`. The remaining ~20 of the 33 files (`nwsym`, `nwreplacemat`, `nwtranspose`, `nwcug`, `nw2set`, `nwhomophily`, `nwhierarchy`, `nwdropnodes`, `nwcloseness`, `nwpermute`, `nw2fromedge`, `nwsmall`, and others) are pure pass-throughs with no independent generate-or-not logic of their own - they just forward `xvars` to one of the files above, so once those are fixed, pass-throughs need no code change at all, only re-verification. **Open design question, needs a decision before implementation starts**: once suppression is the default, `xvars` itself becomes permanently redundant (a no-op that's always true) - either keep it accepted-but-inert for backward compatibility (a script that already passes `xvars` keeps working, just for a different reason), or introduce a new, differently-named opt-in option (e.g. `genvars`/`makevars`) for "please generate the variables," decided once and applied consistently across all ~12 files with real logic. Whichever is chosen, every one of the ~12 real fix sites needs its own test updated/added (most existing `cscripts/` tests for these commands currently assert the *old* auto-generate default, so flipping the default without updating those tests would turn currently-passing tests into false failures - or worse, false passes if a test happens not to check the specific generated variable). | User-requested (feature-list items 1 and 8) | Large (breaking-default-behavior change touching ~12 files with real logic, ~20 pass-throughs needing re-verification only, plus every affected test file) |
| **Network-overwrite-by-default policy** (user-requested feature-list item 10): "make sure we adhere to that policy" (same non-destructive-by-default spirit as the `xvars` item above, but a distinct mechanism - this one is about a `nwset`-created network silently overwriting an existing one of the same name, not variable generation). Partially surveyed: `nwset.ado`'s own core `mat()`/varlist construction path already does the safe thing today - on a name collision it auto-picks a new valid name via `nwvalidate` and warns the user (`"Warning! Switched to netname X because Y already in use"`), rather than silently overwriting - so the package's own general intent already appears to be "don't silently overwrite," with specific bugs violating it in places rather than a deliberate overwrite-by-default design (matching the pattern of several `generate()`/"already exists" guard bugs already found and fixed in unrelated commands earlier this session, e.g. `nwdegree`'s isolates guard, unit 45). Not yet audited command-by-command for `replace`/`noreplace`-style options that might default the *other* way (overwrite unless `noreplace` is given) - that survey is the actual remaining work here, likely smaller in scope than the `xvars` item above but not yet sized. | User-requested (feature-list item 10) | Medium (survey each command's own `replace`/`noreplace`/`generate()` collision handling; likely smaller than the `xvars` item, not yet sized precisely) |
| `nwcommands-dlg.pkg`/`nwcommands-ext.pkg`/`nwcommands-ext1.pkg`/`nwcommands-hlp.pkg` sit as pending, uncommitted deletions in the working tree (found during the root-folder cleanup, harmonisation unit 53, and deliberately left untouched rather than guessed at). `nwinstall.ado` references all four by name via `net install "nwcommands-X", all` from `http://nwcommands.org` - unclear whether these root-level copies are the actual source of truth meant to be uploaded there on release, or regeneratable build artifacts the way `nwcommands-ado.pkg` is kept in sync with `_pkg_ado.txt` (no corresponding `_pkg_dlg.txt`/`_pkg_ext1.txt` was found, only `_pkg_ext.txt` for one of the four). Needs understanding the actual release/publish process before deciding whether to finalize the deletions, regenerate the files, or restore them. | Harmonisation follow-on | Small (once the release process itself is understood) |
| **Test-methodology gap**: any `cscripts/test_*.do` with an uncaptured `nwuse`/`nwwebuse florentine` (or `nwimport`'s own UCINET fetch) call that is NOT the file's literal last statement silently skips everything after it under this session's own regression-sweep methodology (`do file.do`, checking the resulting log for `r([0-9]`) - Stata's plain `do` aborts the whole remaining script on the first uncaptured error, so a "0 unexpected `r()` codes beyond the known dead-URL failure" result has actually only verified the file's content *before* that call, not "everything except that one line" as this document's own prior wording implied. Found while placing a new test in `cscripts/test_nwplot.do` (harmonisation unit 50) - that file's own Plot A-G SVG-export block (and anything else after its `nwuse florentine` at line 96) has not actually been exercised by any sweep this session, or possibly since it was written. Confirmed empirically (a minimal repro: `do` a two-line script with an uncaptured failing command followed by a `di` - the `di` never runs) rather than assumed from Stata documentation alone. Likely affects some or all of: `test_nwdegree.do`, `test_nwexport.do`, `test_nwplotmatrix.do`, `test_nwshared.do`, `test_nwsimmelian.do`, `test_nwuse.do`, `test_nwvalue.do`, `test_nwimport.do` - not audited file-by-file yet. Two independent fixes needed, in either order: (1) wrap each such call in `capture` (or an explicit availability check) so the rest of each file's own content actually runs regardless of network/data availability, and (2) resolve the underlying dead URL itself (see the `webnwuse`→`nwwebuse`/Box.com item below) so the *intended* real-data tests can pass again rather than merely stop silently swallowing the failure. Until both are done, treat any "clean sweep" claim for the 8 files above as verified only up to their own first uncaptured external-data call, not end to end. | High (undermines confidence in this document's own prior certification claims for the affected files) | Medium (mechanical `capture`-wrapping once the Box.com redirect unblocks re-verifying the previously-unreachable content) |
| `webnwuse`→`nwwebuse` consolidation and Box.com data redirect (nwcommands.org's own data host is dead - see the test-methodology gap above, and the 8 files it currently manifests as "known baseline failures" in). User provided a Box.com shared folder (`https://app.box.com/s/iptptcerwj3mjsa3ukva0tsue8dp1w1u`) as the new host, but its file listing renders client-side and no per-file direct-download URL could be obtained through available tooling (no browser automation in this environment) - user chose to skip this item until direct links or the exact filenames can be provided some other way. Once unblocked: make `nwwebuse.ado` the real, documented command (currently a one-line wrapper around `webnwuse.ado`, which has the actual implementation and its own `.sthlp` but no proper SMCL doc header) and delete `webnwuse.ado`/`webnwuse.sthlp`; update every `.ado`/`.sthlp` reference (a wide list - `nwtriads`/`nwset`/`nwname`/`nwload`/`nwbetween`/`nwqap`/`nwconstraint`/`nwfromedge` and ~30 `.sthlp` files' own doc-header examples) to call `nwwebuse` instead; redirect `$nwwebpath`'s default to the Box.com host; test actual data loading once real direct-download URLs are known. | User-requested (feature-list item 3) | Large (wide `.ado`/`.sthlp` reference sweep) once the Box.com file-access blocker is resolved |

| `nwmovie`/`nwmoviexy` have zero automated test coverage (confirmed genuinely working end to end for the first time in harmonisation unit 52 - a real `movie.gif` produced and inspected - after fixing the `_nwsyntax_other`/typo bugs that made every prior call crash) but writing a `cscripts/` test needs care: the final rendering step shells out to ImageMagick (`convert`) and then to the OS's default GIF viewer (`shell open ...` on macOS), the same class of side effect that has kept `nwplotjs` untested throughout the package's history. Also worth covering while a test is being written: `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s own still-open Pending item that `nwmovie.ado`/`nwmoviexy.ado` both have a `capture drop _frame_x _frame_y` compound-drop (the same antipattern fixed in `nwplot.ado`, unit 44) whose actual risk was never confirmed either way. | Harmonisation follow-on | Small-Medium (needs a way to verify the generated frames/GIF without triggering the ImageMagick shell-out or the OS viewer-open step) |

`nw_helpwriter.ado`'s line-copy loop remains not robust to certain backtick-quote patterns in a `.ado` file's lines (doc header *or* program body — the loop macro-processes every line unconditionally before deciding whether to write it), and separately can *silently truncate* a `.sthlp` (no error at all) if a macro-mangled line happens to evaluate to the literal string `***/`, prematurely closing the copy. Deliberately not fixed at the mechanism level in this pass (harmonisation unit 16) — correctly escaping arbitrary source lines is a larger, separate, riskier change than the isolation fix that unit made; assessed and declined once already (see unit 16's own certified row). Four known, confirmed-safe files are affected: `nwimport.ado` (genuine program-body content triggers a hard "too few quotes" crash, but its own test independently fails anyway for an unrelated reason - a known baseline failure - so it was never going to get certified regardless); `nwplotjs.ado` (its embedded-JavaScript content eventually exceeds Stata's maximum macro/line length; confirmed via `git diff` to leave the file completely unmodified, not corrupted); `nwplot.ado` (found while modernising `nwplot`, harmonisation unit 33 - its own pre-existing Mata layout-algorithm code trips the same "too few quotes" crash, but only after the doc-header copy has already completed, confirmed via the crash location); and `nwcomponents.ado` (found while adding `nwkcomponents`, harmonisation unit 37 - its own pre-existing Mata body content trips the identical crash, same safe "after the doc header closes" pattern). In every one of these last two cases the `.sthlp` regeneration was verified to be complete and correct despite the crash - only the automatic "last certified" stamp was skipped (added by hand instead, since each file's own test genuinely passes). No audit has been done for whether any *other* file's doc header trips this - the 43-file bulk backfill in unit 16 happened not to hit it, but that doesn't rule it out elsewhere. | Stage 1 follow-on (infra) | Small-Medium |
